# gm-new design directions

This note turns the evidence in `research/deep-research.md` into design directions. Each direction is marked as confirmed by the evidence, or as a hypothesis gm-new must test on its own workload before it becomes a design rule.

## Decided

- Scope: a fresh worker that replaces gm's loop, not a fork of gm. Reason: one worker, one log and one loop is the simplest design the evidence supports.
- Language: Node.js 22 or later, ES modules (see "Node floor 22 (2026-11-03)" at the end of this file). Dependencies: one pinned runtime dependency, acorn 8.18.0. No native code and no submodules. Any further dependency needs a measured gain in turns or accuracy, and a measured memory cost per worker, recorded here first. Decided 2026-11-02 as the joint decision of the owner and the orchestrator, which replaced the zero-dependency rule; see "Dependency rule, joint decision" below. The orchestrator rejected the agent's earlier change, which had added thorns and node:sqlite and raised Node to 22.5, and that change is reverted by the commit that records this rule. Reason: acorn is small and has no native code, and it gives exact call sites, which cuts the turns spent verifying callers and impact.
- Shape: gm-new is an MCP server (stdio, JSON-RPC written by hand with Node built-ins, no network listener) plus a skill at `skills/gm/SKILL.md`. The Claude Code session is the model. The CLI is a thin wrapper for scripted witnesses. Reason: owner decision, relayed by the gm agent (2026-11-01). It supersedes the earlier single-CLI shape, because the host model is already the worker.
- Self-iteration: gm-new runs on itself. The project .mcp.json starts tools/supervise.mjs with --runs runs/self and task tasks/self.md. Each server start gets its own run directory under runs/self. A run resumes only with an explicit --resume <run-id>. finish closes only that run, and a later session starts a new one. The skill and the self-task say the same. The step budget is 100000, which is correct for a shared project run. The gm agent decided this on the user's instruction (2026-11-01). The user approves the server once, in /mcp, the first time Claude Code opens the project, and nothing in gm-new can grant that. ccbridge sessions do not read project .mcp.json. gm-new writes nothing into any host's global config. The project .mcp.json is the only registration, and it lives in the repo.
- Hot reload: tools/relay.mjs holds the host connection and runs tools/supervise.mjs, which runs the worker as a child (see the relay bullet below). A change under src/ or skills/ holds new host requests, waits for calls in flight, pauses the worker with SIGTERM, starts a new one on the same run, and resumes the run from its log. A call still running is never interrupted or answered with an error: the restart waits until its result is logged. Background jobs stop at a restart, and each stop is logged. A broken source is reported, and the next good change recovers. It registers nothing in any config. Reason: the user asked for hot reload while the worker runs.
- Hot reload, decided and witnessed (gm agent, 2026-11-01): the project .mcp.json runs the host-facing relay, which runs the supervisor and the worker, so a running session reloads on a source change.
- Relay, so the supervisor can be replaced without a reconnect (user request, 2026-11-01): `tools/relay.mjs` is the only file the host runs, and it holds the host's stdio and never changes in normal work. It runs `tools/supervise.mjs` as a child. A change to `tools/supervise.mjs` swaps the supervisor: the relay refuses a file with a syntax error and keeps the running supervisor, otherwise it holds new host lines, waits for every request it sent to be answered, stops the supervisor with SIGTERM, and starts a new one on the same run. The supervisor pauses the worker on SIGTERM, so the run stays open, and the new supervisor restarts the worker with the reason `changed: supervise.mjs`. A supervisor that dies is replaced by the relay after a backoff (1 s, doubling to 30 s), and each call it had taken is answered with the failure text. The host sees one `notifications/tools/list_changed` notice per swap, and no disconnect. Reconnect needed: once, to move the project's `.mcp.json` from `tools/supervise.mjs` to `tools/relay.mjs`. After that, only a change to `tools/relay.mjs` or `.mcp.json` needs one. Reason: the user asked for no further reconnects while the worker is developed.
- Crash rules, decided with the relay (2026-11-01): a crash that the supervisor can catch (an uncaught exception or rejection) pauses the worker before it exits, so the run stays open. A SIGKILL of the supervisor cannot be caught by it, so the worker catches it instead: the worker runs with `--supervised yes`, and the end of its stdin pauses the run, and the next start resumes it. Before this fix a SIGKILL closed the run; the crash witness `crash-1793563150633` below shows that old behaviour and is superseded.
- Supervisor exit pauses, never closes (gm agent defect report, 2026-11-01): the old supervisor's SIGTERM ended the worker's stdin, which closed run `2026-11-01-19-50-16-6b638b` at seq 233 (inFlight 0, last result seq 231, commit 5d90f25, already pushed). Now every exit of `tools/supervise.mjs` (SIGTERM, SIGINT, the end of its stdin) pauses the worker with SIGTERM and waits for it to exit before the supervisor exits, and the worker pauses on the end of its stdin when it runs supervised. Witness, scripted (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/pause-fix/run-1793563965152/run`, driver `drive.mjs` beside it): three supervisor lives on one run. Life 1 ended with SIGTERM (paused at seq 5, reason SIGTERM from the supervisor), life 2 with SIGKILL (paused at seq 10, reason the supervisor ended stdin), life 3 with the end of stdin (paused at seq 15). Each life ended with a paused event and no end event, and each next life resumed the same run (restart at seq 6 and 11). The run has no end event at the end. A pause kills the worker's shell and job groups first, then waits for the calls to settle, so a pause orphans no shell. Before the change, a SIGKILL after the 5 s pause left a `sleep` running, found after the catchable-crash test.
- Relay witnesses (scripted, copies of the tree under `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/hmr2`, drivers `drive.mjs`, `drive-throw.mjs`, `drive-crash.mjs`): `run-1793563317775` (a 3 s `run_shell` in flight at the swap was answered ok before the swap; two `restart` events with reason `changed: supervise.mjs`, one `notifications/tools/list_changed` notice per swap; a syntax-broken edit was refused with the old supervisor still answering; the restored file swapped again; the relay pid and stdio stayed up and the relay exited 0 after stdin ended). `throw-1793563286672` (an injected throw while a `run_shell` ran: the call settled with signal SIGTERM, the run has `paused` then `restart` with reason `retry after a failed start`, one run, and no orphan `sleep`). `crash-1793563150633` (SIGKILL of the supervisor: the call got the failure text, the retry started, and the run closed as the known limit above). Each server start gets its own run, and finish closes only its run. Witness: two sessions in a row, runs 2026-11-01-18-14-03-4867a3 (closed when its stdin ended) and 2026-11-01-18-14-03-8d72d4 (finished). The second session took its first step, unaffected by the first run's finish.
- Replacement principle: gm-new changes anything that can be changed or improved. No design choice is kept because gm made it. Each inherited choice is re-decided on evidence, and gm-derived plumbing is removed rather than adapted. Reason: the user's instruction (2026-11-01), that gm-new exists to change everything, not to bear the weight of gm's decisions.
- Independence from gm: gm-new does not use gm. Its code, skill, MCP server, runner, tests and builds use no gm MCP tool, no gm skill, no gm runner and no gm state. gm is the comparison target for arm B only, and the arm B configuration in the removed live runner is comparison tooling. Reason: gm-new replaces gm, so it must not depend on it (the user's instruction, 2026-11-01). Earlier builder briefs that named the gm MCP for code search are superseded.
- Model: gm-new makes no API calls. In MCP mode the host session is the model. The CLI has one model, `scripted`, which is offline and deterministic. Reason: a project decision (2026-11-01), taken on the user's instruction that gm-new is an MCP tool and does not interact with any API. No API key is needed, because interactive Claude Code runs on OAuth. Anything that would call an API is reported to the gm agent to do, not done here.
- Anthropic adapter: removed. gm-new holds no API key and sends no Messages API request, so the earlier decisions (the default model, the key from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, the fetch adapter) are superseded. Reason: a project decision (2026-11-01), taken on the user's instruction that gm-new is an MCP tool. Spend belongs to the host session on the Claude Code login, not to gm-new.
- Context: no summarisation. `next_context` returns the rules, the task, the witness requirement, the events new since the last step in full, the seen events as compact records within two horizons (H1 = 14 and H2 = 12 events), and `prefixSha`; `query_log` reaches every event. Decided 2026-11-02 as the owner's request, approved by gm; see "Feed compaction". Reason: the log stays complete, so no lossy summary can drop a fact, and each event is transferred in full once.
- Governance: `rules.md` in the run directory is re-read at every step, placed first in the prompt and never compacted. Reason: rules that a summary drops are violated (finding 3).
- Tools (MCP): `next_context`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir`, `run_shell` (60 s default timeout), `job_start` (detached shell job), `job_status` (state, exit code and output tail, with a bounded `waitMs` poll), `query_log`, `finish` (needs a witness). The server writes every event to the log, and the host model calls `next_context` each step. The server answers concurrent requests: it reads stdin without blocking and replies by JSON-RPC id, so the host's parallel tool calls run in parallel and the server measures them. Reason: owner decision (2026-11-01). Each tool is one plannable step (finding 4), and background jobs let shell work overlap other actions (item 2).
- Kernel and phase plan (gm agent, 2026-11-02, decided by the gm agent, recorded here). The kernel comes from the gm agent's inventory of gm's 193 verbs: filesystem (read_file, write_file, edit_file, list_dir, stat_file), exec (run_shell and the job verbs; no language stem, since this Node project runs node through run_shell), git (the 11 verbs through the host git binary, with explicit paths for commit and add), ci_status (through gh api), and row tracking (prd_* and unknown_*). Dropped, plumbing only: the spool, leases, the FSM as data, the wasm sideload and sibling registry, install and skill serving, the hourly self-update, Dream-RSI, config sync over git, writer locks, and the witness ledger. Rows are files in `work/rows/<id>.yml`, and a row resolves with a witness of a seq in this run's log plus a commit that exists in the repository. Zero dependencies stays. The embedder and the vector store are dropped. The gh CLI stays as an external tool, because git push authentication and CI need it; it is not a gm component. Search is phase 2: BM25 over tracked files with no embedder, plus literal, regex and whole-word modes. callers and impact parse with acorn 8.18.0, decided by the joint decision of 2026-11-02 (see Dependency rule, joint decision). Phase 3 runs the project's tests and checks through run_shell, each with a witness, and no test files are written. Phase 4 (fetch, then browser over CDP) is optional. The gate for done: gm-new edits a small real change to itself through its own tools, in a live Claude session through airc, the commit goes through its own git verbs, and CI passes. Only after the gate do the gm dependencies come out, each removal in its own commit. The CLI prompt lists the original verbs only; the kernel verbs run in both modes, and the MCP tool list describes them to the model.
- Search (phase 2, gm agent 2026-11-02): `search` ranks the lines of git-tracked text files by BM25 (k1 1.2, b 0.75), and an identifier also matches its camelCase or snake_case parts. `grep` finds text by literal (default), regex, or word (a whole identifier), with optional ignoreCase and limit. Both are read-only and need no path, so they run beside writes; a result can show a file as it was just before a concurrent write. Files are read only if they are tracked, not symlinks, inside the workspace, text (no NUL in the first 8000 bytes), and at most 1 MB; each file is re-read only when its mtime or size changes. Known limits: a regex runs without a timeout, so a pattern with catastrophic backtracking blocks the server (patterns are capped at 200 characters); BM25 can rank a call site above the definition of a symbol that both lines contain; a workspace that is not a git repository is refused. callers and impact parse with acorn 8.18.0 (joint decision, 2026-11-02). Reason: the gm agent's phase plan (2026-11-02), phase 2.
- Events over polling (user direction, 2026-11-01): `job_status` with `waitMs` waits on the job's completion event, the promise that settles when the process exits, with one deadline timer. It no longer re-reads the status file every 100 ms. Witness, run 2026-11-01-19-05-04-223331: job `evjob` (`sleep 2`), then one `job_status` call with waitMs 10000. The job ended at seq 35 and the reply came at seq 36, 0.347 ms later, in one call. The scripted measurement before the change had a 9.5 ms gap with 100 ms polling, and six calls for the same job.
- Long-horizon scripted measurement (gm agent item 3, no API calls): `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/longrun/drive.mjs`, run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/longrun/run-1793560405886/runs/longrun`. 1000 read_file calls gave 2002 events at the time of the queries. The log is 547700 bytes, which is 273.6 bytes per event. The 1000 reads took 634 ms, which is 0.63 ms per call. query_log medians of 7 calls each: a kind filter (kind result, first page) 3.14 ms; a time window (the middle 100 ms of the run) 4.89 ms; a cursor page near the end (after the last seq minus 300, kind result) 4.19 ms, with next 1790 after seq 1702; and no filter, the first page, as a baseline, 4.70 ms. This is a read-only scripted workload. It is not a long-horizon PRD workload, so the numbers give the log's cost, not a model's.
- git_rm verb (added to fix a missing verb, the owner's rule, 2026-11-01): `git_rm` removes the given workspace paths from the disk and the index with `git rm`, so the removal is staged, and git_commit commits it through the gate. Witness, scripted in a throwaway repo (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gitrm/run-1793561647151`): `git_rm` of `a.txt` returns ok, the file is gone from disk, `git status` shows `D  a.txt`, and `keep.txt` is untouched. `git_rm` of `.` is refused.
- Long-horizon witnesses (scripted, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/longhorizon/drive.mjs`, run `run-1793560605024`, fresh directories). W1, recovery: a `git_push` to a bare origin whose pre-receive hook sleeps 15 s was killed with SIGKILL after 3 s. The restarted worker's next_context carries a recovery note at last seq 4 that lists `seq 3 git_push`, and the log has `restart` then `recovery` events. W2, repeated id: `read_file` with request id 7 ran, the worker was killed with SIGKILL, and a worker restarted on the same run answered id 7 with the original text. The log has one `duplicate` event and one action with rpcId 7. W3, job lifetime: a `sleep 10` job with `--job-lifetime-ms 2000` was killed at its lifetime and logged as `job-killed` with reason lifetime and lifetimeMs 2000. Its state is killed, and the next next_context names `slow`.
- Commit gate witness (the live session's run, started 19:09:21 UTC): the passing commit is `1e40fcb`, with its git_commit result at event 39, and all four checks pass. A refusal for a bad doc path is event 49: `DESIGN.md: src/no-such-module.mjs` is missing, the docs check fails, and the commit is refused. The probe line was then removed before the next commit. The gate costs about 0.4 s per commit on this tree: syntax 139 to 185 ms, ascii 6 to 7 ms, docs 6 ms, smoke 179 to 245 ms.
- Driver rule (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01): a driver never deletes its own evidence directory on start. Each start makes a fresh directory, stamped with the start time, and cleanup happens only after the witness is recorded and the path is not in KEEP. Witness: the `caps` driver started twice, giving `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gmnc/caps/work-1793559853214` and `work-1793559857423`. Both survive, each with a 38-event log, and both runs read 16 pages that concatenate to the file. The other drivers were patched the same way: `poll`, `restart`, `search`, `checks`, `kernel`, `supervisor-test`, `replay-test` and `concurrency`.
- query_log cap (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01): a reply holds at most 50 events and stays within 20000 characters once serialized, because the reply escapes the JSON it carries. When events remain, `next` is the seq of the last event returned, and the caller passes it back as `after`. Witness, run 2026-11-01-18-39-35-820e47: `kind` restart, page 1 has 50 events and 6872 characters, with next 469. Then `after` 469 returns the last 4 events, seqs 473 to 537, with no next. `kind` result, page 1 has 48 events and 19949 characters, with next 118. Then `after` 118 returns 29 events, seqs 121 to 188, and 19836 characters, with next 188.
- Debug verbs (my item 6): `query_log` takes `kind`, and `fromMono` and `toMono` for a time window in monotonic milliseconds, with `js` optional. Live witness in run 2026-11-01-18-39-35-820e47: `kind` restart returns only restart events, and `kind` tools with a window of 100000 to 1000000 returns the tools events at seqs 258, 267, 299, 313, 317, 333, 359, 363, 367, 371, 417, 421 and 425. A CLI replay, `node src/cli.mjs replay --run <id> [--workspace <dir>]`, reads the run's task.json and its recorded reply events, and runs them through the scripted model in a new run. Every event of the replay carries `replayOf`, so the log marks the replayed events. Witness: the default scripted run `2026-11-01-18-59-28-24c04d` replayed as `2026-11-01-18-59-28-6d23f3`, with 6 of 6 results equal (none differs) and 23 of 23 replayed events carrying replayOf. This is a harness test only. A replay reproduces the tools, not the model's choices.
- Concurrency on and off (my item 5, scripted, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/concurrency/drive.mjs`): four independent `fetch` calls go to a local server that answers after 500 ms, three trials per arm. On (`--max-inflight 8`): median wall 549 ms, maxInFlight 4, idleMs 5.7. Off (`--max-inflight 1`): median wall 2042 ms, maxInFlight 1, idleMs 5.8. Runs: `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/concurrency/work/run-on-1` to `run-on-3` and `run-off-1` to `run-off-3`. Idle time barely moves, because the summary counts idle only while no call or model is in flight, and the calls overlap rather than wait. The on arm cut wall time by about 3.7 times on this task.
- Replay by request id (brief change 6, a harness test only, not a tool the model can call): `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/replay-test/drive.mjs` replays the 17 recorded actions of the 300 KB read run (`gmnc/caps/runs/caps`) through a fresh worker. Each request id is the action's original seq. With the same input, 17 of 17 results match, and the first differing result is none (run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/replay-test/work/same-input/run`). With one character changed in the third page, the first differing result is index 2, old seq 7 and new seq 8, which is the third read (run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/replay-test/work/changed-input/run`). This tests the tools only. A replay says nothing about the model, because model output varies at temperature 0 (80 unique completions in 1,000 runs, measured elsewhere).
- Supervisor witness (brief changes 1 and 2, scripted, run `2026-11-01-18-56-41-dac944`): the supervisor was run from the repo against a copy of the source under `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/supervisor-test/work/root`, so the touches never changed the repo. Test 1: a 35-second `run_shell` (request id 101) was still running when a source change requested a restart. The restart waited, and the call came back with ok true and exit 0, not an error. A repeated request id 101 sent at 1 second got the same reply, and the marker file has one line. The log has a `duplicate` event (seq 4). Test 2: a 60-second job was running when a source change restarted the worker. The log has `paused` (seq 6, `SIGTERM from the supervisor`), then the job `killed` (seq 12), then `restart` (seq 14, the same run). No `sleep 60` process survived, and the run has no `end` event. The live server still runs the old supervisor until the owner restarts it, so this scripted test is the witness for both changes.
- Open step, live status (gm agent, 2026-11-01): the recovery note, the persisted request ids and the job lifetime go live in a gm-new server only after the owner reconnects that server. This session's worker already runs them, because its source changed under hot reload, but the other servers run the code they started with until a reconnect. The reconnect is done. The server started at 19:32:27 UTC on 2026-11-01 runs them: its action events carry rpcId (the first action is seq 3, with rpcId 4), and its error log is empty.
- Duplicate map, persisted (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01): the map is rebuilt from the log at startup, so a repeated request id after a restart is answered from the log and never rerun. The earlier limit (the map lived only in memory) is closed.
- Restored scripts (2026-11-01): the `caps`, `poll` and `restart` drivers set BASE to their own directory, so each start deleted its own script. The cited files were gone. They are restored at their cited paths under `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gmnc/`, with BASE moved to a `work` subdirectory, so a rerun cannot delete the script. The recorded run logs are the evidence, not a rerun.
- Scratch moved (housekeeping rule from the gm agent, 2026-11-01): the scratch cited in this file under `/tmp/gmnc/` now lives under `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gmnc/`, with the same relative paths. Uncited scratch was deleted. Janitor note for the gm agent: cited scratch is still needed as evidence, and it is under the 24-hour sweep unless the janitor exempts cited paths.
- Row parity (my item 4, run 2026-11-01-18-39-35-820e47): every refusal is one dispatch. A malformed id (seq 321), an event seq not in this run's log (seq 323), and a commit that is not in the repository (seq 325) are refused by prd_resolve. An id of the other kind is refused by unknown_resolve (seq 327, reworded at seq 335). A status that is not open, resolved or all is refused by prd_list (seq 329). The list sort now compares code points instead of using localeCompare, so the order does not depend on the host's locale.
- Oracle after the fix (brief change 5 follow-up, same script, rerun in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gmnc/oracle`): a const, let or var declaration now keeps its right-hand side for call tests, and keyword names are refused by callers and impact. On the 12 named functions, the regex finder has 76 sites, the oracle 75, the tp 75, the false positives 1 (`run (` in a regex literal) and the misses 0: precision 98.7%, recall 100%. On every identifier that is not a keyword (1792 regex sites, 1747 oracle sites), the misses are 0, so recall is 100%, and the false positives are 45, so precision is 97.5%. The 45 are text inside strings, templates and regex literals, which the known limits name. The three misses found before the fix are now found.
- Oracle measurement for callers (brief change 5, subagent with Node's bundled acorn 8.18.0 through --expose-internals; scripts in /tmp/gmnc/oracle, the repo is unchanged): 22 of 22 tracked source files parse. On the 12 named functions the regex finder gives precision 98.7% (75 of 76) and recall 100% (75 of 75). Its one false positive is `run` in `src/bench.mjs:106`, a regular expression literal that starts with `run (`. Over every identifier in the sources, recall is 99.8% (1753 of 1756) and precision is 80.4%. Keyword lines such as `if (` account for 388 of the 428 false positives, and 40 more come from text inside strings and templates. Excluding keywords, precision is 97.8%. The three misses are all `const NAME = x.NAME(...)`: the definition test takes the whole line as a declaration, so the member call on the right-hand side is skipped. Against the pass bar (precision at least 0.9 and recall at least 0.8, set by the gm agent), the named functions pass. The keyword and declaration causes are listed here, and the fix follows in its own commit.
- Wait for a job (brief change 4, gm agent 2026-11-01): a 3-second job, measured two ways in scripted drivers (`/tmp/gmnc/poll/drive.mjs`, run `/tmp/gmnc/poll/runs/poll`). Bounded polling, one job_status call with waitMs 500 repeated until the job leaves running: 6 calls, and the final reply came 9.5 ms after the job ended. One long wait, one job_status call with waitMs 30000: 1 call, and the reply came 0.2 ms after the job ended. The extra latency of polling is 9.3 ms, under the 100 ms threshold, so the default is the one long wait, which uses fewer calls. A job that runs past 30000 ms needs a second call.
- Tool list hash (brief change 7, gm agent 2026-11-01): each start logs a `tools` event with the sha256 of the served tool list. A resumed run whose last `tools` hash differs logs `tools-changed` with `old` and `new`. A changed description invalidates the host's prompt cache (documented), so tool descriptions stay stable unless a change is needed. The worker does not see the host's cache usage, so the usage fields stay in the host transcript, as recorded earlier, and the log does not claim a saving.
- Retry after a failed restart (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01): a failed restart retries by itself, starting at 1 s and doubling to a 30 s cap, and it needs no source change. A call that arrives while the restart is failed is answered at once with the failure text, and a held call is answered the same way. Witness, a scripted test with a fresh run directory `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/supervisor-retry/run-1793559709334`: the flag was set and one source change broke the restart. A call during the failure was answered with the failure text in 1 ms. The retries ran at 1000, 2000, 4000 and 8000 ms with no source change. The flag was then cleared, which is not a source change, and the restart recovered after 5033 ms, with 10 error replies on the way. The live server runs the old supervisor until the owner reconnects it, so this scripted test is the witness.
- Restart and pause (brief changes 1 and 2, gm agent 2026-11-01): a restart waits for every call in flight to have its result logged. The 30-second error is gone, and no call is answered with a restart error, so the host does not repeat a call that is still running. The supervisor pauses the worker with SIGTERM. The worker writes a `paused` event, stops its jobs, and exits, and the supervisor sends SIGKILL after 5 seconds. Closing stdin is not part of the pause, because it would end the run as closed, and a closed run stays closed. A repeated tools/call request id is answered from the first reply and logged as a `duplicate`, never run again. Reason: the gm agent's research brief, with Claude Code's default tool timeout of about 28 hours.
- Output pages (brief change 3, gm agent 2026-11-01): `read_file` returns one page of at most 20000 characters, from `offset` (default 0) with `limit` (default 20000). Its result carries `total` (characters in the file), `offset` and `next`, the offset of the next page, or null at the end. `grep` takes `offset` over matches and stops a page at 20000 characters of output. Its footer reads "showing A to B; next offset N". `job_status` with `offset` returns one page of the job output, with `next`. Without `offset` it keeps the 2000-character tail. Offsets count JavaScript characters (UTF-16 units). Reason: the gm agent's brief, so one result cannot flood a context window.
- Callers and impact [row unknown-parser-decision] (item 3, gm agent 2026-11-01, zero dependencies): `callers` lists the call sites of an identifier in tracked .js, .mjs, .cjs and .ts files, by regex over each line. A call is the name followed by an open parenthesis and not a property access. A member call is `.name(`. A definition line (function, class, const/let/var, or a method shorthand) is excluded and marks its file as defining the name. A call in a file that imports a defining file is marked linked. `impact` lists the defining files, the files that call the name, and the importers of those, out to depth levels (default 2, max 5). Import edges come from a regex over `from`, `import(`, `require(` and `import` specifiers, resolved inside the repo only. Every answer says approximate. Known limits, stated in each answer: dynamic calls, re-exports and shadowed names are not resolved, and a line that starts with a comment marker is skipped. A string that contains `name(` is still counted. Reason: the gm agent's decision for zero dependencies (2026-11-01). Item 5 of the brief measures this regex against an acorn oracle.
- Fetch (item 2, gm agent 2026-11-01): `fetch` is an HTTP GET of one http or https URL through Node's built-in fetch, with no body, no cookies and no credentials. Redirects are followed by hand and capped (`maxRedirects`, default 5, up to 10). The timeout covers the headers and the body (`timeoutMs`, default 15000, up to 60000). The body is read up to `maxBytes` (default 2000000, up to 20000000), and `truncated` says when the cap cut it. HTML is reduced to text, and script, style, noscript and comments are dropped before the tags are removed. The result carries status, bytes, finalUrl, redirects and truncated, and a non-2xx status is reported as data, not as an error. Known limit: nothing blocks localhost or private-network addresses, so a fetch can reach services on the worker's host or LAN. The text is untrusted input, and the model must treat it as data. Reason: the gm agent's item 2 (2026-11-01).
- Commit gate (owner decision, relayed by the gm agent, 2026-11-01): there is no CI, and the quality checks run inside the git_commit verb on every commit. Four checks run before the commit, and a failed check refuses it. `syntax` parses every tracked module with node --check. `ascii` finds non-ASCII text in every tracked text file. `docs` requires every repo path that DESIGN.md, AGENTS.md or README.md names in backticks to exist in the index, and every KEEP path to exist on disk. A path counts as a repo path when its first segment is a top-level name in the index. Runtime directories, placeholders, URLs, method names and absolute paths are not checked. `smoke` runs the CLI on a scripted task, which must finish, and a negative control with one step, which must stop at max_steps. Each check reports its result and time in the git_commit result event. No test files and no test directory exist. Reason: the owner's no-test rule, applied to the commit path.
- Gaps, recorded not hidden (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01, from arXiv 2609.02749, Sec. 3.2: "any gap that survives the checks is recorded ... rather than hidden"): every `git_commit` result and its `commit` event carry `notCovered`, a fixed list of what the gate does not check: the semantic correctness of the change, behaviour the smoke run does not exercise, doc content beyond path existence, and CI (none configured). A refused commit carries it too. `finish` takes an optional `gaps` array, logged in the `end` event with the witness, which is still required. A bad `gaps` value is refused and the run stays open. Witness: finish, scripted (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gaps/run-1793563683727/run`): a `gaps` array with a number in it was refused with a `refusal` event, the run stayed open, and the next finish logged `gaps` with its two strings in the end event. The commit witness is the commit that adds this line: its `git_commit` result carries `notCovered`, and its `commit` event is in the live run log. The tool list hash changes, as expected.
- Long-horizon rules (gm agent decisions, 2026-11-01, from the DeepSeek superagent prototype's documents 003-execution.md, 007-architecture.md and 000-philosophy.md in dsh-superagent-prototype). Grade: documented design from a research prototype, not measured, and untested on our workload. (1) Recovery note: a run that resumes after a restart carries a recovery note in the next next_context. It names the last seq and each action that started and has no end event, with its tool and arguments, and it asks the model to verify outside effects (git log, git status, the remote, the row files) before repeating a push, a commit, a fetch that posted, or a row resolve. (2) Persisted request ids: each action records its request id, and at startup the worker rebuilds the duplicate map from the log, so a repeated id is answered from the log and never run again. An action with no end event was interrupted, and its repeat gets an error that asks for the outside effects to be checked. (3) Background job lifetime: a job is killed after 30 minutes by default (flag `--job-lifetime-ms`). The kill is logged as its own `job-killed` event with the output tail, and the next next_context names it. (4) Log growth: the log stays complete, and nothing is deleted. The measured growth and query cost are in Measured.
- Background jobs keep state in `runs/<id>/jobs/<job>.json` (written by rename) and output in `<job>.out`. A job still running at run end is killed and awaited, and its end is logged as a `job` event. Reason: status is readable by any process, and no child outlives the run.
- Path confinement covers the file tools only: `read_file`, `write_file` and `list_dir`. Paths stay inside the workspace, a `..` segment is refused, and a symlink that leaves the workspace is refused, including a dangling one (checked with `lstat` in `nearestExisting`). Refusals are logged as `refusal` events and not thrown. `run_shell` is not confined, and doctor says so. Known limit: a shell command can create a hard link to a file outside the workspace, and the path checks do not see it. A path check does not close this, because a shell can do anything the user can. Reason: an escape attempt through the file tools shows up in the log, and path checks cannot confine a shell (gm agent, 2026-11-01).
- Loop: plan, act, observe, repeat. Each reply is one JSON action or an array of actions; actions that touch no common path run together in one wave with `Promise.all`, and a shell command, `query_log`, `finish` or unknown tool runs alone. Every reply, action and result is appended to the log with monotonic times. Stops on `finish` or at `maxSteps` (default 50). An unparseable reply counts as one step. Reason: work item 1 needs multi-action replies, and the wave rule keeps the log replayable.
- Wave planning uses real paths, not lexical ones. An existing path uses `realpath`; a new file uses the `realpath` of its parent plus its basename. Existing files are grouped by dev and ino, so hard links serialise. Writes that share a group go in one serial wave. Reason: a symlink alias or hard link to a file written in the same reply would race under `Promise.all` (found by the item 5 witness).
- Storage: `runs/<run-id>/` holds `log.jsonl` (append-only), `rules.md` and `task.json`, and `runs/` is git-ignored. Reason: runtime state is never tracked.
- Remote: `AnEntrypoint/gm-new`, branch `main`, pushed by the single writer after each committed item. Reason: the standing owner instruction says to push AnEntrypoint work, and the owner asked for this repository (2026-11-01). The repository stays private; making it public is the owner's decision.
- Doctor: the last run is the newest run directory by name, because names are timestamps. Its outcome comes from `end.status`, or else from a `finish` result. Node is checked by parsed major version (22 or newer). Its output says that the Node-below-22 failure path has no live run, that `run_shell` is unconfined, and that the process-group kill is unverified on Windows. Reason: the gm agent accepted these (2026-11-01), so doctor reports what it cannot check.
- Interactive witnesses: an interactive Claude Code session is driven in tmux through `/workspace/airc` (ccbridge), with OAuth login and no API key. ccbridge forbids `-p`, `--bare`, `--api-key` and `--settings`, so the session authenticates as a person does. A witness runs in a temp project under `/tmp` whose `.mcp.json` launches gm-new's server by absolute path, so gm-new writes nothing into its own repo. The server must work on that route, where a trust prompt on first load is expected. airc is not changed by gm-new, and its untracked files are not committed or deleted. Reason: the gm agent's decision (2026-11-01), which keeps spend on the owner's login.
- Orphans: a background job and a foreground shell command run in their own process group (detached spawn). A job kill, a foreground timeout and the run end kill the group: `kill(-pid)` on POSIX, `taskkill /T /F` on Windows. An interrupt (SIGINT or SIGTERM) on the CLI kills every group at once with SIGKILL, then exits, so no new call starts while the process goes down.
- Next context: `next_context` never waits behind a path call or a running shell. It reads the rules and takes a snapshot of the log at call time, which is safe because the log is append-only. Reason: a host should not lose a step's context for the length of a shell. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01). Witness: `/tmp/gmnc/keep/run`. The next_context reply (seq 4) comes before the 3 s run_shell result (seq 5), at 206 ms against 3129 ms.
- Foreground rule: after a foreground shell call returns, its process group is SIGKILLed, so no child outlives the call. Background work goes through `job_start`, or in the CLI `run_shell` with `background` true and a job name. The rule is in the run_shell tool description. Reason: no child outlives the run, and a grandchild that outlives its foreground call breaks that rule. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01). Witness: run `2026-11-01-12-02-53-e4e69d`, where `sleep 47 &` was gone when the call returned.
- Windows: the process-group kill (`taskkill /T /F`) is unverified, and doctor says so. Do not claim Windows support until a Windows witness exists. Reason: this machine is Linux. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01). Reason: no child outlives the run, and a kill that reaches only `sh` breaks that rule (run-C left an orphaned `sleep`). Owner decision, relayed by the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- MCP queue: MCP mode queues calls by real path, with the same realpath and dev:ino grouping as wave planning. Two calls that touch one file, with at least one write, run in order. A shell call runs alone. Reads of distinct paths may run together. Reason: a host burst can write one file twice, which is silent data loss, and option (b), accepting the race, would leave that case known. Owner decision (2026-11-01).
- Queue cap: `--max-inflight` defaults to 8, and calls beyond the cap wait FIFO. Reason: the cap bounds the server's work, and the witness needs it to be deterministic. Owner decision (2026-11-01).
- Steps: each `next_context` call is one step. The run summary reports `contextCalls` with the same value. The harness uses wall time and idleMs as its primary measures, and step count as a secondary one. Reason: one explicit definition of a step, and the primary measures are the ones concurrency changes. Owner decision (2026-11-01).
- prefixSha: one definition in both modes, the sha256 of the rendered stable prefix (rules, task, witness requirement, the tool list with names, descriptions and input schemas, and fixed instructions, in that order). Both modes render the tool list with `renderTools` and hash the same way, since the API caches tools with their schemas ahead of the system text, so the schemas are part of the stable prefix. The CLI value changed once: `f713c772...` (run `2026-11-01-11-35-02-40d1bf`) to `676623b5...` (run `2026-11-01-12-02-34-c35d97`). The MCP value is `beb6146d...` after Phase 1 (Measured), and it changes with the tool list, as it did when the run_shell description gained the foreground rule (earlier value `60254465...`). Reason: one name with one definition, so the hash compares across modes. Owner decision (2026-11-01).
- Dependencies: the joint decision of 2026-11-02 applies, which replaced the zero-dependency rule: one pinned runtime dependency, acorn 8.18.0 (see "Dependency rule, joint decision"). The DeepSeek Harness is in developer preview and warns of breaking changes, so gm-new does not depend on it. Reason: any further dependency needs a measured gain in turns or accuracy and a measured memory cost per worker. Source: the DeepSeek prototype (github.com/deepseek-ai/dsh-superagent-prototype): MIT, per the raw LICENSE file and the GitHub license API; not on npm, since package.json has private: true. The Harness developer-preview warning is from the product page and the repo. Checked against the primary sources in research run wf_261fe87f-4c6.
- Comparison trials (item 7): each comparison arm runs at least 3 trials per task, across at least 3 tasks, and the report gives the median and the spread, not one run. Reason: a single run is noise, and the prototype's Minimal-mode gain is unmeasured on controlled tasks (same source as above).
- Live concurrency: 1 Claude Code session at a time, across both arms, and one batch at a time (the removed live runner enforces the cap and a lock, and refuses a higher `--concurrency`). The cap was 3 and the gm agent lowered it to 1 on 2026-11-01, when the batch was stopped. Reason: the machine is saturated (the agentplug runner uses about 7 cores and 2 GB, the MCP servers total 5.1 GB, and each live session runs its own MCP chain), and the 1-minute load average was 8 to 10. The cap rises back to 3 only after the runner's concurrency cap is committed and the 1-minute load average is under 6. Trial results from before the change stay valid, since the cap changes no measurement. The original reason still applies: the OAuth rate limits are unknown, and a limit hit mid-trial would corrupt the timing. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- Live metrics: every live trial is graded by its checker in `tasks/<task>/` and measured by one parser, the removed transcript parser, for both arms. Wall time runs from the prompt record to the first end_turn. Tool time is the union of tool_use to tool_result intervals. Model time is the union of gaps from each request (the prompt or a tool_result) to the next assistant record. Idle time is the wall time covered by neither. Reason: both arms are measured by the same code, from the transcript timestamps, not from our own clocks. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- Live validity and load: an invalid trial is a trial whose session never became ready, or whose transcript never held its prompt. Invalid trials are listed, never dropped silently, and the report counts them. A slot is re-run once after the batch, and if it still fails it is recorded as invalid, so arm A has only two valid t3 trials, and the 3-trial rule is not claimed for t3. The 1-minute load average is recorded at the start and end of every trial. Trials that started at a load of 6 or more are reported apart from quiet-window trials, and trials from before the load was recorded apart from both. The arms are compared only on quiet-window trials, or the comparison is stated as loaded. Development builders wait until the load is under 6, and the live batch stays at one session. Reason: load changes wall time and idle time, so work running now would contaminate the timings. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- Minimal tool set [row unknown-minimal-tool-set]: the comparison of the full and reduced tool sets runs only after the runner's concurrency cap is in and the load is under 6. It does not run under load, and it does not run at all while the runner fix is blocked. It stays an open question until then. Owner decision via the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- Retry, exercised only by scripted faults in gm-new, since gm-new makes no request: retry 408, 409, every 5xx (502, 503, 504 and 529 included), and network errors (ECONNRESET, timeouts) with backoff from 1 s, doubling, capped at 16 s, plus or minus 20% jitter, at most 4 retries. Live retries belong to the host. A 429 with `retry-after` waits that many seconds, and fails with a logged reason if the header asks for more than 60 s. A 429 without `retry-after` retries at most 2 times. Every other 4xx fails at once, logged with its error type. Each retry is an event with attempt, status, errorType and delayMs. Reason: the rules follow platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/errors (fetched 2026-11-01). A spend-cap 429 has no `retry-after` and keeps failing, so it must never loop.
- Log fields: camelCase everywhere (`errorType`, `delayMs`, `prefixSha`, `durMs`). Reason: one naming convention for the log, confirmed by the gm agent (2026-11-01).
- Cache: `prefixSha` is the sha256 of the rendered stable prefix (rules, tool list, fixed instructions), logged on every model event. It is a local check that the prefix does not change between steps. gm-new sends no request, so it places no cache breakpoint: the host manages the cache. The host transcript carries each turn's `usage`, including `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`. The `cache_control` marking and `--dump-request` are removed, superseded by the project decision of 2026-11-01. Reason: a prefix that changes between steps defeats caching, and the prefix hash shows it does not.
- Run summary: at run end the loop writes one `summary` event with maxInFlight, meanInFlight, modelMs, toolMs and idleMs (time with no action and no model call in flight). `query_log` reads it. Reason: concurrency and waiting are measured from the log, not estimated.
- Harness: runs a scripted task with the parallel and background features on and off, and prints each run's wall time and idleMs from its log. No paid calls. Reason: direction 6 requires measured numbers before any feature is default-on beyond items 1 and 2.
- Defaults: background jobs (`--no-background` turns them off) and parallel waves (`--serial` turns them off) are on. Speculative overlap of the next model call (rule 3) and multi-worker fan-out stay off until measured. Reason: owner decision through the gm agent; measured evidence is the only route to change them.

## Simplicity rule

Start with one worker, one log and one loop. Add a component only when a measured run shows the current design failing. This follows the Anthropic harness guidance (low confidence, dated) and the single-agent baseline result (medium confidence).

## Directions

1. **Complete log, queried by code (hypothesis).** Every observation, action and outcome is appended to one log. Recall runs code over the log, not a summary. Evidence: finding 1 (medium). Unknown: log growth and retrieval cost. Test: the same long task run with a log-query worker and a compacting worker, with cost measured.

2. **Validated compaction, only when the log is too big to query (hypothesis).** If compaction is needed, check each summary against the next k steps before using it. Evidence: finding 2 (medium, self-reported, one replication dispute). Test: summary accepted or rejected by a judge on gm's own traces, compared against no validation.

3. **Governance rules outside the compactor (hypothesis, risk confirmed, fix untested).** Rules that must hold for the whole run are kept in a file that every step re-reads, so they cannot be summarised away. Evidence: finding 3 supports the risk (medium, observational). The Constraint Pinning remedy was refuted 0-3, so this direction is not evidence-backed. Test: a rule set of gm's kind, violation rate with and without re-injection.

4. **Context operations as worker actions (hypothesis).** Compaction and log queries are actions the worker plans, like file edits and commands. Evidence: finding 4 (medium). The 57.6% result is one run against same-family baselines, below frontier models, so it is not evidence that the design is best.

5. **Summariser trained with the worker (out of scope for now).** Finding 5 (medium) needs training infrastructure gm-new does not have. Keep it as a later option, not a day-one design.

6. **Measure one worker before fanning out (confirmed in direction, threshold unusable).** Finding 6 (medium) supports measuring a single-worker baseline before adding parallel workers. The saturation threshold (finding 7, low) must not be used as a constant (94% and 45% are unverified).

7. **Subagent isolation (open).** [row unknown-subagent-isolation] The claim that isolated subagents help was refuted 1-2. Parallel subagents stay in gm-new only where a measured run shows they beat one worker on the same task.
8. **Proactive mode (future, not built).** [row unknown-future-directions] The agent watches a repo with no task. Source: the DeepSeek prototype README (github.com/deepseek-ai/dsh-superagent-prototype), checked against the primary sources in research run wf_261fe87f-4c6. It describes a proactive coding agent for one repository, and the article's designs for it are unimplemented. gm-new is task-driven, so this is a future direction with no evidence here.
9. **Verification-gated admission (future, not built).** [row unknown-future-directions] If gm-new learns from its runs, an item is admitted only after a check, and the gaps the check did not cover are recorded, not hidden. Source: arXiv 2609.02749 (Repo-To-Skill, AREX-Skill), Sec. 3.2: "No skill is admitted on the strength of its sources alone," and "any gap that survives the checks is recorded ... rather than hidden." The gains there are self-reported, from one preprint with no replication. The paper excludes construction from both runtime conditions for MLE-bench and PaperBench; it does not say whether construction is counted for FrontierCS or PassNet.
10. **Matched-budget falsifier (future, not built).** [row unknown-future-directions] Once a learned artifact exists, compare it with and without the artifact, with the same model, the same budget and the same tasks, at least 3 trials per task. Count the construction cost inside the budget. The source paper (arXiv 2609.02749) excludes construction from both runtime conditions for MLE-bench and PaperBench, and does not say whether construction is counted for FrontierCS or PassNet. On FrontierCS, token use rises about 82% (2.46M to 4.47M per task) and tool calls rise from 64.7 to 105.0 (figures as read by the gm agent, 2026-11-01), so a falsifier must count that run cost as well. Trigger: this falsifier is built only when gm-new writes a reusable artifact from its runs. Until then it is not built.
11. **Raw-replay arm (future).** [row unknown-future-directions] When there is something to replay, add an arm that replays the raw log instead of a distilled skill. SkillEvolBench (arXiv 2605.24117) finds raw replay often beats distilled skills. The full log already keeps the raw trajectory (direction 1). Skill distillation is not built: the log is the raw trajectory, and the falsifier in direction 10 is triggered only when gm-new writes a reusable artifact from its runs. SkillEvolBench is cited from the gm agent's reading (2026-11-01).

## Concurrency evidence (digest, research/concurrency.md)

- Parallel tool calls in one turn cut wall-clock 40.6% and cost 35.9% in one self-reported study (medium, arxiv 2602.07359). Use: work item 1, measured in item 7.
- Overlapping tool execution with generation can cut latency (medium, arxiv 2603.18897, serving-system paper). Speculation can hurt under high load: unsupported, because the brief found no support for that claim. Use: default off until measured.
- Fine-grained tool streaming may return invalid JSON tool input. Use: not enabled. The parser already handles unparseable replies.
- Prompt caching: order is tools, system, messages; put the breakpoint at the end of the stable prefix, before the event window. Measured 41 to 80% cost cut and 13 to 31% faster first token (medium, arxiv 2601.06007). Use: rule 5, item 4.
- Rate limits: honour retry-after first, then backoff (high, Anthropic docs). Cached input tokens do not count toward input-tokens-per-minute for most models (high). Use: rule 6, item 3.
- Primary-sourced by the brief (gm agent, 2026-11-01): the minimum cacheable prefix for claude-haiku-5-5 is 512 tokens, cache writes cost 1.25x and 2x (one for each cache TTL), and cache reads cost 0.1x. Still unverified: the back-pressure measurement. No saving is claimed from these until a live usage run measures it.

## What the evidence does not settle

- Whether a query-the-log design beats validated compaction on the same tasks (open question 2).
- Whether subagent isolation helps long-horizon outcomes (open question 4).
- Whether rule re-injection [row unknown-rule-reinjection] restores compliance without losing permitted actions (open question 1).
- Whether a reduced tool set [row unknown-minimal-tool-set] matches the full set on the same tasks with the same trials (the prototype's minimal configuration, as reported by the gm agent). Open. Cost: about 18 more live trials across both arms, 3 tasks with 3 trials each, under the same load rule. Trigger: a decision that depends on whether the tool set can shrink. Not run: the gm agent closed the project without it (2026-11-01).
- Whether learned-skill gains transfer to troubleshooting work. Unmeasured, so any transfer is an experiment, not a gain.

## Owner decisions (resolved)

The three questions this section used to ask are answered in Decided: scope, runtime and language, and remote.

## Next step

Work follows the gm agent's phase plan (2026-11-02, see Decided). Phase 1, the kernel tools, and phase 2, search, are witnessed in Measured. callers and impact use acorn, decided by the joint decision of 2026-11-02. Phase 3, JIT checks through run_shell, is witnessed in Measured. Phase 4 (fetch, then browser) is skipped: no project of gm-new needs it yet, and the plan makes it optional. The commit gate is built and in use (commit 1e40fcb, witnessed by events 39 and 49). gm-new must not depend on gm. The live comparison is closed (see "Project close"). Still open and recorded: the minimal tool set (not run), and G3 (cache effect), now measured as a cache share, with no saving claimed.

## Decisions on the open items, 2026-11-01 (gm agent)

Each item below records the decision and its trigger. A trigger is the only condition under which the item reopens.

- Minimal tool set: closed, not pursued. No decision depends on it. Live trials use the owner's login and are rate-limited. Trigger to reopen: a concrete question that depends on the tool set.
- Rule re-injection [row unknown-rule-reinjection] (open question 1): deferred. Trigger: a live run shows a rule violated after a summary. Summaries do not happen while the log is complete. No live trials are spent now.
- Subagent isolation [row unknown-subagent-isolation] (open questions 3 and 4): deferred. Trigger: a long-horizon run shows a single worker hitting a limit. Until then no subagents are built.
- Windows process-group kill: a named limit. Windows support is not claimed until a Windows witness exists.
- Proactive mode, verification-gated admission, the matched-budget falsifier and the raw-replay arm: future, unchanged. Not built.
- Validated compaction and the RL summariser [row unknown-compaction-rl]: deferred. Trigger: a scripted run at 20000 events where query_log takes over 100 ms. Below that, compaction is not built. The measured point is 2002 events, with query_log at 3 to 5 ms (commit 2121c2d).
- CI: closed. The owner chose no CI for gm-new. The commit gate is the check. The gate is built: commit 1e40fcb passed all four checks (its git_commit result is event 39), and a commit with a bad doc path was refused (event 49).

## Open items, status 2026-11-02

- Minimal tool set [row unknown-minimal-tool-set]: closed by the joint decision of 2026-11-02, not run. No decision depends on it.
- Rule re-injection [row unknown-rule-reinjection] (open question 1): blocked. It needs live trials, so it waits for the owner's live-trial budget.
- Subagent isolation [row unknown-subagent-isolation] (open questions 3 and 4): open. Blocked: it needs a long-horizon run that shows a single worker's limit. The scripted long-horizon run measures log cost, not that limit.
- Windows process-group kill [row unknown-windows-kill]: unverified. Blocked: there is no Windows machine here.
- Validated compaction [row unknown-compaction-rl]: deferred, and not needed now. The log stays complete at 273.6 bytes per event, with query_log at 3 to 5 ms over 2000 events. Build it only when the log is too big to query.
- RL summariser [row unknown-compaction-rl]: deferred. It needs training infrastructure.
- Proactive mode, verification-gated admission, matched-budget falsifier and raw-replay arm [row unknown-future-directions]: future, not built. Blocked: no learned artifact exists yet.
- CI for gm-new: closed by the owner's choice (see Decisions). It reads "none configured", and the commit gate is the quality check.
- Long-horizon design: documented in the prototype and built here, but untested on our workload.
- Parser for callers and impact: decided, acorn 8.18.0 (joint decision, 2026-11-02).
- Sandbox for run_code: decided, none (orchestrator, 2026-11-02; the owner may reverse it).
- Browser: not built (held by the owner, 2026-11-02).
- Search recall, wiki answer quality and per-verb tokens: partly or not measured. They need a measurement the owner approves, so they stay open.

## Research consolidation

The status of each research item, as of 2026-11-01. "Implemented" means built. "Measured" means a run in this repo shows an effect. "Witnessed" means a run shows the mechanism works. Each row cites its limit.

| Item | Status | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Background jobs (item 2) | implemented, measured: scripted median 3175 ms on, 5296 ms off | none beyond the scripted model |
| Live harness and summaries (item 7) | implemented; the scripted harness measured; the live trials partly measured | arm A has two valid t3 trials, one short of three; the trial rule is recorded as not met |
| Parallel waves (item 1) | implemented and witnessed (overlap in run 2026-11-01-11-27-17-d7f205); effect not measured | writes are too short to show a gain (42.6 ms on, 27.6 ms off) |
| Retry (item 3) | implemented, scripted witness | the live network error and the live retry-after header are not witnessed |
| Prefix hash (item 4) | implemented | gm-new sends no request; the host manages the cache, and its transcript reports cache usage |
| Queryable log (F1) | implemented, not measured | none; every event is logged and query_log reads it |
| Rules re-read each step (F3) | implemented, violation rate not tested | rules.md is re-read each CLI step and on each next_context call |
| Context as a worker action (F4) | implemented in part | query_log only |
| Validated compaction (F2) | deferred (row unknown-compaction-rl) | the log is complete, so no summary exists |
| RL summariser (F5) | deferred (row unknown-compaction-rl) | needs training infrastructure |
| Saturation threshold (F7) | deferred (row unknown-compaction-rl) | the threshold is unverified |
| Subagent isolation | open | row unknown-subagent-isolation |
| Proactive mode [row unknown-future-directions], verification-gated admission, matched-budget falsifier, raw-replay arm (directions 8 to 11) | deferred | no learned artifact exists yet |
| Minimal tool set | open | cost and trigger under open questions; row unknown-minimal-tool-set |

Research status, added 2026-11-01 (one line per open item):
- Validated compaction [row unknown-compaction-rl]: deferred. The log is complete, so no summary exists. Direction 2 builds it only when the log is too big to query.
- Subagent isolation [row unknown-subagent-isolation] (open questions 3 and 4): deferred. These need a long-horizon run that shows a single worker's limit.
- Rule re-injection [row unknown-rule-reinjection] (open question 1): open. It needs live trials, so it waits for the owner's live-trial budget.
- Minimal tool set [row unknown-minimal-tool-set]: open and load-gated. It does not run under load, and it is not requested.
- Proactive mode, verification-gated admission, the matched-budget falsifier and the raw-replay arm [row unknown-future-directions] (directions 8 to 11): future, not built.
- Completed-job surfacing (gm agent build 2, 2026-11-01): built. `next_context` carries `jobsEnded`: every job that ended since the last `next_context`, with its name, state (`exited` or `killed`), exit code, signal, and the first 20000 characters of its output (`next` is the offset for `job_status`). It works like `jobsKilled`. A worker counts from the last `next_context` action in the log, not from its own start, so a job that a restart stopped is named by the first `next_context` of the new worker. Witness, scripted against `src/mcp.mjs` (run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/jobsended/run-1793563621174/run`): a 3 s job ended, and the next `next_context` named it with state exited, exit code 0 and output `finished-three` (the job event at seq 8); the call after it named nothing. A 60 s job was running when its worker was paused with SIGTERM (state killed, signal SIGTERM, seq 19); the next worker on the same run named it in its first `next_context`, and the run had no end event. Limit: a worker that is SIGKILLed cannot write the `job` event, so a job it leaves behind is not reported as ended, and its shell can outlive it.
- Transient retry for the network verbs (gm agent build 1, 2026-11-01): built. `fetch`, `git_fetch`, `git_push` and `ci_status` retry a transient failure three times: a timeout, a reset or refused connection, a failed connect, the remote hanging up, HTTP 408, 429 or 5xx. The backoff starts at 1 s, doubles to a 16 s cap, with plus or minus 20% jitter. Each retry is a `retry` event with tool, attempt, errorType and delayMs. A permanent failure, and any other 4xx, fails at once. Witness, scripted against `src/mcp.mjs` (run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/retry/run-1793563463734/run`): `fetch` of a path that answers 503, 503 and then 200 logged retry events 4 (HTTP-503, 1094 ms) and 5 (HTTP-503, 2295 ms), then status 200 with attempts 3; `fetch` of a 404 logged no retry event; `git_fetch` to a closed port logged retry events 10, 11 and 12 (connect, 935, 2153 and 3250 ms), then ok false with attempts 4 and exhausted true (seq 13). Limits: no live network error and no live Retry-After header are witnessed, the Retry-After header is not read for these verbs, and `ci_status` uses the same classifier but was not run live, because `gh` is not run in this check.
Caveat: none of the evidence is a long-horizon PRD workload, and none of our live tests is long-horizon.
DeepSeek advantages, one line each (gm agent request, 2026-11-01): built, rejected or unbuildable, with the reason.
- Transient-failure retry in the network verbs: built (build 1, commit 72bd01f), witnessed in the research status list above.
- Completed-job surfacing, `jobsEnded` in `next_context`: built (build 2, commit fcf2ad3), witnessed in the research status list above.
- Recovery note after a restart, and persisted request ids: built and witnessed by the long-horizon runs above.
- Job lifetime limit and the `job-killed` notice: built and witnessed by the long-horizon runs above.
- Complete append-only log, queried with `query_log`, with no summarisation and no rule compaction: built. Rules are re-read on every step and never compacted.
- A waiting or idle state: unbuildable. MCP gives no turn-end signal from the host, so gm-new cannot tell when the host waits for the user. Decided, not built.
- Per-ticket directories: rejected. The owner bans worktrees and clones, and the run log already separates the runs. Decided, not built.
- Subagent isolation [row unknown-subagent-isolation]: deferred, not built. Trigger: a long-horizon run that shows a single worker's limit (see the subagent isolation entries above).
- Validated compaction and an RL summariser [row unknown-compaction-rl]: deferred (see the research status above).
- Proactive mode, verification-gated admission, the matched-budget falsifier and the raw-replay arm: future, not built. The falsifier is triggered only when gm-new writes a reusable artifact from its runs (direction 10).
Long-horizon design (recovery note, persisted request ids, job lifetime, complete log): documented in the DeepSeek superagent prototype's documents (003-execution.md, 007-architecture.md, 000-philosophy.md). The grade is documented design from a research prototype, not measured. It is built here and witnessed by scripted runs, but it is untested on our workload.

Gaps the research says to close:

- G1, overlap of the next model call with tool work (C2): built. A CLI-only `--overlap` flag, default off. It is witnessed in scripted runs, and it has no live run. MCP cannot overlap, because the host owns the loop.
- G2, parallel gain (C1): built and measured with simulated latency. `--latency-ms N` is scripted only, and every event it touches carries `simulatedMs`. The numbers below are simulated, not live.
- G3, cache effect (C3): measured by the gm agent as a cache share from the host transcripts, not as a saving. Arm A is about 91% and arm B about 83% (medians, quiet trials). No saving is claimed, because the price multipliers are unverified here. See "Measured".

Limit, kept for safety: `run_shell` always runs alone. Parallel waves cover `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir` and `job_status`. `job_start` is a barrier as well, because it starts a shell. The shell barrier stays.

## Live comparison, quiet batch

The quiet batch ran at cap 1, one attempt per trial, with the pause rule above a 1-minute load of 6. Load was recorded at each trial's start and end. Quiet means the start load was under 6. Each task was held to 3 valid quiet trials per arm. Medians, with spread as min to max, are from valid quiet trials only.

Arm A (gm-new), 9 valid quiet trials, 3 per task, all passed:

- t1: wall 21384 ms (17890 to 36762), idle 1378 ms, tool calls 7 (gm-new MCP 6).
- t2: wall 33097 ms (28166 to 42082), idle 1991 ms, tool calls 12 (MCP 11).
- t3: wall 26036 ms (25788 to 49736), idle 482 ms, tool calls 10 (MCP 9). The first t3 trial of the batch was the t3-2 re-attempt, and it was valid.

Arm B (gm), 9 valid quiet trials, 3 per task, all passed:

- t1: wall 31093 ms (29339 to 36576), idle 1463 ms, tool calls 3 (gm MCP 2).
- t2: wall 48995 ms (47362 to 55716), idle 3387 ms, tool calls 6 (MCP 4).
- t3: wall 53406 ms (36970 to 83288), idle 4482 ms, tool calls 8 (MCP 6). One trial used one MCP call out of six tool calls.

Loaded trials: none for either arm.

Unrecorded trials, not used in the comparison: arm A has 10 valid, and arm B has 2 valid (t1), all run before load was recorded.

Invalid trials, listed and not dropped: arm A has 9 records, all readiness failures. Six were superseded by a valid run of the same slot, and three are the failed attempts at slot t3-2, which the quiet batch later filled with a valid trial. Arm B has 2 records, both from before the unknown-command resend was added.

Comparison, on quiet trials only, with 9 against 9 trials: arm A's median wall time is lower on all three tasks, and its median idle time is lower on t2 and t3. The spreads overlap on t1, and the arms differ in tool strategy as well as in harness: arm B's skill is the served prompt, while arm A's is copied in. With 3 trials per task, these are medians, not significance tests.

## Code surface, pre-registration (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01)

Recorded before any run of either surface. The criteria below decide. A result is not read against a preferred outcome.

- Surfaces: `tools` (the current per-tool surface, default) and `code` (`--surface code`: the tool list is `next_context`, `run_code` and `finish`, and `run_code` takes a JavaScript body that calls the same verbs as `gm.<verb>(args)`). The witness rule, the commit gate and the rules re-read are the same on both surfaces.
- Tasks, fixed. Each trial starts in a fresh temporary workspace that the harness fills with the same files.
  - T1: `a.txt` and `b.txt` each hold lines. Write `c.txt` with the first line of `a.txt` followed by the first line of `b.txt`, then finish with a witness. Checker: `c.txt` has exactly those two lines.
  - T2: count the lines that contain `TODO` in the `.mjs` files of the workspace. Write the count to `count.txt`, then finish. Checker: `count.txt` holds the scripted count.
  - T3: start a background job that sleeps 3 seconds and writes `done.txt`. While it runs, write `other.txt`. Poll the job until it has exited, then finish. Checker: `done.txt` and `other.txt` exist and the job state is `exited`.
- Pass: the checker passes, and the run ends with `finish` and a non-empty witness, with status `finished`.
- Host tokens per task: the sum of `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` over the assistant messages of the task's host transcript, each message counted once by its message id. The primary figure is this total. Input plus output alone is reported as a secondary figure, not decided on.
- Steps per task: the number of assistant messages that contain a tool call.
- Trials: at least three per task per surface. gm's codemode runs the same three tasks as the comparison arm.
- Decision rule, on pooled trials of T1 to T3: the `code` surface is kept only if (a) its median host tokens per task is at least 20% lower than the `tools` surface's median, (b) its median steps is lower, and (c) its pass rate is not lower. If any of the three fails, the `tools` surface stays the default.
- Load rule: no live trial starts while the one-minute load average is above 6 (DESIGN.md, Live comparison).
- Scripted measurements, which make no model call, run first: (1) the bytes and the approximate tokens (bytes / 4) of each surface's tool list; (2) the MCP calls one fixed scripted run of T1 to T3 needs on each surface; (3) how many prefix hashes change when one tool description is edited, for each description in turn.
- Live measurements are model runs. They are reported to the gm agent to run, and this repository does not run them (AGENTS.md, hard rules).
- Code surface built (gm agent change of plan, 2026-11-01): `--surface code` serves `next_context`, `finish` and `run_code`. `run_code` takes a JavaScript async body that receives `gm`, with one method per verb (`gm.<name>(args)`) and `gm.verbs()` for each verb's description and schema as data. Each verb call is a `code-call` event with its start, duration and result; a failed call returns `{ok:false, error}` and does not stop the body. The body is not sandboxed: it runs in the worker's process, as `run_shell` does. The default was `--surface tools` when this was built; the owner made code the default later (see the batch record). Witness, scripted (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/surface/smoke-1793564287422/run`): the served list is the three tools; a direct `read_file` was refused (seq 4); a body read `a.txt` and `b.txt` and wrote `c.txt` with the first line of each, and a read of a missing file came back as data (code-call seq 8 to 11); `finish` ended the run at seq 16.

- Scripted measurement, step 4 (no model call, 2026-11-01): `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/surface/measure-1793564386798/summary.json`, run against a copy of the tree at commit c7dffea. (1) tools/list: the tools surface has 35 tools and 10234 bytes, about 2559 tokens (bytes / 4); the code surface has 3 tools and 1446 bytes, about 362 tokens, which is 14.1% of the tools surface's bytes. (2) MCP calls for the fixed script of each task: tools surface T1 5, T2 4, T3 5, 14 in all; code surface T1 3, T2 3, T3 3, 9 in all. All six runs passed their checkers and finished (run directories `run-tools-3` to `run-tools-5` and `run-code-6` to `run-code-8` under the same base). (3) prefixSha changes when one description is edited, one at a time: tools surface 35 of 35 served descriptions (the six row tools are edited through their template in `rows.mjs`, three edits that each change two served descriptions); code surface 3 of 3 served descriptions; code surface verb descriptions 0 of 33, which is the point of the code surface. Limits: one run per task per surface, not three trials; the scripts are fixed, so the calls are a floor and not what a model does; the steps and host tokens of the pre-registration are live measures and are not decided here. The default stays `--surface tools` until the live trials decide.

- Discovery reply, scripted (2026-11-01, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/surface/discovery/run-1793564678566/run`): `gm.verbs()` inside `run_code` returns 33 verbs with their descriptions and input schemas, 9654 bytes, about 2414 tokens (bytes / 4). The tools surface's whole list is 2559 tokens, and the code surface's list is 362. The verb names alone are 318 bytes. The pre-registered host-token measure counts this reply in any live run that calls it, so one `gm.verbs()` call spends most of the code surface's prefix saving. The surface is not changed while the live trials run. Measured: the discovery reply is 9654 bytes (run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/surface/discovery/run-1793564678566/run`), against 10234 bytes for the tools surface's whole list (`run-tools-1` in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/surface/measure-1793564386798`). The descriptions and the discovery call stay as they are for this comparison. A names-only split is a new variant, and it needs its own pre-registration after this comparison closes.

## Live batch, closed (orchestrator, 2026-11-01)

The first live batch compared gm-new's two surfaces under the pre-registration in b2301ca. gm's codemode ran as a reference arm, for context only. Results file: `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-orch/live/results.json`, 27 trials, 9 per arm, across T1, T2 and T3 with three trials each. Every trial ran at HEAD 8dbfcc2 (one distinct HEAD across all 27). The served tool-list hash was recorded on each trial: tools 727388701771, code ce4c24c8570d.

Pooled medians over the 9 trials per arm:

| Arm | Host tokens | Input+output | Steps | Passes | Wall ms |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tools (default) | 211544 | 2646 | 9 | 9/9 | 33007 |
| code | 216024 | 2992 | 8 | 9/9 | 33315 |
| gm (reference only) | 356318 | 5654 | 7 | 7/9 | 73394 |

Per task (host tokens, steps): tools T1 199414 / 8, T2 293425 / 11, T3 284664 / 10. Code T1 282227 / 10, T2 226857 / 9, T3 170648 / 7. gm T1 300661 / 5, T2 322440 / 5, T3 657312 / 11 (one of three passed).

Decision, by the pre-registered rule:
- Tokens at least 20% lower than tools: no. Code is 2.1% higher (216024 against 211544).
- Steps lower: yes (8 against 9).
- Pass rate not lower: yes (9/9 against 9/9).
- The pre-registered rule's result: the code surface is not kept, and the tools surface would stay the default.
- Owner judgement, which overrides the pre-registered rule (2026-11-01): code mode is gm-new's default surface. The numbers behind it are parity on passes (9 of 9 on both), steps 8 against 9, and tokens within 2% (216024 against 211544, +2.1%). The tools surface stays available behind `--surface tools` as the fallback. The owner does not want more comparative runs, so the gm-new versus gm draft is dropped and no trials are run. The judgement is the owner's, not the rule's: the rule's result is recorded beside it, so the override is visible.

The gm arm is reference only, and it is not an input to the decision. Deviations: gm's codemode has no finish tool, so its pass is the checker plus a final statement of the result; the arm uses its own served prompt, while the task text was the orchestrator's and is the same for every arm; two T3 trials failed with task_spawn; the arm was launched with `node -e`. Verb names differ per arm, and each trial records the names it used.

Not decided here: module mode and the JIT verb stay parked in scratch, because they were built on the code surface, and the owner's judgement above makes that the default. Nothing is registered for them, and no trial runs. The gm-new versus gm draft is dropped (owner decision, 2026-11-01), and no trials are run.

## Batch witness pass, 2026-11-01

One witness pass covered steps 2 to 5 of the batch before they landed. Report: /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/witness-pass/pass-1793569335788/report.txt. Script: pass2.mjs, run against the final-state copy at /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/final-state2.
- Step 1, the default flip (landed 4a4ecc9): a default launch serves three tools (next_context, finish, run_code), and the tools flag serves 35. Served hashes at this pass: the tools surface 727388701771fdf9, and the default surface a37f7a88ae22c134 (its run_code description changed with step 2).
- Step 2, the imports (landed c931986): an ES module helper loads by static import, single-line and multi-line; a .js helper follows the package type module; a CommonJS helper loads by require. An escaping require, an escaping static import and an escaping dynamic import are each refused and logged as refusal events, and the body does not load. Body files left after the calls: 0. Run directories: pass-1793569335788/esm/run and pass-1793569335788/cjs/run.
- Step 3, the rows and the DESIGN backfill: 24 row files under work/rows/, and 30 DESIGN lines carry a row id.
- Step 4, gm-continue: the plan prints before any work; the handoff is withheld without a witness pass and printed after one; an actionable row left unattempted is a defect and does not stop the pass; an attempted row blocked with its fact stops the pass and is listed; the skill check passes the repo's two skills and refuses a skill that names read_file without gm.
- Step 5, the rename: the skill is served as gm, and the resource gm://skill/SKILL.md reads with the gm-continue invariant and the flow rule. The old URI is refused. Witness, run /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/witness-pass/rename-1793569400228: served prompts gm; the prompt names run_code and gm.verbs(); the resource read has both invariants; gm-new://skill/SKILL.md is refused; the gm-continue setup dry run passes.
- The served skill and the initialize instructions both name run_code and gm.verbs(), and neither names a tool the default does not serve.

## Review functions (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01)

The gm agent's Elephant-Goldfish decision: the research found no measured benefit for the method, apart from one single-author preprint, so the functions embed only what lets this log measure the effect. They are plain functions in `src/review.mjs`, with no tool name. They are registered only after the live comparison closes, because a new tool or a discovery entry would change what is measured.

- Packet, `reviewPacket`: the spec's path, its sha256, and the sorted referenced paths, which must all exist in the workspace. Its hash is the sha256 of that content in canonical JSON. It logs a `review-packet` event. A missing reference, or a spec that is not a file, is refused and logged as `review-refused`.
- Record, `reviewRecord`: one reviewer's verdict on one packet, keyed by the packet's hash. The host spawns the fresh reviewer and passes its reply in. It is refused when the packet is not in this log, when the packet already has a record, when the verdict is not pass, fail or needs-changes, when there is no reviewer, or when the spec's sha has changed since the packet.
