Each record says `isolation: host-attested`. gm-new does not verify reviewer isolation.
- Join, `reviewJoin`: one row per record, with the outcomes after it, up to the next record, in log order: each commit with its gate outcome, each `git_commit` result, and each end with its status and witness. The join now checks the spec's sha at each commit, read from the commit's own tree (`git show <sha>:<spec>`), and at the end from the working tree. A mismatch goes to `unattributed` with reason after-edit, and it is not attributed to the record. The join takes the repository root as its second argument; without it every commit is unattributed. Witness, landed code (gm release of the freeze, 2026-11-01), run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/review/landed-1793568639448/run`: commit 1, which the record covers, is attributed (seq 8, gate ok, and its git_commit result at seq 9). A spec edit committed as commit 2 (seq 13), the commit after it (seq 18), and the finish (seq 24, the working-tree spec) are unattributed with reason after-edit. The earlier witness, which called the join without the root, is superseded.
- Not built as defaults: no pre-edit spec gate, no human approval gate, and no claim that gm-new verifies isolation. A pre-edit gate is a later opt-in flag, added only after it is measured.
- Witness, run `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/review/run-1793564909795/run` (driver `drive.mjs` beside it). Packet hash `152f1ccc2dcd1ff05c5e3b3ef46369292cd7f890899fdcd5fafded02aaa8e8c6` (seq 1); the packet with a missing reference was refused (seq 2). A record keyed by that hash (seq 3, verdict pass, isolation host-attested). A second packet, `4b43f1a3259d8bca5ddbb25c0cbb41a8d4fba359ca32d1651f53a4bec8d9e42b` (seq 4), whose spec was edited afterwards; its record was refused (seq 5, "the spec changed since packet"). The worker then committed the reviewed files (commit at seq 12, gate ok; git_commit result at seq 13, ok) and finished (end at seq 18, status finished, witness "review witness: spec and notes committed after the pass record"). The join of those events gives one row: the pass record at seq 3, with those three outcomes.

## Wiki (gm agent decision, 2026-11-01)

The gm agent asked for the compounding wiki of Karpathy's "LLM Wiki" gist (April 2026, gist 442a6bf555914893e9891c11519de94f). The wiki items are plain functions in a wiki module, with no new tool names (gm agent decision, the code-surface change of plan). Each item is done when its witness is in the log.

- Item 1, raw source archive: built. `fetch` saves the full body, up to 1 MB, as ASCII text in `sources/<id>.txt`. The id is the first 12 hex digits of the sha256 of the stored bytes. Every non-ASCII UTF-16 code unit is written as a `\uXXXX` escape, so the repo's ASCII rule holds and the text can be recovered. The one lossy step is invalid UTF-8, which becomes U+FFFD. A file is written once (`wx`), so a fetch of an existing id leaves it unchanged. The reply carries `source` and `sourceNew`, and each fetch adds one line to `sources/index.jsonl`. The gate's `sources` check refuses any file whose hash does not match its name. Witness, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/sources-fix/run-1793564150358`: `fetch` of example.com gave source 25ddf2c883e0 (sourceNew true); the second fetch gave the same id with sourceNew false, and the file's mtime did not change; the copy's commit of the archive passed `sources`; after the file was edited, the commit was refused with `sources` failing (the file has the hash of 1a56ad7a0901).
- Item 2, wiki pages: built. `wikiWrite` (a plain function in `src/wiki.mjs`, with no tool name) writes `wiki/<page>.md` with the source ids and the claims in its frontmatter. Each claim's quote is matched, after the same ASCII escaping, against its named source. A quote that is not in the source refuses the whole page, and nothing is written. Witness, plain function calls (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wiki/witness23-1793564491099/run`): a true quote was accepted and its page written; a made-up quote was refused with "claim 1: the quote is not in source" and the source id, and no page was written.
- Item 3, index and log: built. Each write sets the page's line in `wiki/index.md` (`- [[page]] - summary`, replaced when the page is already there) and appends `## [date] kind | title` to `wiki/log.md`. Witness, same run: the index holds `- [[halden-bridge]] - Opened in 1912, spans 340 metres`, the log holds `## [2026-11-01] ingest | Halden Bridge`, and the run log has a `wiki` event at seq 1. A first draft wrote the event as `ingest`, because a field named `kind` overwrote the event kind; the field is now `writeKind`.
- Item 4, query: built. `wikiQuery` reads `wiki/index.md` first, and reports the characters it read. It ranks the index entries with the search BM25: `lineOf`, `buildIndex`, `rank` and `tokensOf` are exported from `src/search.mjs` unchanged. It returns the best k pages with their summaries, and it files nothing: an answer is written with `wikiWrite` and kind `answer`. Witness, plain calls (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wiki/witness4-1793564518464/run`): the query "when was the halden bridge opened" read 192 characters of index and returned `wiki/halden-bridge.md | Opened in 1912, spans 340 metres` first, with score 2.616. The answer was filed with kind `answer`, and its log line is `## [2026-11-01] answer | When Halden Bridge opened`.
- Item 5, lint: built. `lintWiki` (in `src/wiki.mjs`) runs in the gate as the `wiki` check, and at finish, where it writes a `lint` event to the log and does not refuse. It checks that every page has an index entry and that the index lists no missing page; that every `[[link]]` resolves; that no page is an orphan, meaning no other page links to it (the index does not count); that every cited source is in `sources/` and every claim's quote is still in its source; and that every page has a line in the log. Any problem refuses the commit. Witness, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wiki/witness56-1793564594153/b/run`: two linked pages, alpha and beta, committed with the wiki check ok ("2 pages, 2 claims, lint clean"); a third page, gamma, whose index line was removed, was refused by the gate with the wiki check failing on "page gamma has no entry in wiki/index.md" and the orphan rule; finish logged a `lint` event with the same two problems.
- Item 6, staleness and contradictions: built. A page is stale when its source's url was archived again as a new id after the page was written; `sources/index.jsonl` records each fetch's url, id and time. A page's `contradicts` names the page it conflicts with. The contradiction is open, and listed, until a later page whose `resolves` names either of them is written. Witness, plain calls (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wiki/witness56-1793564594153/a/run`): the baseline was clean; archiving a new version of the Ostrava url flagged `delta`, which cites 73350f3f11fb, as stale (archived again as 0023ea8045ad); rewriting `delta` on the new version with `contradicts` naming `epsilon` listed "open contradiction: delta contradicts epsilon"; writing `zeta` with `resolves` naming `delta` cleared it, and the lint was clean.
- A page's `updated` field is written as a JSON string. A first draft wrote it bare, which the lint could not parse, and the fix is in the same commit as items 5 and 6.
- Item 7, measurement (scripted, no model call, 2026-11-01): `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wiki/measure7-1793564651503/summary.json`, script `measure7.mjs` beside it. 20 sources, each about 360 characters (a name, six fixed sentences and two fact sentences), and 20 pages written by a fixed script: each page carries its source's two claims, links to the neighbouring pages in a ring, and a summary that names the opening year only. 10 questions, one for each of the first ten sources: five about the year, five about the length. Characters read before the cited source was reached. Through the wiki (the index read and the query output, then the pages in rank order): reached 10 of 10, median 1280.5, total 12797. Through search alone (the search output lines in rank order, over the same sources with no wiki): reached 10 of 10, median 93, total 933. Of the wiki figure, the index is 873 characters, about two thirds, and one page is 353. On this corpus the search-only path read less than the wiki path, for every question. The gist makes no measured claim, so this is the only evidence for the wiki. It is one small synthetic corpus, one run, with summaries written by a script. The index read grows with the number of pages, so the result may change with corpus size; that is not measured here. Recorded as measured, with no saving claimed: at 20 sources the wiki path reads a median of 1280.5 characters, against 93 for search alone. The next measurement must run at 100 or more sources and score answer quality, not only characters read. Answer quality is not measured yet, and the gist makes no measured claim about the wiki, so the compounding synthesis it describes is untested here.

## Run code: freshness and format (2026-11-02)

- Decision, no sandbox (orchestrator, gm agent, 2026-11-02; the owner may reverse it): a run_code body runs in the worker's process, as run_shell does, against the project as it is on disk. Reason: run_shell already runs unconfined, so run_code adds no new authority.
- Browser: held (owner decision, 2026-11-02). Not built.
- Freshness: each call that loads project files takes a new generation, and the resolve hook (src/freshness.mjs) gives every project file the generation as its query. Node caches modules by URL, so before this change the first version of a file stayed for the life of the worker. Witness before: run `runcode-fresh/run-1793570486387`, where all three values stayed 1 after the edit. Witness after: run `runcode-fresh/run-1793570604162`, where the ES module, the module that imports it and the CommonJS file all read 2. A per-file content hash was tried first and failed for the chained case, because the importing file had not changed. Reason: an edit must reach the next call, including a file that imports the edited one.
- Memory, measured (scripted, no model call; `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/memory/loop.mjs`, 1000 run_code calls per run, worker RSS from /proc). Every call returned its expected value in every run.
  - ES module body that loads a project module, each call (run `memory/import-1793570735448`): RSS 75,604 KB before the loop and 127,220 KB after, 51.6 KB per call. The first 500 calls grew 64.6 KB per call and the last 500 grew 38.6 KB per call. The log held 3,009 events.
  - Control, ES module body that loads nothing (run `memory/plain-1793570738800`): 44.2 KB per call. So an ES module body instance costs about 17 KB per call, and a project module instance about 7 KB per call.
  - Control, CommonJS body (run `memory/cjs-1793570741735`, which keeps no module instance): 27.6 KB per call, with 2,008 events. The call path and the in-memory event log account for about 28 KB per call.
  - Retained heap, with a forced GC before each sample (run `memory/import-1793570765530`, worker started with --expose-gc for this run only): heapUsed 7.4 MB at call 100 and 14.5 MB at call 1000, about 8 KB per call retained. The rest of the RSS growth is memory the allocator has not returned.
  - Noise: the same import loop measured 51.6 KB per call in one run and 41.8 KB per call in another.
  - Not measured: restart cost with a log of about 25,000 events.
- Bound, built 2026-11-02: the supervisor restarts the worker when its RSS passes GMNEW_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB (default 512), checked after each line the worker sends, so after each reply, and a restart waits for calls in flight. From a 75 MB start at 52 KB per call, that is about 8,400 module-loading calls, so the bound should rarely fire. The run resumes from its log after the restart. The bound covers growth across calls. It does not cover one call that allocates past the limit, because the check runs between calls. The hard limit for that case is a worker started with --max-old-space-size, which ends the process instead. The witness at a 100 MB limit is in the decisions section below.
- Format: run_code takes format esm or cjs. Left out, it follows the project's package.json type (type module is esm, anything else is cjs). A cjs body is written as CommonJS and loads with require(); a static import in it is refused and logged. Witness: run `runcode-fresh/format-1793570615753`. A cjs body returns 1. A static import is refused (seq 9). An esm body in a cjs project returns the helper's value (seq 12). An unknown format is refused. An escaping require is refused and logged (seq 17). A cjs file edited between calls reads 2. Witness: run `runcode-fresh/esmformat-1793570604488`, where format cjs in an esm project returns 3 through require, and the default there is an esm body.
- The run_code description and input schema changed, so the code-surface hash changed.

## Decisions, 2026-11-02 (agent; revised by the joint decision below)

The agent made these decisions under a delegation that the orchestrator disputed. The joint decision in the section "Dependency rule, joint decision" revised the search, store, insight and wiki bullets, and it is marked in each. Each decision is one line with its reason and its witness. Witness runs are in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/final/`, and the run id is the directory name.

- Parser for callers and impact: acorn 8.18.0, parsed once per file and cached by mtime and size (`src/code-ast.mjs`). Reason: a regex gave false positives inside strings and missed `const NAME = x.NAME(...)`. Witness, run `run-1793571487908`: callers for planWaves, wikiWrite, runBody, createTools and reviewJoin equal a grep oracle exactly (1, 1, 1, 3 and 1 sites). Query time is 102 ms for the first call, which includes the parse, and 9 to 14 ms after. TypeScript and files that do not parse are scanned by lines and marked approximate.
- Search, the SQLite FTS5 index (rejected and reverted by the joint decision, 2026-11-02; the file src/search-index.mjs is removed): it ranked through node:sqlite, one hit per file. The embedder and the vector index were dropped, and their dependencies with them. Reason, measured: recall at 5 over 18 queries (10 natural, 8 identifier) on this repository was lexical 9 and 8, semantic 8 and 5, and fused 9 and 6. A semantic weight of 0.25 or more in the fusion lost identifier hits, and 0.1 gained nothing (sweep in `final/weights.mjs`). Through the served tool, run `run-1793571487908`: 17 of 18. The miss is log.mjs for a generic query. A new file is found by the next search (the probe in the same run).
- The store moved from libsql to node:sqlite (rejected and reverted by the joint decision, 2026-11-02). Reason at the time: node:sqlite is built in, has FTS5 with bm25 (checked on Node 24), and needs no native binary. The decision that holds (gm, 2026-11-02): stay with the in-process BM25, with no startup and no native module. The blockers are the 13.2 MB of idle resident memory per worker, measured (see the Dependency rule section), and the native-code rule. The in-process BM25 was measured on the same 18 queries and the same snapshot: 16 of 18 through the served search tool (run recall-js/run-1793572079413), against 17 of 18 for FTS5. The gap is one query, the retry query (src/retry.mjs ranks 10); both indexes miss the append-only log query, and the FTS5 run's query text was not logged, so the one miss recorded for FTS5 is taken from the note above. libsql's vector support was real and was not used, because the vector side measured no gain.
- Insight (dropped by the joint decision, 2026-11-02, with thorns and its submodule): the insight verb ran thorns analyze on the workspace and returned one overview: files and lines by language, the dependency graph, orphan and circular files, duplicated code, complexity hotspots and the most used identifiers, capped at 20000 characters. An overview can be a run_code script over built-ins when someone needs one. Witness, run `run-1793571487908`: insight ok, 5915 characters.
- Wiki: `wiki_write` and `wiki_search` are served. `wiki_search` ranks the wiki pages with the in-process BM25, tracked or not, since the revert. `wikiQuery` was removed in the FTS5 change, and the orchestrator accepted that removal: it ranked only the index summaries, through BM25 helpers that the change deleted, and `wiki_search`, which ranks the full pages, replaces it. No separate reason was recorded, and no quality comparison was run. Witness, run `run-1793571487908`: a page written from an archived source, found by wiki_search, and a made-up quote refused with no page written.
- Review: `review_packet`, `review_record` and `review_join` are served. Reason: the live comparison that held them back closed on 2026-11-01. Witness, run `run-1793571487908`: a packet, a pass record, and a join that lists the record with isolation host-attested.
- Memory bound built (see the memory section). Witness, run `run-1793571487908`, with the limit at 100 MB: 1500 of 1500 calls answered, and 21 restarts logged as memory limit 100 MB passed. The bound is checked between replies, so one call that allocates past the limit is not caught before it ends. The hard limit for that case is `--max-old-space-size`, which ends the process instead. Restart cost with a log of 25,000 events is not measured.
- Browser: not built. Reason: no project needs it, and the owner held it.
- Minimal tool set: closed, not run. Reason: no decision depends on it. A live run needs model trials that gm-new does not make; they go to the gm agent, and they wait for a load under 6 and a runner fix that is blocked.
- CI: closed. The gate is the check (existing decision).
- Windows process-group kill: a named limit, because there is no Windows host.
- Rule re-injection, subagent isolation and validated compaction stay deferred, with the triggers in the open-items section.
- The gm runner's `.gm/` and `.agentplug-kv/` ignore entries stay. The runner serves every gm session on this host, so its files are the owner's decision.
- Dependencies on gm: the removals listed above stand. The airc CI witness is not run, because CI is closed.

## Dependency rule, joint decision (owner and orchestrator, 2026-11-02)

The owner and the orchestrator decided this together, weighed on efficiency and turns, with the many-agents-per-machine goal first. It replaces the zero-dependency rule in the Language line above.

- Kept: acorn 8.18.0, pinned exactly, as the one runtime dependency. It is small, has no native code, and gives exact call sites, which cuts the turns spent verifying callers and impact. callers and impact stay on acorn. The verbs wiki_write, review_packet, review_record and review_join stand, with the memory bound at GMNEW_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB (default 512), browser not built, the minimal tool set closed, and CI closed.
- Dropped: thorns and its submodule, and the insight verb. node:sqlite and the FTS5 index. The Node floor of 22.5, which goes back to 20. search and wiki_search are back to the in-process BM25.
- Rule for any further dependency: a measured gain in turns or accuracy, and a measured memory cost per worker, recorded here first. No native code and no submodules.
- The owner's statements, as recorded: "the project doesnt have to be zero dependencies, these have been iterated a lot for lightness" (earlier in this session); "ok you make all decisions from here on out you have enough information to decide whats best across the board, we want gm-new completely finished, we like your acorn decisions, pick whatevers most performant" (the command arguments, 2026-11-02); and "you make all decisions from here on out" (the answer to the question on the dependency conflict, 2026-11-02).
- Measured, idle resident memory of the worker (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/rss/idle.mjs`: start, initialize, one next_context, two seconds idle, then VmRSS). Before, at 9687875 with thorns, the insight verb and node:sqlite loaded: 75,500, 75,640 and 75,764 KB, median 75,640 KB (73.9 MB). After, with acorn only: 62,024, 62,084 and 62,096 KB, median 62,084 KB (60.6 MB). The difference is 13,556 KB, about 13.2 MB, or 18 percent.
- Witness, run `run-1793571974475` (a copy of the tree, with the worker run from the repository): callers for planWaves, wikiWrite, runBody, createTools and reviewJoin equal a grep oracle exactly (1, 1, 1, 3 and 1 sites). impact of reviewJoin names src/review.mjs as defining, src/review-tools.mjs as calling, and lists its importers. search finds src/loop.mjs for planWaves. wiki_search finds a wiki page written untracked, and search does not return it.
- Measured: the in-process BM25 on the 18 recall queries, 16 of 18 through the served search tool (recall-js/run-1793572079413, on the snapshot of final/run-1793571487908). The FTS5 figure on the same snapshot is 17 of 18. The gap is one query, the retry query. The decision is to stay with the in-process BM25 (gm, 2026-11-02).

## Feed compaction (Decided change, 2026-11-02)

The owner asked for the most compact feed the agent can use. Each event should be transferred in full once, when the agent first sees it, and after that only in compact form, within two horizons. gm approved it as a Decided change, with these constraints, which hold: the log stays complete and query_log reaches all of it; replacement records are fixed templates, not summaries, so no model writes them and nothing is trained; the rules are re-read at every step and stay first in the prompt; the witness requirement is unchanged; a replacement record keeps seq, kind, tool, path, bytes changed, exit status, error text, refusal reasons and witness text; output compaction never drops an exit code, an error line, or the first and last lines of a result, and each compacted output records its full size and the seq of the full output; the horizon lengths come from measurement; the compactors are deterministic.

The rule, as built (src/compact.mjs). next_context returns the events after the last feed in full, at most 20 of them. It returns the events already seen as compact records: H1 = 14 events back, then H2 = 12 further back. Older events are not in the feed, and a count `older` says how many. The last feed is read from the log: it is the total on the last next_context result. Each module owns its strategy next to its tool, as a COMPACT export: tools.mjs, search.mjs, fetch.mjs, git.mjs, code-ast.mjs, rows.mjs, wiki-tools.mjs, review-tools.mjs and runcode.mjs. The lifecycle events are in mcp.mjs. The clock fields of a full event (t, mono, inFlight, startMono, durMs) are raw log data, not compactor output; a compact record has none of them.

Two changes the constraints required. (1) A verb called inside run_code was logged as a code-call with its name, ok and durMs only, so its output and exit code were not in the log. query_log could not reach them, and a failed shell call lost its exit code. A code-call now logs its arguments, exit code, signal, output and error. The log grows by those fields. (2) A failed call keeps its output whole up to 60 lines, since a failure's lines are not all marked by an error keyword, and its error lines are marked either way. A failed call with more than 60 lines records the count of the rest, and query_log reaches all of them. This is a gap against the strict constraint: lines beyond 60 of a failing call are not in its record.

Measurement. The scripted session (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/compact/session.mjs, 151 steps, a shell command that fails every seventh step and a body that throws every tenth; run session-1793595543749) gives 5030 bytes per step for the old feed, the last 20 events in full. Replayed step by step with the new rule, H1 = 14 and H2 = 12 give 4997 bytes per step. The event rate is 7.14 events per step (run session-1793595380012, the same script before the code-call fields were added, so the event count is unchanged). Trade-off, from the same run: H1 = 7 allows H2 = 24; H1 = 14 allows H2 = 12; H1 = 21 allows H2 = 2. H1 is two steps, which is what "a few turns" comes to here, and H2 keeps both horizons meaningful.

Witness, run witness2-1793595580169: two sessions of 60 steps with the same script, and a query for the failing calls. prefixSha is identical at every step. The compact records are identical, and the full records are identical once their clock fields are removed. Each event is sent in full at most once (431 events in full over the run). The 8 failing calls were compacted, and each kept its exit code 3 and its output line boom. query_log, asked by kind for the failing calls, returned the full output and exit code of seq 51 and seq 101. The older count was below every record shown. Replaying the log step by step reproduced every live feed.

Not measured here: the MCP round trip of query_log over a log of 3000 events. The in-process time was 0.065 ms at 3000 events on the MCP logs, measured earlier.

## Event delivery: channels rejected, next_context stays (2026-11-02)

The owner asked whether events could be pushed into the agent through MCP notifications, with a lookup that clears what it returns, instead of the feed. Checked against the Claude Code channels documentation (code.claude.com/docs/en/channels and /channels-reference, read 2026-11-02). A channel server's notifications/claude/channel events reach the model as channel tags. A custom server is outside the preview allowlist, so it needs the development flag --dangerously-load-development-channels server:<name>. The flag is ignored under -p, and events arrive only while the session is open, batched into the next turn.

Probe, in scratch and outside the repository (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/channel-probe): over stdio, the server advertised the channel capability, pushed a notification each period, and its lookup returned each pending event once, in order (events 1 to 3, then 4 and 5). Nothing from the probe is committed. The live part, whether the agent sees a pushed event without a tool call, was not run.

Decision (gm, 2026-11-02): no channel. A channel depends on a Claude Code development flag and on the host's experimental channel support. That is a feature that only one harness has, and gm-new uses no harness-only feature. It gains nothing either: the events batch into the agent's next turn, which next_context already does.

Accepted (gm, 2026-11-02): the lookup semantics, delivered through next_context and not a new verb. Each event the agent has not seen is delivered once in next_context, within the horizons of the approved compact feed, and query_log reaches anything older. This is the feed change already decided (Feed compaction above), so no new Decided change is needed.

## Stuck check and the open-jobs snapshot (agent decision under the owner's delegation, 2026-11-02)

The owner asked that the agent get all progress from every source when it queries, and also the notices about what is still happening, so that it can later tell when something has stuck. Decisions, made by the agent under the owner's delegation:

- A running job is stuck when it has made no progress for stuckMs, default 180000, the same as the run_code budget. Progress is: output written by a shell job (the mtime of its output file); a verb call a body has in flight, or has finished (the body's newest code-call in the log); or the job's start.
- A verb call in flight counts as progress until it returns, so a call that never returns is not flagged. Its own timeout is what bounds it.
- A queued body is waiting for a slot, not stuck. Its clock starts when its thread starts.
- The monitor checks every monitorMs, default 15000. It logs job-stuck once when a job becomes stuck, and job-unstuck once when it progresses again. A job that settles while stuck is not reported unstuck; its end notice is the report.
- next_context carries the changes since the last step as jobsStuck and jobsUnstuck, which are events and so are delivered once. It also carries jobsOpen, a snapshot of every open job (job, body, state, runningMs, sinceProgressMs, stuck), limited to 20 with jobsOpenMore for the rest. The snapshot is not an event, so nothing is logged for it, and it is sent at each step because it says what is happening now.
- The two numbers are flags, --stuck-ms and --monitor-ms. The witnesses use 2000 and 500.
- New event kinds, job-stuck and job-unstuck, use the generic compaction, which keeps their fields.

Witnesses, run on a copy of the working tree (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/next-tree) before the change was applied to the repository, so that the counts' tree was not changed while they ran:

- stuck-1793600083637: a silent shell job (sleep 8) is flagged at 2.4 s, and jobsOpen shows it stuck. A chatty job, which writes every second, is never flagged. A body that waits without a verb call is flagged at seq 19, unstuck when its list_dir lands at seq 21, and stuck again at seq 26. A body with a 5-second run_shell in flight is never flagged.
- queue-1793600107424: of five bodies that wait 4 seconds with four slots, the fifth is listed as queued, not stuck, with no job-stuck before its thread starts (body-started at seq 28).
- Regression on the copy, same seqs as before: budget (returns at 2055 ms, started at seq 7, exited at seq 12), lifetime (killed at seq 19, 9 calls before and 0 after), finish, restart (seq 9 and 10), crash (seq 12), shell (SIGKILL), cap (replies in order, at most 4 running, RSS 136 MB at peak) and capstop (no queued body starts, six killed).

## Decisions and named limits, 2026-11-02 (gm)

- The verb rename is decided against (row prd-verb-rename, closed). gm.verbs() stays, and it is not renamed to gm.list and gm.describe. Reason: the discovery cost is measured at 9654 bytes, and the signatures of the ten most-used verbs in run_code's description already cover them. A split would change the served surface for a benefit that has not been measured.
- No long live run has happened yet (named limit, row unknown-long-live-run, held). Trigger: the owner decides whether a long live run is required. The scripted long-horizon witnesses (run run-1793560605024, /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/longhorizon) are scripted evidence only. They are not a long live run.

## Relay list notices (2026-11-02)

After a swap of the supervisor, the relay compares the new worker's tools, prompts and resources lists with the lists the host last fetched. The relay keeps a digest of each host list response, so the comparison is against what the host has seen, not against the previous worker. For each kind that differs, it sends the host that kind's list_changed notice. A kind the host never fetched gets none, and nothing is sent when nothing differs. The supervisor's own list notices during a swap are dropped, since the relay decides them. A restart the supervisor makes from a source change still sends its three notices, as before.

Witness, run relay-witness/run-1793595783835: the new relay runs on a copy of the tree, with a scripted host, and three swaps are triggered by changes to tools/supervise.mjs. Swap A changes nothing: no notice. Swap B changes only the prompt argument description (src/mcp.mjs): only notifications/prompts/list_changed is sent. The supervisor's own restart before B sent its three notices, which are not part of the swap, and the host's next prompts/list returned the new description. Swap C, after a refetch, changes nothing: no notice. The worker's log seqs of the restarts are 4 (swap A), 7 (the mcp.mjs change), 10 (swap B) and 13 (swap C).

The live relay runs the code it started with until the owner reconnects once, so the live witness waits for that reconnect. The witness above is a scripted relay running the new tools/relay.mjs.

## Memory root fix (2026-11-02)

The generation is taken only when a project file changed. A fingerprint of the project's files (path, mtime and size, skipping node_modules, runs, .gm-new, .git, sources and vendor) is compared with the last one, and a body file is named by its text and the generation, so an identical body in the same generation is one module.

Witness, run memory-root/run-1793595966251 (a project with a helper, a leaf and a chain that imports the leaf): 1000 of 1000 calls that load the helper returned 1. Generation events: 1 after those calls with no edit, 2 after the helper was edited, 3 after the leaf was edited, and still 3 after one more call with no edit. The helper read 2 after its edit, and the chain read 2 after the leaf edit, so no import is stale.

Memory, measured with the loop tool on the fixed code (memory/fix-plain.json and memory/fix-import.json, 1000 calls each). The identical ESM body grew 21.0 KB per call, against 44.2 before this change. The body that imports a project module grew 24.4 KB per call, against 51.6 before. The module instances no longer accumulate. What remains is the event log, which the worker keeps in memory, and each call adds about five events. Resident memory is therefore not flat. A flat figure needs the log to leave memory, which is a storage change and not part of this fix. The restart bound at GMNEW_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB stays as the backstop.

## Horizon fields witness (2026-11-02)

Each kind the gates depend on is taken from a real log that holds it. The event is placed in the first horizon, and then in the second, by the feed code the worker uses (feedOf over the log up to the event). The script is /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/compact/horizon-fields.mjs. All 18 checks passed.

The records keep the fields the gates read. A commit (run review/run-1793564909795, landed-1793568639448) keeps its sha, paths and gate outcome in both horizons. A refusal (run runcode-fresh/format-1793570604328) keeps its reason. A finished end (run review/landed-1793568639448) keeps its status and its witness text. A closed end keeps its status and a witness key, which is null because nothing was finished. A review keeps its verdict and isolation, and a review-packet keeps its packet hash, in both horizons. A finish result keeps its witness and its full-output reference in both horizons. A failed shell call (run compact/witness2-1793595580169) keeps its exit code and the error line in the first horizon, and its exit code in the second.

query_log over the full log of run compact/witness2-1793595580169 reaches the first event (seq 1) and the last (seq 437).

## Log window (2026-11-02)

The log lives in its file. The worker keeps the byte offset of every event, one number each, and the last 512 events. Any other event is read from the file at its offset. A restart rebuilds the offsets and the window from the file, in chunks. The feed reads its events through the log (count and at), so it never holds the run. query_log and the other full-history callers read the file. The map of tools/call replies, which answers a repeated request id, keeps the last 256 requests instead of all of them.

Witness, run log-window/run-1793596211661: 1000 identical run_code calls, 0 failures. query_log, paged through run_code with after, returns every event of the log from seq 1 to seq 3098, contiguous. A restart on the same run directory resumes at step 3, and the resumed feed (18 records) equals the feed a replay of the file gives at that point.

Memory. Resident memory from call 20 to call 1000 grew 17.4 MB. That is the witness's figure, and it includes the heap V8 grows early on, so it is more than "a few MB". From call 100 to call 1000 it grew 6.4 MB (memory/window-heap.json, the loop tool in heap mode). The heap after a forced collection grew from 7.61 MB at call 100 to 8.15 MB at call 1000, about 0.6 KB per call. So the worker retains almost nothing per call. The residual RSS growth is V8 keeping the heap pages it has committed. A figure flatter than this needs a smaller starting heap or a V8 flag, and neither is set here.

The feed witness (compact/witness2, 60 steps) and the horizon-fields witness passed on the new log.

## Background budget for run_code (2026-11-02)

gm asked that a body never hold the agent for more than a few minutes, and never leave work the agent cannot see. The decisions, one line each with its reason:

- The foreground budget is 180 s by default and timeoutMs is at most 300000: a body must not hold the agent for more than a few minutes (gm).
- A body past its budget keeps running as a background job, named body-<seq>, and run_code returns at once with the id and the verb calls logged so far: the work stays visible and the agent is free.
- The job uses the job machinery's status file and lifetime, and job_status with waitMs (at most 30 s) returns its value or its error: one observation tool, no new one.
- next_context reports each state change once: started, still running every 3 minutes (logged as job-running), exited, killed, failed. A kill is named once, by job-killed, not again as an ended job.
- finish is refused while a background run runs, and names it; stopBackground true kills each one and logs each kill: no work runs unseen past the end of a run.
- A background run is killed at the job lifetime, 30 minutes by default, with its tail logged: a runaway body cannot outlive the job rule.
- A restart kills background runs and logs each as killed. A supervised restart logs it when it quiesces; a crash is found by the next start, which logs the same: no child outlives the worker.
- A body's output is its verb calls, read from the log by the body's seq: the log is the one record, so the tail needs no second file.
- A stop kills the shells the body started and no other run's: each shell is tagged with its body through AsyncLocalStorage when it starts (src/exec.mjs, bodyContext).
- A body's result is in its status file, as a shell job's output is in its file, and the job event in the log carries the state only. The result reaches the agent once, in the exited notice: a result in both would be sent twice.

Limits, stated, not closed:

- Each body runs in a worker thread of its own (src/body-worker.mjs), so a body with a tight loop and no verb call does not hold the session's thread. A stop is worker.terminate(), which also stops a loop that never awaits. Measured: a while(true) body is terminated at its lifetime and next_context answers during the loop in 1 ms (spin-1793597751297).
- A thread costs memory while its body runs: about 12 MB at its peak per running body (rss-1793597751296 gives 14.5 MB per extra body; the retention harness gives 213 MB with ten running against 58.6 MB at base). After the bodies stop, RSS does not return to base: 162 MB with ten bodies stopped, against 58.6 MB at base, so the witness "back within 5 MB of base" is not met.
- The retention is shown, and its cause is named, from these measurements (retention harness, scratch under /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/worker). The main isolate's JavaScript heap is flat: heapUsed 5.4 MB at base and 6.0 MB after the stop, so no session object holds the bodies. The leftover is V8 heap pages of the disposed worker isolates, which stay mapped: after the stop there are 336 anonymous mappings of 128 to 512 KB, 83 MB, against 30 mappings and 7 MB at base (smaps-check2). It is not glibc: capping the malloc arenas at 2 leaves 161.3 MB, and small mmap and trim thresholds leave 157.8 MB, against 161.8 MB at the default. It is not the body code: ten bare workers stopped leave 123 MB against 52 MB at base, the same retention. Resource limits (young generation 2 MB, old 32 MB) lower the peak by about 2 MB per thread, to 9.8 MB, and leave the retained set as it was (123 MB).
- The retention is bounded by peak concurrency, not by the number of bodies run: three waves of ten give peaks of 213, 223 and 225 MB and stopped levels of 162, 173 and 175 MB (rss-waves). Sequential bodies climb once, by about 30 MB for the first thread, and then stay at 88 to 93 MB over twenty bodies (rss-seq).
- Accepted by gm: the retention is bounded, and the bound is the peak. The peak is limited by the cap below, so the memory that stays is limited with it. No pool of threads is kept, since that costs the resident memory gm decided against.
- A cap of four bodies at once per session (src/runcode.mjs, BODY_SLOTS). A body beyond the cap waits in arrival order and its job is queued. The wait is logged as body-queued; a body that waited is logged as body-started when its thread starts and body-finished when it stops, so a body that ran at once adds no event. A body stopped while it waits leaves the queue and never starts. Witness, run cap-1793598706098: ten run_code bodies submitted at once, four running and six queued at the call; all ten replies came back in call order (body-6=0 through body-38=9); the logged running count never exceeded 4; the server's resident memory was 62.2 MB at base and 140.4 MB at its peak. Witness, run capstop-1793598704418: six bodies, the sixth waiting, finish with stopBackground: no body-started event for the waiting body, and six job-killed events with reason stopBackground.
- The lifetime and the notices of a queued body count from the start of its thread, not from its adoption, so a body is not killed for the time it waited.
- The six job witnesses on the capped code, same seqs as before: budget-1793598715407 (2103 ms, body-6), lifetime-1793598715413 (lifetime kill at seq 19, 9 calls before, 0 after), finish-1793598715410 (refused, then stopBackground kill at seq 12), restart-1793598715407 (seq 9 and 10), crash-1793598715410 (seq 12 named), shell-1793598715412 (SIGKILL at 920 ms).
- A new thread costs about 31 ms to start, which is most of the run_code latency: the median run_code call is 42.5 ms, against 0.54 ms for the in-process body it replaced (log-window/run-1793597767931 against log-window/run-1793596939169). A pool of threads started ahead would hide the start, at the cost of one idle thread resident. That is not built.
- A body's console output is drained by the session and not kept: it cannot reach the protocol's stdout, but it is not in the log.
- A body job has no process of its own, so a restart signals nothing for it; it is logged as killed, and its thread goes with the process.
- The three-minute notices and the start notice apply to run_code bodies, as gm named them. A shell job keeps its exit notice and gets no start or running notice.

Witnesses. Each is a real worker on a fresh workspace, driven by a scratch script outside the repo (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/background/witness.mjs). The run id is the scratch directory name; the run is its run/ directory.

- budget, run budget-1793597767857: a 10 s body with timeoutMs 2000 returned at 2070 ms with background body-6. The started notice is seq 7 (job running). job_status with waitMs 30000 returned the value "slept". The exited notice (seq 12) appeared once, at the next next_context, and not at the one after.
- lifetime, run lifetime-1793597767855 (--job-lifetime-ms 4000): a body calling list_dir every 500 ms was killed at the 4 s lifetime. The job went killed at seq 18, and job-killed at seq 19 with reason lifetime and a tail of 9 calls. The body made 9 verb calls before the kill and none after. next_context named it in jobsKilled, and job_status reports it killed.
- finish, run finish-1793597767859: finish with no flag was refused and named body-6. finish with stopBackground true killed it: job-killed at seq 12, reason stopBackground, then the end event with status finished.
- shell, run shell-1793597767873: the body's run_shell of sleep 30 ended with signal SIGKILL at 928 ms (code-call seq 14), when finish stopped the body, not at 30 s.
- restart, run restart-1793597767866 (SIGTERM, the supervisor's path): the quiesce logged the job killed at seq 9 and job-killed at seq 10, reason restart. The resumed worker's first next_context named body-6 in jobsKilled.
- crash, run crash-1793597767859 (SIGKILL, no quiesce): the next start found body-6 running in its status file and logged it killed at seq 11 and 12, reason restart. Its first next_context named it at seq 12.
- notice3, run notice3-1793597842462 (about 7 minutes, a 400 s body, on the thread mechanism): started at seq 7. job-running at seq 9 with runningMs 180004, and again at seq 12 with runningMs 360007. The exit at seq 15 was sent once, with the value "long"; the next next_context had no notice.

Regression, on the final code. The gate passes its seven checks (33 modules parse, 73 text files ASCII, 132 doc paths exist, skills name only served tools, smoke finished). The horizon-fields witness reports allOk. The log-window witness (run log-window/run-1793597767931, on the thread mechanism) ran 1000 identical run_code calls with 0 failures, and query_log paged through 3097 events, contiguous. Its median run_code call took 42.5 ms, against 0.54 ms for the in-process body (run log-window/run-1793596939169). Its resumed feed equals the replay of the file.

Memory over the 1000 calls, RSS growth, measured on this change: 21.1 MB and 22.5 MB on the working tree in the first pass (runs run-1793596922864 and run-1793596934551), 19.5 MB in the final pass (run-1793597497356), 17.1 MB on HEAD exported with git archive (run-1793596939169), and 19.9 MB on the working tree with the per-call async context removed in a scratch copy (run-1793596949041). Runs of one tree differ by 1 to 3 MB. The per-call context is within that spread. The gap to HEAD, about 2 to 5 MB, is not isolated, and it is within the range this log-window section already records as V8's committed heap.

## Updates, setup and the manual (2026-11-02)

Decisions, made under the owner's delegation:

- Updates run in the relay. The relay holds the host's stdio, does not change in normal work, and outlives supervisor swaps, so its timer keeps running through restarts. A check runs 30 seconds after start and then every GMNEW_UPDATE_MS (default 60000 since 2026-11-02, see Update on push; 0 turns the checks off).
- A check pulls only a checkout with no local changes to tracked files, and only as a fast-forward. A history that has diverged from origin is reported and left alone. When package-lock.json changed, the pull runs npm ci, and then the gate runs in its own process. A pull that the gate rejects is undone with git reset --hard to the commit before the pull. The checkout was clean before the pull, so no local work is lost.
- The worker restarts through the supervisor's existing watch on src/ and skills/, so the update adds no second restart path. The run resumes from its log.
- setup.sh is the one setup command. It is idempotent: it installs acorn only when it is missing, pulls when the checkout is clean, runs the gate, and prints the .mcp.json entry. It writes no config file.
- The manual is docs/index.html on main, not a gh-pages branch, because branches are not created. Enabling GitHub Pages (Settings, Pages, deploy from main, folder /docs) is an owner action, since the repository is private and publication is the owner's decision.

Named limits, added:

- tools/relay.mjs changed. A running relay does not reload it, so the hourly check goes live at the owner's reconnect. The timer was witnessed by starting the relay directly, below, not in a live session.
- The print layout was checked as a PDF from headless Chromium. It was not checked on paper.
- The update check needs network access to origin. Offline, it reports the fact and skips.

## Update safety (2026-11-02)

The owner asked for the update check to deploy main into running workers within the hour, and gm asked for two conditions before the hourly update is judged safe. This section supersedes the pull-then-undo rule in the section above: a commit is now checked before the checkout moves to it.

- A commit is checked in a clone of the checkout at that commit. The clone shares the checkout's objects and creates no branch in the checkout. The gate runs there, and the checkout is fast-forwarded only when the gate passes. A commit the gate rejects never reaches the checkout.
- A target that was refused by the gate, or rolled back, is remembered in the process. Origin still points at it, so it is not tried again until origin moves to another commit. Without this, a broken commit on main would be pulled, failed and rolled back at every check.
- The pull never runs into a checkout with changes to tracked files.
- The pull waits while a tool call is in flight, or while a body or job of the current run is running or queued. The relay supplies that test: the calls it has forwarded that have not been answered, and the run's job status files. The wait is checked before the candidate and again just before the pull, and it is retried every 60 seconds, so a body that ends does not wait for the next hour.
- A pull that changes src/ or skills/ restarts the worker through the supervisor's watch. The supervisor reports the worker's state to the relay (running, or failed with the reason). The relay does not forward that report to the host. The check waits for it. When the worker does not start on the new code, the checkout is reset to the commit before the pull, the worker starts on the old code, and the rollback is logged. The wait for the rollback takes only a report that the worker is running, because the supervisor keeps retrying the broken code until the reset, and a failure report from those retries is not the outcome of the rollback.
- When the worker does not start on the old code either, the state is stuck, and the message asks for a person to check the checkout.
- The hourly check was kept until the owner decided. The owner then asked for a push to reach running workers at once, and the interval is one minute now (see Update on push). GMNEW_UPDATE_MS=3600000 restores the hour.

Named limits, added:

- A check takes the time of a clone and a gate, and an npm ci when the lock file changed.
- The refused list is kept in the process. A relay restart retries a refused commit once.
- Between a failed restart and the rollback, the supervisor keeps retrying the broken code, so calls in that window get the supervisor's error reply.
- The tools/relay.mjs and tools/supervise.mjs changes go live only at the owner's reconnect. The witnesses below start the relay directly.

## Rule re-injection (2026-11-02, joint decision: gm and the agent)

The gap: the rules reached the model only through next_context results, which are part of the conversation. If the host summarises the conversation partway through a step, the rules go with the summary until the next next_context call.

The fix: the rules are also in the server's instructions, the text the host puts in the system prompt on every request. Superseded on 2026-11-02: the instructions now hold a pointer to the skill only (see "Reconnect: the connect-time text"). The system prompt is not summarised. The rules are appended to the instructions for both surfaces, after the surface's own text. They stay in rules.md and in next_context, as before. The whole instructions text is 960 bytes, of which the rules are 647, and it sits in each request's system prompt, which is cached. Reason: the rules cost about 960 bytes in the system prompt, which is cheap, and they stay with the run.

Witness, delivery (run rules/run-1793604554, JSON-RPC against the repository's server): the instructions contain the rules verbatim, the rules are numbered 1 to 7, and next_context returns the same text as the run's rules.md. The instructions are 960 bytes.

Not witnessed, and the row stays blocked: whether a rule holds after a summary in a live run. That needs live trials, so it waits for the owner's live-trial budget. The trigger is unchanged.

## Rules watchdog and compaction handling (2026-11-02, joint decision: gm and the agent, reviewed by the orchestrator)

A joint decision of gm and the agent, reviewed by the orchestrator on 2026-11-02. It is not recorded as the owner's request.

- The server watches for the rule breaks it can see: rule 2, a call before the run's first next_context; rule 1, a path or a load that leaves the workspace (top-level refusals, and refusals of verbs called inside a body); rule 3, a finish with no call in its step; rule 4, a finish refused for its witness.
- Each break raises a notice, logged as rules-notice. The notice is carried in the reply of each call (rulesNotice) and in next_context (rulesPending). It asks the agent for one read of the skill with ReadMcpResourceTool (server gm-new, uri gm://skill/SKILL.md), which the agent can do itself. A slash command is not used, because the user types those. A fetch of the skill, through the resource or the prompt, clears the notices. Each fetch is logged as skill-loaded with the notices it cleared, and the clear is logged as rules-cleared.
- The steps the agent takes after a context compaction are in the skill, because a skill survives a compaction and the server's notices only point to it: call next_context at once, follow the rules it returns, do not rely on a summary for a rule, the witness or an event, use gm.query_log for any event the summary does not give exactly, and load the skill again when a notice appears.

Witness (run rules-watch/run-1793605220927, JSON-RPC against the working-tree server): a run whose first call is run_code raised rule 2, next_context carried it, the skill fetch cleared it, and both events were logged. A body that read ../outside.txt raised rule 1 on its reply, with the refusal text. A blank witness raised rule 4. A finish with no call in its step raised rule 3. A clean run, with one read and a witnessed finish, raised no notice.

Condition 1, a notice costs no more than one skill fetch, and does not repeat once cleared. Witness (run rules-watch/one-fetch-1793605325406, JSON-RPC): the first call is run_code, which raises rule 2. One resources/read of gm://skill/SKILL.md clears it. The next next_context has no rulesPending, and the two replies after it have no rulesNotice. The log holds one rules-notice, one skill-loaded with via resource, and one rules-cleared. The five watchdog scenarios were rerun after the change (run rules-watch/run-1793605325678): rules 1, 3 and 4 still raise, rule 2 still raises and clears, and the clean run raises no notice.

Condition 2, the extra turns, is measured in the next live acceptance run, not in a separate run. The method, for the harness to apply to each run's log:
- notices fired: the count of rules-notice events.
- notice-driven fetches, which are the turns a notice adds at the server: the count of skill-loaded events whose cleared list is not empty. A fetch with an empty cleared list is an ordinary load and is not counted.
- host turns: the count of action events, which is one per tool call, so the notice's turns can be read against the clean runs.
The turns the agent spends responding to a notice are not visible to the server, so the live transcript has to be read for them. The rule for the change: if a notice adds turns on a clean run, that is reported before any further change. A clean run is a run with no notice.

Limits: the watchdog sees only rules 1 to 4. Rules 5 to 7 are not observed. It cannot force the agent to comply. Whether the agent reads the skill after a notice, and whether a rule holds after a compaction, is measured only by a live run, which is the open witness of the rule re-injection row.

## Publication (2026-11-02)

The owner decided to make the repository public, so that GitHub Pages can serve the manual. The Pages plan does not allow Pages on a private repository, which GitHub reported when Pages was first requested (HTTP 422).

- Before the change, a scan of the tracked files and of every commit in the history (159 commits) found no credentials or private keys.
- Repository: public. Pages: built from main, folder /docs, at https://anentrypoint.github.io/gm-new/. The first build finished in about a minute, and the page served the manual with HTTP 200.
- The "private" remote rule in AGENTS.md is not changed by this record. It still says private, and the owner decides whether to edit that file.

## Windows under Wine (2026-11-02)

The owner asked for the Windows tests to be run under Wine. Wine is not a Windows host, so this checks the tree kill on Wine 10.0, not on Windows, and the named limit stays open.

Set-up: the Wine 10.0 that the owner's office folder ships, run through its wrapper script, which clears the sandbox's LD_PRELOAD interposers (the raw loader aborted with "free(): invalid pointer"). The Windows build of Node was v24.22.0 for win-x64, and its SHA-256 matched the official SHASUMS file.

Test (run on the Wine prefix's C: drive, the repo's exec.mjs copied in, record in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wine/record/results.json): runCommand starts a shell command that starts a grandchild Node process and then sleeps, with a 4 s timeout, through the same killGroup path the repo uses on Windows (taskkill /T /F).

- Positive control: the test process is alive by process.kill(pid, 0).
- Before the timeout, at 2 s: the grandchild is alive.
- After the timeout: runCommand reports timedOut, and both the parent and the grandchild are gone.
- Clean exit: a plain command returns exit code 0 through runCommand.

Wine limits found along the way, recorded so that nobody repeats them: a child started with an explicit working directory fails under this Wine (any value of cwd, including C:), so the test runs in the default directory and uses absolute paths; stdout through a pipe is unusable (EBADF), so results are written to a file; tasklist writes UTF-16, so its text output does not match a pid, which is why the liveness check is process.kill(pid, 0).

What this does not show: that taskkill behaves the same on Windows. Wine implements taskkill itself, so a Windows host is still needed to close the limit. The row unknown-windows-kill stays blocked, and its body records the Wine result.

Rule re-injection and the live witness are a different limit, and Wine does not touch them: they need live model trials.

## Heap growth: what retains it, and the index file (2026-11-02, gm request)

Method: a worker runs with the heap-snapshot preload (`audit/snap-worker.cjs`), which writes a snapshot on SIGUSR1. The run (`audit/snap-run.mjs`) takes a snapshot after 200 warm-up calls, one after 1000 more `read_file` calls, and one after 1000 more. `audit/snap-diff.mjs` diffs two snapshots by constructor, and by retainer for the objects that exist only in the later one. Snapshot run `snap/read1` is HEAD (96e2abe). Snapshot run `snap/read2` is the index commit (001274b).

Snapshot totals (self size, bytes):

| Interval | HEAD (read1) | Index commit (read2) |
|---|---|---|
| Warm-up end to 1000 calls | +299,680, mostly JIT code: 89 new code objects, 92 KB of them | not taken (the warm-up is the same) |
| 1000 calls to 2000 calls | +23,912 | +10,904 |

Top retainers of the objects that exist only in the later snapshot (second interval, HEAD):
- `Object --property:text-->`: 256 objects, 108,544 bytes. These are the reply memory (`REPLY_MEMORY` in `src/mcp.mjs`), which keeps the last 256 requests and evicts the oldest. It is bounded, so it does not grow.
- `Object --internal:properties-->`: 512 objects, 32,768 bytes. Those are the same kind of bounded window.
- `(object elements)`: +11,720 bytes of growth in the array of the event offsets. That array held one number per event, so it grew with the length of the run. It is the byte-offset index gm suspected.

The first interval's growth is JIT compilation, not retention: after a 200-call warm-up, the heap rises by 0.35 MB, but after a 2000-call warm-up it does not rise over the next 1000 calls.

Change, commit `001274b`: the log keeps one 16-byte record for each event in `log.index` beside the log: a float64 byte offset, a uint32 length, and four zero bytes. The index is written after the lines it describes, in the same flush points as the log. A missing index, or one that does not end where the log ends, is rebuilt from the log, which is the record. The worker keeps the last 512 events in memory, and reads any other event by seeking to its record. `read_file` reads through one buffer, which starts at a page and grows to the largest file read so far (the peak; it never shrinks), and a file bigger than 1 MB is read asynchronously. The bytes are decoded once, and the text is both the reply and the logged output.

Witnesses (scratch, run labels as listed):
- The index covers the log: 2005 events, `log.index` 32,080 bytes, which is 16 bytes times the events (`eq-new`).
- The event log matches HEAD's, event for event, for 2001 events of 1000 calls. The first difference is the run summary's timing fields (`eq-head`, `eq-new`).
- `query_log` still reaches every event: paged bounded, the 3137-event copy gives 63 pages, identical to HEAD (`idx-bounded`, compared with `base-head-bounded`). Paged unbounded, it gives contiguous events 1 to 3279, the first is seq 1 and the last is seq 3279 (`idx-open`). The pending witness also reads 85 events against 78 logged (`pending-witness-1793611099864`, and later runs).
- Heap after a 2000-call warm-up, over the next 1000 calls: `read_file` HEAD 6.92 to 6.87 MB, now 6.88 to 6.85 MB (`gc-top-warm-head`, `gc-top-warm-new`). `next_context` HEAD 7.51 to 7.50 MB, now 7.47 to 7.45 MB (`gc-next-warm-head`, `gc-next-warm-new`). Both are flat.
- Collections per 1000 calls. After a 200-call warm-up, `read_file` goes from 18 at HEAD (`gc-top-before2`) to 16 (`gc-top-after2`). After a 2000-call warm-up, both are 8 (`gc-top-warm-head`, `gc-top-warm-new`). `next_context` after a 2000-call warm-up is 5 at HEAD and 7 now (`gc-next-warm-head`, `gc-next-warm-new`). That is within noise, so the steady state shows no drop in collections. The drop in the warm-up numbers is warm-up only.

The steady-state growth is now 11 KB per 1000 calls (the second-interval total above), down from 24 KB. The index no longer sits in memory, so the memory of the log is the window, whatever the length of the run.

## First open after the index change (2026-11-02, gm condition)

Each existing run gets its `log.index` on its first open, by a scan of its log. Measured on copies of the logs, with the source runs only read (`audit/index-scan.mjs`, `audit/index-scan-all.mjs`, commit `001274b`):

| Run | Log bytes | Events | First open (rebuild), median | Later open (index consistent), median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Largest run in the repository's `runs/` (`repo-largest`) | 287,495 | 577 | 3.3 ms (2.2 to 6.9) | 1.7 ms |
| Largest run overall, a scratch paging run with 20 KB results (`overall-largest`) | 39,115,190 | 5,141 | 88.9 ms (80.1 to 91.0) | 40.4 ms |
| All 540 runs in `runs/`, once each (`index-scan-all`) | 2,907,704 | 9,900 | 102 ms in total, slowest 2.3 ms | not measured |

The cost is a one-time scan per run, about 100 ms for the whole repository. The later open still reads the window's last 512 events, and that is what dominates the 40 ms on the large run, since its events hold 20 KB results each.

## Standing rule: buffer reuse and garbage collection (2026-11-02, owner's rule relayed by gm)

Method, the same for each path: the worker, or the log alone, is run with `--expose-gc` and a 'gc' PerformanceObserver. A forced collection at the start and at the end gives the heap used, and the RSS is read at both points. The workload is 1000 calls, after a warm-up of 200 calls. Scratch scripts are under `audit/`: `gcbench.mjs` (the worker), `gc-trace.cjs` (the preload), `bench-log.mjs` (the log alone).

| Path | Before (HEAD) | After | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Log append, alone, 40000 events (`bench-log` `log-head`) | 8 collections, 0.20 per 1000 events | 6 collections, 0.15 per 1000 events (`log-new1`) | No measurable gain. The writer change (one reused buffer, `writeSync`) was reverted, so it is not committed. |
| Top-level `read_file`, 1000 calls (`gc-top-before`) | 18 collections, 9.66 ms pause, heap 6.51 to 6.87 MB, RSS 68.2 to 74.8 MB | Not changed, since the writer change was reverted | Its path is the reply and the request, not the log. The log is near zero, as above. |
| `next_context`, 1000 steps (`gc-next-before2`, `gc-next-before3`) | 15 collections, heap 6.98 to 7.49 MB | 12 collections (`gc-next-prefix1`, `gc-next-prefix2`), heap 6.93 to 7.43 MB | The prefix was rendered and hashed at every step. It is now rebuilt only when the rules change (commit `96e2abe`). The prefix hash is the same as HEAD's (`78b1eb67...878dc5`). |
| Body with 1000 verb calls (`gc-body-before`) | 4 collections, 6.33 ms pause, heap 7.36 to 7.32 MB | Not changed | Already near zero, and flat after warm-up. |
| Feed reads of older events | 0 log file opens during 1000 `next_context` steps (`count-opens`, `opens-next-head`) | Not changed | The feed reads from the in-memory window, so the disk read path is not hot. |
| Object shapes | Not measured separately | Not changed | The log path is already near zero, so a shape change could show no gain there. Dropped. |

Target not met: the heap grows after warm-up, by 0.36 MB on the top-level path and 0.50 MB on `next_context`, over 1000 calls. That growth is the log's own growth (its offsets and the window). It was not traced further.

## Ship script (2026-11-02, owner rule relayed by gm)

`tools/ship.sh` is in the repository, so every commit goes through it. The first commit added the script unchanged (`7c9fe23`). The fix (`87b43ca`) stages only the paths it is given, stages a deleted path before the gate runs (the gate lists tracked files), unstages those paths when the gate fails, and refuses a path that is neither in the tree nor tracked. `SHIP_ROOT` and `SHIP_PUSH=0` let the witness run in a scratch clone without a push.

Witness, in a scratch clone with pushing off (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/ship-witness`):
- A commit given `README.md` contains that file only. A file staged beforehand, `src/log.mjs`, stays staged. Gate exit 0.
- A commit given a tracked file that was deleted contains the deletion only, and the staged file stays staged. Gate exit 0.
- A gate that fails (a non-ASCII byte in a named file) commits nothing. The named path is unstaged, and the staged file stays staged. HEAD does not move. Exit 1.
- A path that is neither in the tree nor tracked is refused with exit 1 and no change.

## Notifications for pending events (2026-11-02, owner's request)

The owner asked for the best route each host offers, in order: MCP channel events, a Claude Code Monitor, and a background process for any other host. Each pending record is sent to the host as a `notifications/claude/channel` line when it is queued (`src/pending.mjs` listener, `src/mcp.mjs`). The worker declares `experimental: {'claude/channel': {}}` in its initialize result, and its instructions name the three routes in order. The lines pass through the core and the relay unchanged, so this needs no relay change.

What is verified, and what is not:
- Monitor, live, in Claude Code 2.1.301 (this session): a Monitor on a watched file reported a line appended to it as a notification, with its JSON intact (task `b5uzgwec7`). This route needs no server support.
- Channel events, scripted (`audit/channel-check.mjs`, commit `29dccb4`): the capability is declared, and every pending record has exactly one channel notification whose `meta.id` and `meta.kind` match it and whose content is the record. Metadata keys are identifiers, as the host requires.
- Channel delivery into a session, not verified. The host registers a custom channel only when it is launched with `--dangerously-load-development-channels server:gm-new` in an interactive session, and the operator accepts the dialog. The reference says the host drops events from a server it did not register, without an error, and the server cannot tell whether it is registered. So the channel route needs the owner's relaunch with that flag to be witnessed.
- The instructions and the channel capability reach a session only at connect. The live worker gets the new code on the core's next update check, but the host keeps the instructions it read at connect, so one more `/mcp` reconnect is needed for the new instructions to apply.

Routes, by preference:
1. A channel event `<channel source="gm-new" id kind>` in the session.
2. A Monitor armed on `pendingFile` with `tail -n +1 -F`, re-armed when it expires, if the host has the tool.
3. Superseded 2026-11-02 (see "Hot reload across the board, and pending events without channels"): a background task of the host's shell that runs `pendingCommands.wait` from `next_context`, `node <checkout>/src/cli.mjs events --dir <run dir> --wait`. It ends at the next event, so the host's completion report wakes the agent. The job that only polled at each step is gone.
4. `pendingCommands.read` from `next_context`, which is `events --dir <run dir> --read-clear`, for a host with no background task. The older `--run <run>` form still works for runs under this checkout's `runs/`.

Trade-off: if the channel route is live and a Monitor is also armed, each event is reported twice. The instructions say to act on an id once, but the server cannot tell the two routes apart, so the duplicate costs one notification per event. Stopping the Monitor when channel events arrive would remove it, and that is left to the agent.

## Owner decision: compression removed, pending queue added (2026-11-02)

The owner decided, relayed by gm: remove compression. The audit log `runs/<id>/log.jsonl` stays raw and complete, and nothing is compressed or deleted. A query of an old run must not depend on a reader for a compressed form. In its place, a separate pending queue carries only the events an agent must act on, for live feedback and for read-and-clear.

- Removed: the compression sweep, the size cap, the gzip read path and the resume guard for compressed runs (the retention module, retain.mjs, deleted in commit `f4968bb`). Nothing in the repository's `runs/` had been compressed, so nothing needed restoring. The efficiency table's retention row (item 6) describes the compression that was removed, and it is superseded by this decision.
- Added: `src/pending.mjs` and `runs/<id>/pending.jsonl` (commit `f03e01f`). A record is appended only after the log holds its event, so the queue never names an event the log lacks, and it carries the event's seq as its `id`. The kinds are: a job's start and end, a killed job, a stuck or unstuck job, a rules notice and a recovery.
- Live feedback: `next_context` returns `pendingFile`, and the agent follows that file with a shell job (`tail -n +1 -F`), reading the job's output with `job_status`. The skill's section "Pending events" says so.
- Read-and-clear: `next_context` returns the pending events once, and removes them after its reply is written. The CLI `events --run <id> --read-clear` does the same. The queue is renamed, the renamed copy is read, and the copy is removed. A crash between the read and the removal delivers those records again, and never fewer. A consumer skips an id it has seen.

Witness, scratch check `audit/pending-check.mjs`, run `pending-witness-1793611099864`, a real run under `runs/`, removed afterwards:
- A job exit (id 11) is in `pending.jsonl` (exit in file: true), and in the tail job's output (exit in tail: true).
- The first `next_context` returns ids 3, 5, 8 and 11, and the second returns none. The file is gone after the read (0 records left).
- The CLI read-and-clear prints the two events of a second job, then prints nothing.
- A read that crashed before its removal (the copy left behind) delivers its two records again, and the copy is removed (crash re-delivered: true).
- The raw log holds 78 events, and `query_log` paged through the whole log returns 85 (its own actions included), so no event is missing. No `.gz` file exists.

Limits, stated for the owner: a tail job that stays idle for three minutes is reported as stuck, and that report is a pending event, so the agent sees a stuck notice for a healthy tail. Each pending event costs one log flush at the time it is written. Only the kinds above are pending, so a long job's reminders are not.

## Side-effect flush (2026-11-02, gm condition on the batched log)

A side-effecting action's start is flushed before it runs, so a crash after its effect still leaves its start in the log, and recovery lists it as open. The tools that only read are listed in `READ_ONLY_TOOLS` (`src/mcp.mjs`). Every other tool is flushed, and so is a side-effecting verb called inside a body, which now has its own start event naming the body. A code-call closes its start, as a result closes a top-level action. Commit `304ff1d`.

Witness, `audit/recover-check.mjs`: a `write_file` to a named pipe, which never returns, and a `write_file` in a `run_code` body to a second pipe, each followed by SIGKILL before any reply. The start must be on disk at the kill, and a restarted worker on the same run must list it as open.
- Before, HEAD (`head-now`): no start on disk in either case, and the recovery lists nothing for the write. Run ids `recover/run-top`, `recover/run-body` under the scratch directory.
- After, `304ff1d`: the top-level write is open as seq 4 (`write_file`), and the body write is open as seq 6, with `body` 5 (its `run_code`). Both pass.

## Idle trace (2026-11-02, gm request)

The idle wake-ups are V8's memory reducer, a background garbage collector that runs main-thread tasks while the process is idle. Evidence, in scratch under `audit/`:
- A bare Node process that only waits makes no wake-up in 45 seconds (`bare-wait.sh`: 0 switches for a timer, a stdin pipe, and an interval).
- A standalone worker makes 5 wake-ups in 45 seconds by default, and 0 with `--no-memory-reducer` (`worker-idle.sh plain`, `worker-idle.sh no-memory-reducer`).
- A standalone core, with updates off, makes 5 by default and 0 with the flag (`core-idle.sh plain-core-noupd`, `flag-core-noupd`). With updates on, the first update check at 30 seconds adds wake-ups, so the core's numbers were 18 and 13 for the same window.
- The whole stack, traced per thread (`idle-threads.mjs`, runs `trace-now` and `trace-flag`): before, 8 switches a minute on the main thread for each of the relay, the core and the worker. After the worker flag (commit `0d9dd42`), the worker shows 0. The relay shows 0 in this run and 8 in the first, which varies run to run. The core shows 8, because the relay starts it without the flag.
- Rejected: `--no-memory-reducer`. The runtime does not accept the flag through `NODE_OPTIONS`, and `v8.setFlagsFromString` in a preload has no effect (5 switches with it), so the only way to set it is on each process's command line. The flag was added to the worker's start in commit `0d9dd42` and removed again. Owner decision, relayed by gm (2026-11-02): the memory reducer is what returns memory when a worker idles, and memory is the constraint for many agents per machine. Eight context switches a minute are negligible. The hourly update check stays.
- The memory measurement above does not contradict that reason. A 30-second idle is too short to show the reducer returning memory, so the measurement shows no saving only within that window.
- Memory cost, `mem-burst.mjs`: after 3000 calls and 30 seconds idle, 87.1 MB by default and 84.7 MB with the flag. The default did not shrink in the idle period, so the reducer gave no saving here.
- Timers in the stack, from the preload trace: the relay's only timer is its shutdown timeout. The core has its shutdown and startup timers and the update check, which is an hourly interval with a first check at 30 seconds (updates off in these traces). The worker has none while idle, since its stuck check now runs only while a job is open.

## Efficiency audit (2026-11-02, gm request)

Each item was measured before and after with the same harness, in scratch under `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/audit`, and the results are the JSON files named in the table. Each item that showed a gain is committed through `audit/ship.sh`, which runs the gate, takes its exit code, and commits and pushes only when that code is zero. Items that did not show a gain are dropped and say why.

| # | Item | Before | After | Commit | Run ids (scratch labels) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Relay as a pipe | relay.mjs 15671 bytes, 420 lines | 4075 bytes, 85 lines; relay hash `28b8226b...3421` | da34588 | witness `2026-11-02-08-38-48-a16efb` |
| 2 | Lazy-load gate, acorn, wiki, review, wiki lint | spawn to first next_context 76 ms, RSS 61.1 MB | 63 ms, 59.2 MB | eb99897 | `startup-base-head`, `startup-lazy-1`; checks `lazy-check` pass |
| 3 | Batch log writes | 1002 appends per 1000 events (top), 1004 (body); 411 and 347 ms per 1000 calls | 501 (top), 7 (body); 399 and 318 ms | db20205 | `calls-base-top`, `calls-batch-top`, `calls-base-body`, `calls-batch-body`; same events, only the summary's timings differ |
| 4 | Search listing kept by the index's stat | median 10.4 ms per search, 79 lstat per call | 5.5 ms, 79 lstat per call | 702009d | `search-base-search`, `search-cached-search`; freshness check `search-fresh` passes |
| 5 | Stuck check only while a job or body is open | idle: 10 ms CPU and 28 voluntary switches per minute | 0 ms and 16 switches | d96bb5a | `idle-base-idle`, `idle-idle-after`; job check passes |
| 6 | Retention for runs/ | 2757871 bytes over 502 runs; 299515 bytes per 1000 completed-run events | 857860 bytes after the age sweep; 70294 bytes per 1000 events gzipped | 35b869a | `retain-check` (copy of runs, 452 completed runs compressed, event counts equal) |
| 7 | Memoize the prefix | hash 0.018 ms per step; next_context median 0.327 ms | dropped | none | `ctx-base-ctx` |

Notes that go with the table:
- Item 2: the saving is 2 MB and 13 ms. The worker's largest remaining loads are the run-code runtime, and the modules that the server needs for its tool list at startup.
- Item 3: the log is still the record. A reply is written only after the events so far are in the file. A crash between replies can lose the buffered events, which were not yet replied to. The events of a long body go to the file at its start, at each 256 events, and at each reply.
- Item 4: the per-file check stays, so an edit is seen at once. The stats per call did not fall, so the stat part of the target is not met. The code analysis is not changed: it lists untracked files too, which an index key cannot see, so keying it on the index would hide new files.
- Item 5: after the change the relay shows no voluntary switches, and the worker and the core still show about 8 a minute each. Their cause is not traced. The hourly update check is kept. Both are small, and this item does not claim zero wake-ups.
- Item 6: nothing is deleted. The size cap compresses the oldest completed runs until the total is under the cap, and it reports `overCap` when it cannot. A cap that would delete is the owner's decision.
- Item 7: the hash costs 0.018 ms of a 0.327 ms median next_context, and a model step takes seconds. The path is not hot, so the item is dropped.

## Relay pipe and core (2026-11-02, gm request, one reconnect)

This supersedes the relay and supervisor bullets above. The relay has no swap, list, or update logic, so a change to the core needs no reconnect. The relay itself needs one reconnect, and then it does not change.

- `tools/relay.mjs` is the only file the host runs (`.mcp.json` names it). It is a pipe. It creates a named pipe in the temp directory and keeps it open for reading and writing. Host lines go into the pipe, and the core's stdout comes back to the host. It starts `tools/core.mjs` with the arguments it was given. When the core's output ends, it starts a new core after one second. A core that hands over keeps the output open, so the relay starts no second core. It parses nothing beyond the end of a line, and an error is logged while the connection stays open. Size: 4075 bytes and 85 lines. Before, at `72594ed`: 15671 bytes and 420 lines. Relay hash at `da34588`: `28b8226b395566de1d182e13ec1db098e1efb8891bb74328b22247688c041175`.
