- Why a named pipe and not the child's stdin: Node destroys a child's stdin when the child exits, which closes the write end. A core that took over through the inherited stdin then read end of input and shut itself down. Seen in scratch run 2026-11-02-08-19-07-62cddc, and reproduced by `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/pipe-probe/probe.mjs`: the grandchild that inherited the pipe reads end of input after the child exits. The relay's own pipe is not a child's, so it stays open.
- `tools/core.mjs` holds the worker, the restart on a change under `src/` or `skills/`, the list digests and the list notices, the update check, and the rollback path. A pull that changes `tools/` other than the relay replaces the core. The new code is syntax-checked and loaded first (`--probe`). Then the old core pauses its worker, and the new core takes over the pipe (inherited stdin, stdout, and an ack on fd 3). The new core starts its worker and answers `ready`, and the old core exits with code 75. If the new core does not answer, the old core restarts its worker and goes on.
- `tools/update.mjs` takes `replaceCore`. `apply` calls it when `tools/` changed, and a failed handover is rolled back as a failed worker start is.
- `tools/supervise.mjs` is no longer started by anything. It stays until the owner's reconnect, because the relay before this change still runs it. Remove it in the next commit after the reconnect.

Witness, scripted host (no model), run in a scratch clone with a bare origin (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/relay-witness/drive.mjs`, log `drive.log`, summary `drive-summary-final.json`, run `2026-11-02-08-38-48-a16efb`). The commits below are in the scratch origin, not in the public one:
- (a) commit `b58f0bf` edits only `src/run.mjs`: the worker is replaced (worker pid changed), the core and the relay keep their pids, and the host's tools list answers with 3 tools. No reconnect.
- (b) commit `d05b32b` edits only `tools/core.mjs`: the core is replaced (pid 241895 to 243365), the relay keeps its pid, no second core starts, and tools list answers with 3 tools.
- (c) commit `8951d84` edits only `skills/`: the worker restarts and tools list answers with 3 tools. The relay's hash is the same at `da34588`, `b58f0bf`, `d05b32b`, and `8951d84`: `28b8226b395566de1d182e13ec1db098e1efb8891bb74328b22247688c041175`.
- (d) the core is killed with SIGKILL (pid 243365). The relay logs `core output ended`, starts a new core (pid 244161) on the same run directory after one second, and answers the request sent at the kill (`requestSentAtKill` 3, the reply has 3 tools). The relay stays up, and when the host closes its input the relay exits with code 0.

Not witnessed yet: the same with the owner's live host. The live relay (pid 99097) still runs the previous code, so witnesses (a) to (c) on the public origin come after the owner's reconnect. Limit: a line that the core has read but not processed when the core dies gets no reply. Lines still in the pipe are answered by the next core.

## Single-worker limit: query_log paging (2026-11-02, owner request, reviewed by the orchestrator)

The owner asked for the single-worker limit to be fixed as far as possible, for subagents and single workers alike. The measured limit was query_log. Each call parsed the whole log and filtered it before it cut the page, so paging a log of N events cost about N squared over the page size. A reply could also go out over its cap: an event larger than the cap was not cut, so runQuery cut the output mid-string and the reply was not valid JSON. The real log had no such event (its largest is 603 characters), but a log that holds earlier query results does, since each result is up to 20000 characters, and paging that log hit both problems.

What changed (src/log.mjs, src/tools.mjs, src/mcp.mjs):
- `take(after, limit, match)` reads the file from the byte offset of the first event after `after`, and stops at `limit` matches. query_log asks for 51 events, so pageOf makes the same page and the same `next` as before: one event past the page says whether more remain.
- pageOf never returns a reply over 20000 characters. An event that does not fit a reply of its own is cut to its head until it fits, so the reply is valid JSON.
- A query_log reply is logged with its first 1000 characters and `outputChars`, its full length, at the top level, inside run_code, and in the scripted run (src/loop.mjs). The events a query reads are logged in full. gm accepted this on that condition (2026-11-02). Reason: a reply is a copy of events already in the log. Logged in full, each query added up to 20 KB, and a reader that pages to the end reads its own copies: each page returns one copy and each query adds two events, so `next` never clears. The reply the agent receives is unchanged. This changes what the log stores for query replies, so it is the part gm should review.
- query_log rejects an `after` that is not a number. Before, any value was compared as it was.

Measured on a copy of a real run: 3137 events, with the copy's end event removed, so the server resumes the run and does not open a new one beside it. The scripts are scratch files outside the repo, in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/qlog: page3.mjs pages one case per run and times each call as a stdio round trip, and samples RSS; compare.mjs checks two runs page by page, leaving out timestamps and durations; page4.mjs is page3.mjs with SRC for another source log. Each case's log is runs/<label>/run under that directory.

| Case (label) | Code | Query | Pages | Invalid pages | Events | Elapsed ms | Mean call ms | Max call ms | Peak RSS MB |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| base-head-bounded | eeb8fa2 | toMono 45183.966, the copied events | 63 | 0 | 3137 | 531 | 8.3 | 17.8 | 92 |
| new-take-bounded2 | this change | same | 63 | 0 | 3137 | 146 | 2.2 | 3.4 | 85 |
| base-head-open | eeb8fa2 | none | 1000, cap reached | 375 | 4514 parsed | 37667 | 37.5 | 98.3 | 293 |
| new-take-open2 | this change | none | 70, ended | 0 | 3279, seqs 1 to 3279 contiguous | 161 | 2.2 | 3.3 | 84 |
| base-kind-result-open | eeb8fa2 | kind result | 1000, cap reached | 956 | 1047 parsed | 79276 | 79.1 | 176.8 | 328 |
| new-kind-result-open | this change | kind result | 23, ended | 0 | 1046, equal to the result seqs of the final log up to seq 3184 | 72 | 3.0 | 4.5 | 83 |
| new-over-head-log-full | this change, on runs/base-head-open/run | none | 1428, ended | 0 | 7997, seqs 1 to 7997 contiguous, 561 clipped to their head | 3536 | 2.4 | 6.3 | 268 |

Results:
- Page for page, the bounded runs are identical (compare.mjs base-head-bounded new-take-bounded2: 63 pages, identical). On a log with no oversized event, query_log returns the same pages as before.
- Per call, the changed code is about 3.8 times faster on the same 3137 events (mean 2.2 against 8.3 ms). A call reads from its cursor and stops after one page, so its cost follows the page, not the log, unless the filter matches few events and the call reads to the end (mean 2.2 to 3.0 ms over 3137 to 7997 events). HEAD's call grows with the log, since each call parses all of it.
- HEAD does not finish an unbounded page loop. Its own replies were logged in full and read back, and 375 of its pages (956 for kind result) were not valid JSON. The changed code ends every unbounded case here.
- Clipping ran: 561 events too large for a reply of their own are cut to their head (438 of them are over 20000 characters in the copied HEAD log), and every reply in that run is valid JSON.

Not measured: a host session's round trip, and a log of many thousands of events in a live run. Those need the owner's long-horizon run.

Its logged read_file result is 20000 characters and a prefix of the file, so the tool result is in full. Its logged query_log reply (kind result, which holds that read) is 1000 characters with outputChars 13923. The MCP path logged 70 query replies in scratch run mcp-cap-check, each at most 1000 characters with outputChars. Replay of the run, 2026-11-02-08-01-28-3d9a08, finished with the same witness. A copy of the run whose logged query_log reply was replaced by CORRUPTED REPLY (the copy was deleted after the replay) replayed as 2026-11-02-08-01-28-a29c5a, with the same witness, and that replay's own query reply was the full reply, not the corrupted text. cli replay reads only the model's reply events, so no logged query reply is an input to a replay. Run 2026-11-02-08-00-51-a10ca3, from before the scripted path had the cap, logged its 13923-character query reply in full; that gap is closed.

## Subagent handoff (2026-11-02, owner request, reviewed by the orchestrator)

The owner asked for subagent isolation to be fixed as far as possible, once the single worker's limit showed (the section above). The mechanics are built. Whether isolation helps a long-horizon outcome is not measured, and it stays open.

- A child session is a new run with its own run directory, log, feed and context. Isolation is by construction: the child's `next_context` shows only its task and its own events, and its log is separate from the parent's.
- The handle is the child's run id. `next_context` now returns it as `run` (src/mcp.mjs, the reply of `context`). Before, the id was only on stderr, so a child could not name its log to the parent.
- The parent reads the child's result through the CLI, `node src/cli.mjs query --run <id> --js "..."`, and keeps only the witness. The skill section "Handing work to a child session" (skills/gm/SKILL.md) tells a session to do this.
- The workspace is shared. Children and parent can write the same file, and nothing enforces a separate workspace. Collisions are repaired after the fact (see "Collisions: repair after the fact", at the end of this file), and the skill states that policy at every hand-off.

Witness, scripted (no model: the calls are sent over stdio by /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/handoff/handoff.mjs, with the temporary workspace deleted after it). The child run is 2026-11-02-07-58-10-95bf7c, under runs/. Its `next_context` reply has `run` equal to the id on its stderr (`sameRun` true). Seq 3 and 4 are `next_context`; seq 5 and 6 are `read_file` of notes.txt, whose output starts with "alpha"; seq 7 and 8 are `finish` with the witness "child read notes.txt with read_file, first line: alpha"; seq 11 is `end`, status finished, 1 step. The parent's CLI query returned the finish output and the end witness, the same text, exit 0 for both.

Not measured, and not run here: whether isolation helps (a child against one worker on the same long task), and a Claude Code subagent that loads the skill. Both need live model runs, which gm-new does not make (AGENTS.md). They are reported to gm, to run. Subagent isolation stays a named limit until a live run measures it (gm, 2026-11-02).

The tools hashes are the first 16 hex digits of the sha in the run's tools event.

## Measured

- Update safety, witnessed (run update3/run-1793603564, real git repositories, final code): benign pull updated; a commit that breaks syntax was rejected, and the checkout was untouched; the same commit on the next check was refused; a dirty checkout gave dirty with HEAD unchanged; a diverged history gave diverged; an upstream revert was pulled; a busy callback gave waiting with HEAD unchanged, and the pull landed once it cleared; an unreachable origin gave offline; the timer logged 2 checks in 2.6 s and none after stop. The log for the dirty case reads "origin main is 1 commit(s) ahead, but the checkout has changes to tracked files, so nothing was pulled".
- Rollback, witnessed through the real relay, supervisor and worker (run relay4/run-1793603581, host driven over JSON-RPC): a commit that passes the gate and makes the worker exit before its handshake was pulled, the worker failed, and the log reads "rolled back 14276ab to b27c63c: the worker did not start on the new code (the worker failed to restart (the worker exited (code 1) before the handshake)); the worker answers on b27c63c". The checkout was back at its pre-pull commit and clean, and the worker answered tools/list with its three tools.
- Busy wait, witnessed in the same run: with background body body-9 running, a benign commit landed upstream. The log reads "origin main is ahead; the pull waits for 1 body or job(s) running or queued". The checkout did not move while the body ran, and "pulled 1 commit(s) to 5ffe447; the gate passed" followed the end of the body.
- No retry of a rolled-back commit (run relay3/run-1793603546): the commit was rolled back once and pulled once. Over the six seconds after the rollback, six "points at a commit that was refused" lines were logged, with no second pull, and the worker answered tools/list with its three tools.

- Updates, the check through every state (scratch, real git repositories, run run-1793602564, tools/update.mjs): updated (a benign push was pulled, the gate passed, the checkout equals origin); reverted (a push with a syntax error: the gate failed with "Unexpected token '='", the checkout went back to the prior commit, and its tree was clean); dirty (origin ahead by 2, a checkout with an uncommitted edit: HEAD unchanged); diverged (a local-only commit: HEAD unchanged); updated after the upstream revert (ahead by 3, the gate passed); offline (an unreachable origin was reported); timer (startUpdates every 1000 ms logged 2 checks in 2.6 s, none after stop, HEAD unchanged).
- setup.sh, first run in a fresh clone with no node_modules (run setup-check/run-1793602589): it installed the dependency with npm ci, the update check said current, the gate passed, the .mcp.json entry was printed, exit 0. Second run: dependencies present, update check current, gate passed, exit 0.
- Relay, started directly against a scratch checkout behind origin with a local edit (run relay-check/run-1793602683, GMNEW_UPDATE_MS=1000): 3 update lines in 3.5 s. The first was "origin main is 1 commit(s) ahead, but the checkout has changes to tracked files, so nothing was pulled". The checkout's HEAD was unchanged.

## Reminder carries the open PRD window (2026-11-02, owner request)

Each reminder of a background body (`job-running`, every `--notice-ms`, default 180000, the same flag pattern as `--stuck-ms`) carries `prd`: the PRD rows' total and open counts, the first five open rows in list order as `{id, title}`, and `more`, the open rows past the window. The counts are read when the reminder is made, so a row added during the body is in the next reminder. `next_context` passes `prd` through in `jobsRunning`. A failed row read is logged as `prd.error`, and the reminder is still made. Each reminder is 391 bytes with a five-row window.

Witness: scripted host over stdio, outside the repo (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/notice/notice-check.mjs`), run `notice-witness` with `--notice-ms 1000`, in a temporary workspace that was removed afterwards. Seven PRD rows were added and one resolved (total 7, open 6). A background body (`body-27`) slept 4 s, and an eighth row was added at 1.6 s while it ran. Reminders: seq 31 at 1002 ms (total 7, open 6, window 5, more 1), seq 38 at 2002 ms (total 8, open 7, more 2), seq 39 at 3003 ms (total 8, open 7, more 2). The window ids equal the first five ids of `prd_list` open at the end (`windowMatchesOpenList`). The `next_context` step after the body carries seq 31's `prd` in `jobsRunning`. `runGate` passes.

Limit: the reminder reaches the agent at its next `next_context`, not at the moment it is made. `skills/gm/SKILL.md` documents `prd` in its background-work step, in the same commit as the code, on gm's decision (one commit).

## Name and install (2026-11-02, owner's decision)

- The server is named `gm`, and it replaces the older `gm` server. The name is `gm` in the project `.mcp.json` key, in `serverInfo`, in the channel source (`<channel source="gm" ...>`, enabled with `--dangerously-load-development-channels server:gm`), in the skill's references, and in the approval list `.claude/settings.local.json`. The repository, its folder and its remote keep `gm-new`, because renaming them is outward-facing. The handshake names inside `tools/relay.mjs`, `tools/core.mjs` and `tools/supervise.mjs` are internal and stay, since the relay is frozen. Witness: a fresh scripted worker reports `serverInfo.name` `gm`, the channel source `gm`, and the same tool-list hash as before (`8fa6ea65...`, 3 tools).
- Install (2026-11-02, owner's direction; supersedes the add-mcp-per-agent install of this date): `./setup.sh` checks the tools, installs acorn if it is missing, runs the gate, and prints three commands. They run through `npx -y add-mcp@2.6.1`: `list -g`, then `remove gm -g -y`, then `"node <checkout>/tools/relay.mjs" -n gm -g -a claude-code -y`. setup runs none of them. The removal comes first, so any old or new gm entry is gone before gm is installed. add-mcp is not in `package.json`, so the one runtime dependency stays acorn, and the worker never loads add-mcp, so its memory cost per worker is zero. Reason: add-mcp through npx is the install path, and nothing more complicated is wanted.
- What add-mcp does, measured in sandbox HOMEs (fresh directories, no real config touched): (a) a global install with `--all -g` wrote 25 config files in one run (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/install/probe/install-all.out`); (b) `remove gm -g -y` matches any server whose name contains `gm`, so `gm-new` went too, and a server named `gmail` would as well, which is why `list -g` comes first; (c) `remove` reached only agents whose config directory exists: the probe's `.claude.json` was not reached, because that sandbox had no `~/.claude`, and with the directory present it was (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/install/claude-test/`); (d) a user-level install with `-a claude-code` writes `mcpServers.gm` with command `node` and the relay path as its only argument, and keeps the other keys; (e) a command string splits at spaces, so setup refuses a checkout path with a space. Earlier multi-agent installs wrote the global configs, which is why the user-level install names one agent.

## Collisions: repair after the fact (2026-11-02, owner's decision)

- Children and the parent edit the shared codebase at the same time. No branches, worktrees or path claims. When every child has finished and the parent's edits are in, one validation run covers the whole result. A failure is repaired in one pass, and the validation runs again. The skill (Handing work to a child session, steps 4 and 5) states this at every hand-off.
- Reason, from the owner: a post-repair validation run costs far less than a collision-avoidance strategy such as branches, worktrees or claims.
- Replaces: the path claims under `runs/claims` in the owner's subagent design, and the skill rule against two sessions writing one file. Neither was built, so no code is removed.
- Not measured: whether repair after the fact stays cheap on a real parallel edit. The first validation run after concurrent edits is that measurement, and it needs a live model run.

## Rules in the gm skill (2026-11-02, owner's direction)

- The seven rules of an MCP run are in `skills/gm/SKILL.md` word for word. The owner's reason: the skill is the last one loaded, so its rules are still there after a summary. One source holds the text: `MCP_RULES` in `src/prompt.mjs`. The served instructions and the run's `rules.md` use it, and the duplicate in `src/mcp.mjs` is removed. The text is unchanged. Witness (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/rules/rules-witness.mjs`): the served rules equal the rule block in HEAD, with 7 rules.
- The gate checks the gm skill against those rules (`ruleBodies` and `skillCheck` in `src/skills.mjs`). The check reads the skill without its `gm.` prefix, so `gm.query_log` matches `query_log`. Witness: the repository's skill passes; a copy of the skill without rule 4 fails with "gm skill lacks rule: finish needs a witness".
- Host facts, as reported by a subagent that read the Claude Code docs through a summarizing fetch (the exact wording is to be confirmed on the page): invoked skills are re-attached after compaction, "capped at 5,000 tokens per skill and 25,000 tokens total; oldest dropped first" (context-window page, "Invoked skill bodies"), and the budget fills from the most recently invoked skill. The server instructions are not documented to survive compaction. The skill is about 2,700 tokens (characters divided by four), under the 5,000 cap, so the cap is the limit to watch as the skill grows.
- Not documented in what was read: whether an MCP prompt invocation (`/mcp__gm__gm`) counts as an invoked skill for re-attachment. So the rules' survival is argued from the documented skill behaviour, not yet witnessed in a session.
- The row `unknown-rule-reinjection` stays blocked. Presence of the rules after a summary now has a documented route. Whether the model keeps to them after a summary is still unmeasured, and that needs a live run.
- gm and gm-continue: the owner's rule is that the cycles between them are not limited and not tracked. The skill's setup invariant (the gm-continue setup runs before any work) stays. Nothing in gm-new counts or limits those cycles (checked by search of the repository).

## Reconnect: the connect-time text (2026-11-02, owner's question and direction)

- Question: is hot reload good enough that the owner never relaunches? Before this change, no. Hot reload covers the worker (`src/`, `skills/`) and the core (`tools/core.mjs`, through the update check). Three things still needed a host reconnect: the bytes of `tools/relay.mjs` (the host holds its stdio, so the relay cannot reload), the instructions in the initialize reply, and the capabilities in it. The host reads those once per connection, so a change to the instructions needed a `/mcp` reconnect even though the worker had reloaded.
- Change: the name, the capabilities, the instructions of both surfaces and the relay file are in `src/connect.mjs`. The instructions now hold a pointer to the gm skill, and nothing else: the rules and the routes for pending events are in the skill, and `next_context` repeats the rules each step. `CONNECT_SHA` is a hash over those values, and the gate's `connect` check fails on any change until the owner accepts the new value and records it. So a reconnect is needed only when that check fails.
- Witness (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/witness-connect/connect-witness.mjs`, scripted, no model): the instructions are 540 characters on the code surface and 327 on tools, against about 1,550 characters before (code: 311 for the verbs, 584 for the pending routes, 647 for the rules). Neither carries `# Rules` or the routes. The capabilities equal HEAD's. The repository check passes. A sandbox copy with one changed phrase fails with the reconnect message. A relay started with no arguments, from a directory outside the checkout, answers as `gm`, on the code surface (the default), with the module's capabilities and instructions.
- Still needs a reconnect, once: the live self session still runs the earlier relay and instructions, so the owner's `/mcp` reconnect brings it to this text. After that, the hot paths need none.
- Still needs a relaunch, for channel mode only: Claude Code has to start with `--dangerously-load-development-channels server:gm`. That is a launch flag on the host, not a server change. The Monitor route does not need it.
- Trade-off for the owner to accept: the rules left the system prompt. The joint decision of 2026-11-02 (rule re-injection) put them there, and it is superseded in that decision's text. The rules still reach the agent through `next_context` on every step and through the skill, which the host re-attaches after a compaction within its cap. Whether the server's instructions survive a compaction is not documented either way, so nothing is lost that the docs guarantee.
- Not changed: the relay bytes (its hash is in `src/connect.mjs`), the core, and the worker's behaviour.

## Hot reload across the board, and pending events without channels (2026-11-02, owner's direction)

- Direction: hot reload across the board, not a reconnect per rule change. The rules stay out of the connect-time text (see "Reconnect: the connect-time text").
- Rule edits reach a run in progress. A resumed run takes the rules the server holds now (`openRun`, `refreshRules` in `src/run.mjs`). When `rules.md` differs from `MCP_RULES`, it is rewritten, and the log gets a `rules-updated` event with a 12-hex hash of the text before and after. Each step reads `rules.md`, so the next `next_context` returns the new rules. Before this, only a new run took an edit, and a run in progress kept the old text while the served skill carried the new one.
- Witness, scripted host (no model), through the real relay in a copy of the working tree (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/rules-hmr/rules-hmr.mjs`, result in `result.json` there), run `2026-11-02-12-27-56-edf075`: rule 3 edited at 0.2 s; the worker restarted (`changed: prompt.mjs`) at 0.5 s; the new rule was in `next_context` on the first poll at 2.2 s, on the same run, with a new `prefixSha`; `rules.md` was updated; the log has `rules-updated` (seq 6). The relay was not restarted and sent no second `initialize`. An earlier attempt was not counted: its edit hit the first occurrence of the sentence, which is in `DEFAULT_RULES`, so the witness now edits `MCP_RULES` only.
- Pending events without a channel or a Monitor: route 3 in the skill is now a background task of the host's shell that runs `pendingCommands.wait`, which `next_context` returns with absolute paths. `events --dir <run dir> --wait` waits until the queue has an event (checked every 200 ms), prints the events once, clears them, and exits. With no event it exits at its timeout (default 1800000 ms) with no output. The host reports when a background task ends, so the events reach the agent as they land; the agent acts on the output and starts the task again. `pendingCommands.read` (`--read-clear`) is the foreground fallback for a host with no background task. The earlier route 3 was a job read at each step, which did not wake the agent between steps.
- Witness, `events --wait` through the real CLI in scratch run directories (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wait-witness/wait-witness.mjs`): an event at 2 s printed its record and exited 0 at 2076 ms, with the queue gone; no event with a 1500 ms timeout exited 0 at 1660 ms with no output; a queued event printed at 261 ms; `--read-clear` printed once, then nothing; a missing directory, no mode, and a zero timeout each exited 1 with a message. The wait command from `next_context`, run by `sh` from `/tmp` with an event queued after 2 s, exited 0 at 2066 ms with the record, in the rules witness above.
- `takePending` skips a copy that another reader removed after listing it, instead of throwing (the same run path reads the queue). The wait adds a second reader, so this matters now.
- Limit: the connect-time text (name, capabilities, instructions) and `tools/relay.mjs` still need a reconnect when they change. MCP has no notification for the instructions, and the relay holds the host's stdio, so neither can be reloaded in place. The gate's `connect` check fails on any change to them until the new value is recorded in `CONNECT_SHA`.
- Not isolated: in one failed witness attempt (the edit that hit `DEFAULT_RULES`, whose scripted host then threw), the copy's relay and core stayed alive for about three minutes after the host died. The witness that finished cleanly had its relay exit 0.1 s after stdin closed. The cause of the first is not known, and no fix is claimed.

## Friction from oscraft run 23125-406580 (reported by oscraft-35, 2026-11-02, task "do")

- grep crashed the worker: `gm.grep` for 'python3' with limit 40, alongside other calls, ran out of heap near 2 GB. The cause: `loadFiles` read every tracked file, tokenized every line, and kept the lines in a cache that is never released, so grep held the whole tracked text. Fix (`src/search.mjs`): grep reads one file at a time, matches its lines and keeps nothing. Paths under `node_modules/` and `vendor/` are skipped unless `vendor` is true. The `search` and `wiki_search` indexes are unchanged.
- Witness, grep (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/friction/grep-probe.mjs`, a corpus of 120 tracked files of 900 KB plus one `node_modules` file, heap limit 2048 MB): before, the heap after one call was 1399 MB and the first hit was in `node_modules`; after, it is 22 MB, with no `node_modules` hit and 1500000 matches.
- Witness, grep totals on this repo: `grep` equals `git grep -I -c -F` for 'runCommand' (16), 'alpha' (5) and 'function ' (412).
- git_commit refused every commit in oscraft: the gate read gm-new's own `tools/relay.mjs` in that workspace, so the connect check (added with the connect-time text) threw ENOENT out of the gate, and the commit failed. Fix (`src/gate.mjs`): gm-new's own checks (ascii, docs, sources, wiki, skills, connect, smoke) run only in a gm-new checkout, recognised by the package name `gm-new`, which a deleted file cannot change. In any other workspace they are not run, and `notCovered` names them. Syntax there is narrowed as described in "Commit gate in other workspaces" below. A check that throws is now a failed check with its message, not an error out of the gate.
- Owner's review: in another workspace the gate no longer applies gm-new's ASCII and doc rules. The gm-new checkout keeps every check.
- Witness, gate: on a fresh repo that is not gm-new (`gate-probe.mjs`), before, `runGate` threw ENOENT; after, it passes with `failed` empty, syntax only, and five own checks named in `notCovered`. Negative control: a copy of gm-new with `tools/relay.mjs` deleted (removed afterwards) fails `syntax` and `connect`, with the reason, so the own checks still refuse.
- The `git commit --only` that oscraft-35 used as a workaround bypassed the gate. It is not endorsed. With this change, git_commit in oscraft runs the generic checks through the gate.
- read_file: `offset` and `limit` count characters, not lines (a page is at most 20000 characters). The description now says so. The peer's 50-character result was `limit` 50, which is 50 characters.

## Commit gate in other workspaces (2026-11-02, reported by oscraft-15)

- A commit in oscraft was refused with "refused: the commit gate failed: syntax" and no file named. The syntax step ran `node --check` on every tracked module, and six tracked gm probe files under `.gm/witness-out/` (each with a top-level `return`) fail it. The block came from the previous change, which kept syntax on the whole tree outside gm-new.
- Fix (`src/gate.mjs`, `src/git.mjs`): in a gm-new checkout, syntax still checks every tracked module. Elsewhere it checks only the modules the commit names, and a file is in scope when it is one of the commit's paths or lies under one. `notCovered` says so. The refusal message now carries each failing check's detail, so the file names appear in it.
- Witness, the real `git_commit` verb in a scratch repository that is not gm-new (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/scope-probe.mjs`, working directory `gate-scope/work`, removed by the probe on the next run). A tracked bad module and a clean module were staged. Before the fix, committing the clean file was refused with "refused: the commit gate failed: syntax". After it, the clean commit passed (head moved to "add clean module"), and committing the bad file was refused with "refused: the commit gate failed: syntax: syntax errors in probe.mjs".
- The probe files are not touched and not excluded by name. Their top-level `return` is a question for the probe writer: it can wrap its body in a function.

## Query log window, last, and run_code's action seq (2026-11-02, reported by oscraft-16)

- Problem, run 57050-684029 (oscraft-16): `js` saw one reply page (50 events or 20 KB), so a count over the run was wrong (1, against 40 read_file calls in the log), and a lookup of the last seq found seq 14. `last` was ignored without a word, so a tail returned the first page. An unknown run_code argument was ignored, so `code` read as a missing body ("body must be a non-empty string"). A run_code reply named no action.
- Change, `src/log.mjs`, `src/tools.mjs`, `src/mcp.mjs`, `src/runcode.mjs`:
  - `after` is the since filter. Only the events after that seq are read, and the file is read from the first of them.
  - `last` N, from 1 to 50, returns the newest N matching events, oldest first, and has no next. Without js, the newest events that fit a reply are returned, and `omitted` counts the older ones that did not fit. A tail walks back one event at a time, so a tail for a rare kind over a long log reads the log back to its N-th match.
  - `js` sees one window of matching events: at most 1000 events, read from at most 4 MB of log. When the window ends before the end of the log, `next` is set, and the next call with `after` set to it reads on. The window ends at the last event it read, so no event is skipped or read twice.
  - An unknown query_log argument is refused by name, and the refusal lists the arguments query_log takes. The argument types are checked.
  - run_code refuses an argument it does not take, by name. Its reply, foreground or background, carries `actionSeq`, the seq of its own action event. A refused action carries `actionSeq` too.
  - Body console output is not returned and not logged. The run_code description says so. This is unchanged, and the owner's decision on console capture is still open (see the body section above).
- Not built, routed to the owner (oscraft-16): a reopen verb and a status argument for prd_resolve, and a run that opens again after finish. run and next_context refuse with "run has ended" after finish, and only a reconnect opens a new run. The second is the shared-run question.
- Witness, the real relay on a copy of the working tree, a scripted host with no model.
  - Before: run 2026-11-02-16-18-44-8db6cd (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/query-fix/query-witness.mjs`, `witness-result.json`). The unknown run_code argument returned "body must be a non-empty string". `last: 3` returned the first page (next 14). The js count of read_file code-calls was 1, and its last seq was 14. The log holds 40 read_file code-calls, the last at seq 53.
  - After: run 2026-11-02-16-24-02-7d27f6 (`after-witness.mjs`, `after-result.json`). The unknown run_code argument is refused by name (seq 6, actionSeq 5). An unknown query_log argument inside a body is refused by name. A plain run_code reply has actionSeq 11, which is the seq of its action event. `last: 3` returns seqs 53, 54 and 55 with no next. `after: 53` returns seqs 54 to 58. The js count is 40 with no next. After 1100 more read_file calls, the whole log was counted in two windows: 655 read calls (next 679, the window ended before 1000 events, which the 4 MB budget does) and 485 (no next), total 1140, equal to the log's 1140 read_file code-calls.

## Update on push (2026-11-02, the owner's direction)

- Owner: gm-new must update and hot reload when we push, not up to an hour later. Before this, a push reached a running checkout at the next hourly check.
- Change, `tools/update.mjs`: DEFAULT_UPDATE_MS is 60000, was 3600000. Each check fetches origin main. The rest of the check is as before: a pull happens only into a clean checkout, only as a fast-forward, after the gate has passed the new commit in a clone, and not while a call, body or job runs. A pull that changes src/ or skills/ restarts the worker through the supervisor's watch. A pull that changes tools/ other than relay.mjs replaces the core. A worker that does not start rolls the pull back.
- Latency: the checks tick every 60 seconds from the start of the core, and the first check runs at 30 seconds. A push is pulled at the next tick, so at most about a minute after the push. The gate runs inside the same check, and it was not timed separately.
- Witness, the real relay, supervisor and worker, with the default settings (GMNEW_UPDATE_MS not set). The runner is a checkout of a scratch bare repository, not AnEntrypoint/gm-new, and the pusher pushes a change under src/ to it. Run 2026-11-02-16-27-12-145d7c (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/update-push/update-push-witness.mjs`, `update-push-result.json`). The first check at 30 seconds logged nothing. The push was at 36.1 seconds. At 61.1 seconds, 25 seconds after the push, the log reads "restarting the worker (changed: search.mjs)" and "pulled 1 commit(s) to b05d1c4; the gate passed, and the worker answered on the new code". A run_code body read the pulled file and found the pushed line (ok true, output "true").
- Limits, named:
  - A checkout with local changes to tracked files does not pull, so a push reaches a checkout only once that checkout is clean. A push from this checkout needs no pull: its files changed on disk at the edit, and the src/ watch restarted the worker then.
  - tools/relay.mjs is frozen and is not reloaded. A push that changes it reaches a host on the host's reconnect.
  - Each running checkout runs one git fetch a minute. Its network and wake-up cost is not measured here. The hourly default was chosen for idle wake-ups (see the idle measurements above). GMNEW_UPDATE_MS sets another interval, and 0 turns the check off.

## Agent notes split (2026-11-02, the owner's direction)

- AGENTS.md keeps the project facts that troubleshooting found, the decided list, the concurrency design, the current state and the continuation rule. Its process went to `skills/dev/SKILL.md`. The server serves only `skills/gm/SKILL.md`, so runs do not load the dev prose.
- The continuation rule: before new work, write what troubleshooting found. A project fact goes to AGENTS.md and a process lesson to the dev skill. A line that needed no troubleshooting is deleted. AGENTS.md went from 10106 to 9499 bytes.
- gm-continue is `skills/gm-continue/SKILL.md`, the batch planner committed in fc9b168, extended with step 3 (fill the missing rows and write the run's notes) and the finish invariant. A copy is installed in the host at `/config/.claude/skills/gm-continue/SKILL.md`. The backup at `/config/.claude/skillbak/gm-continue/SKILL.md` is the gm-era version and is not used.

## Dual skill setup (2026-11-02, the owner's diagnosis)

- Why the oscraft agent stopped before the drain was done: the setup had one skill and no finish step. The gm skill had a setup invariant for gm-continue, but nothing loaded gm-continue at finish, and nothing made the run keep working while rows were open. `finish` is not refused while PRD rows are open. It is refused only for a missing witness and for a running background run.
- Change, `skills/gm/SKILL.md`: a finish invariant. Before `finish`, load gm-continue. If it lists work, keep working in this run and do not finish. Step 7 points to it. The seven rules are unchanged.
- Change, `skills/gm-continue/SKILL.md`, extended and not replaced: its committed batch planner stays (setup, build then witness, hand-off, re-entry, end). Step 3 fills each missing row with `gm.prd_add`, writes the run's facts to AGENTS.md and its process to `skills/dev/SKILL.md`, and commits by explicit path. The hand-off carries any open row. The finish invariant loads gm with the remaining rows when work remains. gm names gm-continue as its finish invariant, and gm-continue names gm, so each skill lists the other.
- The first version of this change (commit 7e8c716) wrote over the committed gm-continue and dropped its batch planner. The planner is restored in the commit that records this, from fc9b168.
- Witness, through the relay on the working tree (run `2026-11-02-16-37-29-e8ce42`, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/gm-continue/witness.mjs`, `witness-result.json`): `prompts/get gm` carries the finish invariant. The list step returned no open rows. The fill step added the one missing row, and its loop skipped the row already open. The listing shows both rows open. The finish was accepted with rows open.
- Open, for the owner: `finish` is not refused while PRD rows are open, so the invariant is the only guard. A refusal, with a `stopRows` flag that ends the run and lists the open rows in the end event, would make the guard a tool rule. It changes the decided finish semantics, so it is not built.
- Not witnessed here: a host session that loads gm-continue by name. That needs a live session with the skill installed.
- Superseded for gm-continue by "Skills over MCP (2026-11-03, owner's direction)" at the end of this file: the server serves gm-continue as an MCP skill, so a copy in the host's skill directory is no longer how it reaches a host.

## Finish, the connection, and restarts (2026-11-02, reported by oscraft-16)

- Report: run 68247-767948 was finished with 124 PRD rows open, and the finish was sent by a child agent. The child shares the parent's gm connection, so it shares the run, and its finish closed the parent's run. The witness was the child's text, not a drain witness.
- Cause, in the code: a run belongs to one server connection. `finish` checks that a witness and gaps are well formed, and that no background run is running. It does not check which agent sends it, so any agent on the connection can end the run.
- The served gm skill said the opposite: "finish closes only this session's run". That line is corrected in step 7. The behaviour is unchanged.
- Restarts: a restart or a roll ends a run with `paused` and no `finish`. oscraft-16 reports 42 run directories with logs, and many are three-event runs (start and tools) that close without a finish. Their turns ended in prose, or the server rolled.
- Not built, for the owner (the shared-run question): (a) a child agent gets its own run through a key the caller passes to next_context; (b) `finish` is refused unless the caller passes the run's key; (c) each child gets its own connection, and so its own run.
- Until one of these is chosen, a child agent on a parent's connection must not send `finish`, and the finish invariant in the gm skill is the only guard.

## Finish refused while rows are open (2026-11-02, the owner's yes)

- `finish` is refused while any PRD or question row is open. The refusal names up to five of them, and the run stays open, so the agent can work the rows and call again. `stopRows: true` ends the run anyway, and each open row is logged as a gap in the end event.
- Code: `openRows` in `src/rows.mjs`, exposed as `tools.openRows` in `src/tools.mjs`. `finish()` in `src/mcp.mjs` checks it after the background-run check and before `stop('finished')`, so a refused finish does not end the run.
- The rule-4 notice fired on any refusal whose text contained "witness", so a row titled with the word triggered it. The notice now matches only the witness refusal's own text, "needs a non-empty witness".
- Witness, through the relay on the working tree (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/finish-rows/witness.mjs`, `witness-result.json`). A, run 2026-11-02-16-41-44-dad598: one row open. finish was refused with the row named, one refusal event with actionSeq 10, and no rule notice. run_code still answered. finish with stopRows ended the run, and the end event's gaps hold "open row prd-mvzhkujjf018: open row for the finish witness". B, run 2026-11-02-16-41-28-da7d18, no row: finish was accepted and the run ended.
- The gm-continue witness (`2026-11-02-16-37-29-e8ce42`) finished with rows open. That finish is refused now, which is the behaviour this change is for.
- Not decided here: the shared-run question. A child agent on a parent's connection can still send `finish`, and the refusal for open rows does not check the sender.

## Row states: open means actionable (2026-11-02, the owner's report)

- Report: the gm-continue setup printed "124 open rows and 0 actionable" for a checkout. The owner's rule: an open row is actionable, so a row that cannot be worked is not open.
- Cause, in the code: the planner plans a row only when `owner !== true` and `triggerMet === true`. No row verb writes either field, and `renderRow` writes only the names in `FIELDS`, so a verb-written row never has `triggerMet`. Every open row written by a verb was therefore unplanned. The two fields came from commit 96ab430, which was written by hand. In this checkout the setup printed 10 open rows and 0 actionable, with 7 blocked.
- Change, `src/rows.mjs`: a row is `open` (the worker can work it now; finish is refused while one is open), `blocked` (it cannot be worked now; `fact` names the unmet condition and `trigger` names what reopens it, and an owner decision is a trigger that names the owner), or `resolved`. New verbs, one per kind: `prd_block` and `unknown_block` take `{ id, fact, trigger }` and need an open row; `prd_reopen` and `unknown_reopen` take `{ id }` and need a blocked row, and they remove the fact and trigger. `prd_list` and `unknown_list` take `status: "blocked"`. A blocked row does not hold finish.
- Change, `tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs`: it plans every open row, and lists each held row with its fact and trigger. A blocked row without a fact or a trigger is reported as a row defect.
- Migration: the 10 non-resolved rows are blocked, each with a fact and a trigger. Three were open questions waiting on an owner's approval, and they are blocked now with that approval as the trigger. The `owner` and `triggerMet` lines were removed from the 21 rows that had them.
- Witness, through the relay on the working tree (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/row-states/witness.mjs`, `witness-result.json`, run 2026-11-02-16-49-10-2b35dc): a block with an empty fact was refused; a block with a fact and trigger was accepted, and the row file shows `status: "blocked"`, the fact and the trigger; a reopen of an open row was refused; a reopen of the blocked row was accepted and its fact and trigger were removed; `finish` was refused and named the open row; the planner planned the open row and held the blocked one with its fact and trigger; a hand edit that removed the fact made the planner report the row defect; `finish` with `stopRows` ended the run with the open row as a gap (end event seq 32). An earlier run of the same script, 2026-11-02-16-49-00-4ee64e, showed the same refusals and holds. It stopped at a witness-script bug after the planner, and was not kept.
- Real checkout after the migration: `node tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs` prints "Batch plan: 0 open row(s)" and "Held rows: 10", each held row with its fact and trigger, and no defect.
- Not decided here: which held rows the owner opens. `prd_reopen` or `unknown_reopen` opens one when its trigger is met. The setup's "open runs" count is a count of run directories with no end event, not of rows, and it is not changed here.
- Which checkout reported the 124: /workspace/oscraft, not gm-new (reported by oscraft-16 through oscraft-12, 2026-11-02; not inspected from here). Its work/rows holds 148 files, 124 open, and 122 of the open rows carry an owner value. None has a triggerMet field, so the old planner planned none of them. The fixed planner plans every open row, so it would plan all 124 if their status is open. Whether an owner value should hold a row is open, for the owner.
- Reported with it: run 68247-767948 was finished at log seq 315 by a child on the parent's connection (ok true), with about 124 PRD rows open. The open-row refusal (6deacd0) is not witnessed on the server that serves run 76804-883309, whose next_context still returns the seven old rules. Open, for the owner: which checkout serves that run, and whether it has the refusal.

## Rows without a hold (2026-11-02, the owner's rule)

- Rule: a row is open until it is implemented, or, for a PRD, deleted. Nothing holds a row. This supersedes the `blocked` state, its fact and trigger, and the block and reopen verbs described in "Row states" above (commit 7aa0e96).
- Change, `src/rows.mjs`: statuses are open, resolved and all. `prd_delete({ id })` removes a PRD that is not resolved, and git keeps the file. An open question is resolved with a witness, never deleted. The block and reopen verbs are gone, and so are their `fact` and `trigger` fields.
- Change, `tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs`: it plans every open row. Any other status is a defect, and the setup names each such row.
- Data: the held PRD `prd-adaptive-codemode` is deleted, under the owner's rule. Its pre-registration was parked and never run. Nine held open questions remain as `blocked` rows, and the setup reports each as a defect. The owner has not yet said whether each is to be resolved with a witness or raised as a PRD.
- Witness, through the relay on the working tree (run 2026-11-02-16-57-31-5a3e5e, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/rows-delete/witness.mjs`, `witness-result.json`): `prd_delete` of an open PRD returned ok and removed its file. A second delete found no row. `prd_delete` of an open question was refused. `gm.prd_block` is not a function. `finish` was refused while the other PRD and the question were open, and named both. `stopRows` ended the run with both as gaps.

## Skill in place (2026-11-02, the owner's direction)

- Before: a change to a skill file was a restart. The worker read SKILL.md once at boot, so each edit restarted it. The restart paused the run (a `paused` and a `restart` event) and killed background jobs.
- Change, `src/mcp.mjs`: the skill is read on each request (prompts/list, prompts/get, resources/list, resources/read). A file caught mid-write keeps the last text that parsed. A skill that never parses fails at its first request, not at boot.
- Change, `tools/core.mjs`: a change under `skills/` queues a `refresh` transition. It compares the host's lists and sends a notice only for a list whose content changed. It does not restart the worker. A refresh is dropped while another transition is queued, because that transition announces the lists.
- Change, `tools/update.mjs`: a pull restarts the worker only when it changes `src/`.
- Witness, a copy of the working tree through the relay (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/skill-hmr/witness.mjs`). Baseline, run 2026-11-02-16-59-25-f9f06d: one edit gave one restart and one paused event, and the new text showed after 502 ms. After the change, run 2026-11-02-17-00-01-137a3c: no restart and no paused event, one refresh line in the core's stderr, and the new text showed after 502 ms.
- Not measured: how the host reacts to a list notice when a skill's name or description changes. The witness changes only the body.
- Not changed by this section: a change to a `src/` module outside the reloadable set still restarts the worker. The reloadable set is in "Hot reload in place", below.
- Shared checkout is live (2026-11-02, an incident). Between about 17:00 and 17:04, uncommitted edits under src/ and tools/ in /workspace/gm-new were live. A takeover replaced the cores of hy3d (run 79960-959581), oscraft (79788-955320) and cc (85517-1014344), and their workers started from that tree. hy3d's worker failed with "renderTools is not defined", and its background jobs died with it. The tracked files were restored to HEAD 3bcda99 at 17:04, and the untracked modules were removed. The half-made change is kept in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/hot-reload/wip`. Witness of the restored HEAD: run 2026-11-02-17-04-49-eb672a. Rule: a change is built in a scratch copy and applied to the checkout once it is witnessed (skills/dev/SKILL.md).
- Reported, not reproduced here: on oscraft, between about 16:21 and 16:36, restarts opened new three-event runs. Later restarts stayed inside the same run. The cause is open.

## Hot reload in place (2026-11-02, the owner's direction)

The owner asked for the most in-place reload possible, so an edit costs a restart only when a worker must hold code that a reload cannot give it.

- Reloadable set, `src/hot-config.mjs`: git.mjs, rows.mjs, fetch.mjs, code-ast.mjs, wiki-tools.mjs, review-tools.mjs. Each is a leaf: tool definitions, compaction table and verb builder. Every other module under src/ is a singleton (log, jobs, search paging, the surface) and still restarts.
- Loader, `src/hot.mjs` and `src/hot-hook.mjs`: a module.register hook adds `?gen=N` to a reloadable module's URL while a generation counter, shared through a SharedArrayBuffer, is above zero. A bump makes the next import of each reloadable module a fresh instance, including the modules it imports. Singletons keep their plain URL, so their state survives.
- Worker, `src/mcp.mjs`: surfaceOf builds the surface (tool list, verbs, registry) from a set of leaf modules. reloadLeaves bumps the generation, loads the set, builds the session's verbs from it (tools.mjs setLeaves) and swaps it in. Each step is built before anything is replaced, so a failed build leaves the old code serving. The worker logs a `reload` event with the generation.
- Sessions: createTools takes the leaf set. Its default is the boot set, which the CLI uses. A session started after a reload serves the new code.
- Prefix: the prefix hash is keyed on the surface, so a reload that changes the tool list changes `prefixSha`, as a restart would.
- Core, `tools/core.mjs`: the watcher gathers one debounce window of changes under src/. A window that names only reloadable modules is a `reload` transition. Any other name restarts, as before. A refused reload (a module does not load) is logged as "reload refused", and the old code keeps serving. Transitions run through the TRANSITIONS table.
- Updates, `tools/update.mjs`: a pull restarts the worker only for a src/ module outside the reloadable set. A pushed leaf change reaches the worker through the same watcher, and the update line says the change is served in place.
- Witness, local edits, `witness.mjs` in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/hot-reload2, run 2026-11-02-17-11-39-ce943c: a leaf edit reloaded 401 ms after the edit (generation 1). A background job started before the reload exited with its output. A broken rows.mjs was refused ("Unexpected token ';'") and the old code kept serving. The restored file reloaded (generation 3) with no restart event. A change to log.mjs restarted the worker once, with one paused event, and the run id did not change. All eleven checks pass. An earlier run, r1 (2026-11-02-17-11-11-e94539), failed three checks: its listing used the default filter, which holds no open rows in the copy. The listing now uses status all.
- Witness, pushed changes, `push-witness.mjs`, run 2026-11-02-17-13-02-5b0ffb, a scratch origin and a runner on default update settings: a pushed leaf commit was pulled at the 30 s check and reloaded in place 31064 ms after the push, with no restart line. A pushed singleton commit restarted the worker once, 29561 ms after the push. The update line says the worker answered on the new code, and the run id did not change. All six checks pass.
- Witness, batch apply, `apply-witness.mjs`, run 2026-11-02-17-14-52-781765: a runner on the committed code took in the seven changed files. The old core handed over to the new core in 1204 ms, and the new core took over on the same run. A leaf edit then reloaded on the new core. All six checks pass. The apply cost two worker restarts: one for the src/ window (hot-config.mjs is new and not reloadable) and one for the handover. A background job running at the time was killed.
- Not measured: the memory cost of a reload. Node keeps each generation's module instances in its module map.
- Not closed: a pushed commit whose reloadable module does not load is not rolled back. The old code keeps serving, and the next restart would start on the broken code. Whether the gate loads the leaf modules was not checked.
- Not closed: the src/ and tools/ windows debounce separately, so one batch can restart a worker twice. Merging the windows would save one restart. Not done.
- Not closed: a singleton that imports a leaf statically keeps the boot code. Today only tools.mjs (its default, used by the CLI) and skills.mjs (the gate, a separate process) do so.

## Wake by a watcher, long-poll and host-idle (2026-11-03, the owner's direction)

Problem. oscraft run 79788-955320 ended its turn with no watcher running and asked the user whether to finish. Its last model action ended at 03:29:54 UTC, and no `next_context` followed after 03:26:41. A supervisor SIGTERM paused it at 03:39:26, and the run has no finish event. Nothing reached the idle agent: a paused run wrote no event that woke it, and the wait route was optional. The fix needs no channel and no Monitor. The agent starts a watcher at the start of every turn, and the watcher exits on the events that matter.

Changes:
- `src/pending.mjs`: `paused` and `host-idle` join the pending kinds, so a supervisor pause and a quiet spell reach the watcher. `offPendingAppended` is added.
- `src/mcp.mjs`: `next_context` takes `waitMs`, capped at 300000. The call holds until an event is pending or the wait passes (`untilPending`). The hold is an active call, so the watchdog does not count it as idle. A watchdog queues one `host-idle` event after `--host-idle-ms` (default 600000) with no call in flight and none since. It queues once per quiet spell, and it stops when the run ends. The channel notification (`notifications/claude/channel`) is removed.
- `src/connect.mjs`: the `claude/channel` experimental capability is removed, and CONNECT_SHA is recorded as 740b70f6, per the owner's direction (no channels). A host that connected earlier keeps its cached capability until it reconnects.
- `skills/gm/SKILL.md`, Pending events: the watcher starts at the start of every turn while the run is open, and a turn does not end with the run open and no watcher running. A host with no background process uses `next_context` with `waitMs`, and `pendingCommands.read` at each step. The Monitor and channel routes are removed.
- `skills/gm-continue/SKILL.md`: the next step after the list is the gm skill, with the list and what is left as its task (INVARIANT). A pass that closes no row is not a stop: the loop ends with `finish`, `stopRows: true`, and each open row named in the gaps, with the witness.

Witnesses (scratch copies, real relay, `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wake`):
- `wake-witness.mjs`, run 2026-11-03-04-32-22-6acbf2 (`wake-result-w1.json`), all ten checks pass. A job exit woke a `--wait` watcher after 3063 ms. `next_context` with `waitMs` returned on a job exit after 2005 ms, and it timed out after 3004 ms with nothing pending. A SIGTERM to the worker woke a watcher with a `paused` record after 471 ms, and the same run answered afterwards. The initialize reply declares no channel capability, and no channel notification was sent. A finish with an open row was refused, and `stopRows` ended the run with that row as a gap. A second relay with `--host-idle-ms 3000` woke a watcher with a `host-idle` record after 4061 ms.
- Regression, `reload-witness.mjs` (in-place reload), run 2026-11-03-04-32-43-522e21: all eleven checks pass, and the leaf edit reloaded in 401 ms.

Not witnessed:
- The host's wake. The witness runs the watcher as a child process. Whether a host wakes an agent when a background process ends is a property of the host, and no live session has been observed doing it yet.
- A planned restart also pauses the run, so it wakes a watcher too. oscraft run 79788-955320 had 38 restarts, so its agent would have woken 38 times. The cost in a live session is not measured.
- Whether a host cuts a waiting `next_context`. The wait is capped at 300000 ms for that reason.

## Self-pause keeps calls held, and the live stops (2026-11-03, the owner's direction)

Problem. The worker's own pause (SIGTERM, `pauseRun` in src/mcp.mjs) logs `paused` and exits 0. The core read every exit while running as a crash: it answered each call with "the worker exited unexpectedly (code 0)" and waited for the 1 s retry before it started the worker again. A `job_start` answered that way wrote no job event, so a watcher waiting on that job could not wake. Witness of the defect, before the change: turn-loop run 2026-11-03-04-49-00-19a58c. The SIGTERM was at 2386 ms, the worker exited at 2943 ms, error replies ran until the restart at 3222 ms, and a watcher on `jobB-4` waited until the 150 s watchdog. The log of that run was removed with its scratch directory.

Change. `tools/core.mjs`, `onWorkerExit`: an exit with code 0 and no signal, while running, is a self-pause. The core sets the state to `restarting`, answers each call in flight with `PAUSED_BEFORE_ANSWER` (check query_log, then repeat the call if it did nothing), and queues a restart at once. New calls are held until the handshake is replayed. Any other exit fails the calls as before. The gm skill, Pending events, says a job whose start answered with an error wrote no job event, so do not wait on it.

Witnesses (scratch copies at `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/selfpause/tree`, real relay, `turn-loop.mjs` in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/wake`):
- turn-loop, run 2026-11-03-04-52-50-209d27: three jobs. The SIGTERM killed `jobB-2`, the loop started `jobB-3`, and the run finished in four turns. Restart reason "the worker paused itself". No error reply after the pause, and the finish was accepted with end status finished.
- turn-loop, run 2026-11-03-04-52-12-c1ec9f: the first run on the fix, before two loop fixes (the refusal test and the state line). It also finished.
- wake-witness, run 2026-11-03-04-53-10-d03586: all ten checks pass.
- reload-witness, run 2026-11-03-04-53-27-22219c: all eleven checks pass.

Live runs at the apply (04:48 UTC). Both were idle, with no watcher and no call since before 8ed0e94. hy3d run 79960-959581: its agent ended its turn at 03:14 UTC by asking the owner to decide the push. It restarted on the new code at 04:33:45 and logged host-idle at 04:43:45. oscraft run 150276-4824995: its agent ended at 03:30 UTC by asking the owner for an answer, and it has 7 events since 03:39. Neither agent has run a turn under the new instructions, so the watcher wake is not yet seen live. The apply restarts those idle workers.

Live witness of the host wake, 2026-11-03, self run `2026-11-03-05-07-18-ba0516` (this session's host). A watcher (`events --wait`) started in the background woke the agent: its exit came on the job's `running` record at 05:08:08 UTC, and the host re-invoked the agent with the task notification. next_context then read the `exited` record of job `wake-probe` (exit 0, output PROBE-DONE, at 05:08:11 UTC). So a host wakes when a background watcher exits. The start of a job also wakes it, so each job costs two wakes. Open: a `running` job record should not end a watcher. It is not changed yet.

Stdout and stderr test, 2026-11-03. A background shell (Bash `run_in_background`) wrote one line to stdout and one to stderr at 8 s and 20 s after the agent went idle, and exited at 30 s. No transcript entry was written until the watcher's `host-idle` record at 05:19:49 UTC, which was the first new turn (self run `2026-11-03-05-07-18-ba0516`, test `bfjqc1m73`). So a background shell wakes the agent on exit only, not on its output. Monitor differs: each of its stdout lines becomes a notification, and stderr does not. Runner output reaches neither a background shell nor a Monitor, so the runner's text does not wake an agent. The earlier test `bhfj1d6es` wrote while the agent was still in its turn, so it does not decide the question.

Instructions name no host feature (2026-11-03, the owner's direction: every agent uses whatever its host offers, without naming a host feature). `skills/gm/SKILL.md`, Pending events, now asks for the most effective wait the host offers, one that ends at the next event and hands over the events. A host that cannot hold a wait calls `next_context` with `waitMs`. Witness: a search of the skill for Monitor, Claude Code, channel, task-notification, run_in_background, background process and background task finds no match, and the search of the other agent-facing files (AGENTS.md, README.md) finds only the statement that the Claude Code session is the host, which identifies the host and uses no feature. The code comment at `src/mcp.mjs` line 482 is not served to agents, and it is unchanged.

Backgrounded tool call as a wait (2026-11-03, the owner's direction: use a host's background tool calls when it has them). Where a host can run a tool call in the background, a `next_context` call with `waitMs` run that way is a wait, and its return wakes the agent with the step's events. The skill says so, and a foreground host still holds the step with the same call. Not witnessed here: this host's MCP tools take no background option (its shell tool does), so this path has no live witness yet. Each return is one step, and a held call counts as an active call for the host-idle watchdog, as the `waitMs` witness above shows.

## Skills over MCP (2026-11-03, owner's direction)

- Change: both skills are served as MCP skills under SEP-2640 (the io.modelcontextprotocol/skills extension, Final). `src/connect.mjs` declares the extension and keeps the resources capability. The server answers skills/list and skills/get for gm and gm-continue. Each file of a skill is a resource, read from its skill directory on each request, with its sha256 digest and size, as the SEP requires. The gm prompt is unchanged.
- URI rename: gm://skill/SKILL.md becomes skill://gm/SKILL.md, and gm-continue is served at skill://gm-continue/SKILL.md. The SEP requires the final segment of a skill path to equal its frontmatter name. The old path was `skill`, not `gm`, so the old URI did not conform. resources/read of the old URI is now refused with -32602. The connect-time pointer, the rules notice, and both skills name the new URIs.
- Frontmatter defect, found by the witness: the gm-continue description held ": " inside a plain YAML value ("run: it fills"). A strict YAML reader reads that as a mapping and refuses the file (PyYAML: "mapping values are not allowed here"). The description now reads "run; it fills". `parseSkill` in `src/skills.mjs` refuses a value with ": " or " #" in it, a quoted value, a value that starts with a YAML indicator, and a frontmatter line that is not key: value, so a served object is never partial. A served skill's name must equal its directory, and the gate's skills check parses each served skill.
- Core: no change. `tools/core.mjs` forwards every method, and its list notices cover tools, prompts and resources only. The SEP defines no list-changed notification for skills. A change to a skill's file set or description changes resources/list, and the core's refresh sends resources/list_changed for that. A body edit changes only the digests, which a host reads again with skills/get.
- Connect-time text: CAPABILITIES gains the extension, and the pointer names skill://gm/SKILL.md. CONNECT_SHA moves from 740b70f60adc89b8 to ec7180e23afa5292, which is connectSha of this checkout. The owner's direction asked for the change, so the new value is recorded. A host reads the initialize reply once, so it must reconnect (/mcp) before it sees the extension and the new pointer. Until then, its connect-time pointer names gm://skill/SKILL.md, which the server now refuses. The rules notice and the gm prompt name the new URI, and the server answers skill://gm/SKILL.md to any host that asks for it.
- Witness, on the scratch tree /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/skills-mcp, with the direct stdio check (/tmp/gm-scratch/witness-src/witness.mjs) and the relay check (relay-witness.mjs, through tools/relay.mjs and the core):
  - Before (HEAD code), run run-before-1793684087108: 7 of 21 checks pass. tools/list returns 3 tools, sha256 1789daed0f689ccebb4830b91a78ea39eef67fe8a1cab25d8435d85575f54176. resources/list holds gm://skill/SKILL.md only, and skills/list is -32601.
  - After, run run-after-1793684246552: 29 of 29 checks pass. tools/list returns the same 3 tools and sha256. resources/list holds skill://gm/SKILL.md and skill://gm-continue/SKILL.md. skills/list has two complete entries: skill://gm/SKILL.md (11713 bytes, sha256:aa9dd8c64f9d...) and skill://gm-continue/SKILL.md (3734 bytes, sha256:cae2b43a4986...). Each listed resource reads back with its digest and size, 2 of 2. skills/get returns each entry equal to its listing entry, an unknown uri is -32602, resources/read of gm://skill/SKILL.md is -32602, and prompts/get gm carries the finish invariant. The log holds skill-loaded events with skill gm and gm-continue.
  - Relay, run directory relay-runs-after/2026-11-03-05-38-04-20a5f1: 9 of 9 checks pass through tools/relay.mjs and the core, with the same tools sha256.
  - Negative control, frontmatter: PyYAML safe_load on the original gm-continue frontmatter fails, and on the gm frontmatter it parses. `parseSkill` refuses the original gm-continue text ("description has ': ' inside it") and parses the edited text.
  - Syntax: node --check passes for src/mcp.mjs, src/skills.mjs and src/connect.mjs.
  - Gate, runGate on the scratch tree, with the tracked file list read from the repository index through GIT_DIR: gate true, failed empty. syntax 41 modules parse; ascii 85 text files; docs 219 paths; sources 3; wiki none; skills 3 skill(s), and gm and gm-continue parse as served skills; connect ec7180e23afa5292; smoke finished, and the negative control stopped at max_steps.
- Limits: a host that has not reconnected keeps the old pointer, as above. The parser reads plain scalars only, so a served skill with quoted values or a nested metadata map is refused until parseSkill reads that form. Files are served as UTF-8 text, and no served file is binary. The SEP limits (512 resources and 16 MiB per skill) are not checked, and both skills are far below them.
- Not verified here: whether Claude Code lists MCP skills in its Skill list. That needs a live /mcp reconnect and a look at the host's Skill list. The copy at /config/.claude/skills/gm-continue is not touched here; the lead removes it after this witness.
- Applied to the checkout /workspace/gm-new: the live workers on it (self, hy3d and oscraft) restart, because src/mcp.mjs, src/connect.mjs and src/skills.mjs are not reloadable (Hot reload in place, above). Their background jobs are killed, and each run resumes from its log.
- Witness on the checkout after the copy: the direct check, run run-checkout-1793684372786, passes 29 of 29, with the same tools hash (1789daed0f689cce) and two resources read back at the listed digests and sizes. The relay check passes 9 of 9. The dev skill's line was re-applied on HEAD 378bc1c, because a peer commit (b90b9c3) had changed that file.

## Protocol conformance and cancellation, batch 1 (2026-11-03)

Plan: /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/maximal-plan.md, sections 2 and 7. The owner adopted O1 to O8 as recommended: negotiate 2025-11-25 and 2025-06-18 only; server/discover answers -32601; the run id under com.anentrypoint.gmnew/run, on results only; a cancel honoured for a foreground run_shell and for no other call; progress every 5 s; a job_stop verb; annotations set on every tool.

- D1, `src/mcp.mjs`: initialize echoes the requested protocolVersion when it is 2025-11-25 or 2025-06-18, and otherwise answers 2025-11-25. The connect text is unchanged, so CONNECT_SHA holds and no reconnect is needed.
- D2: server/discover falls through to method not found, -32601.
- D3 (F7): a name the surface does not serve is a JSON-RPC error -32602, "Unknown tool: NAME". On the code surface the message keeps the guidance that a verb is called as gm.<name>(args) inside run_code. A tool that fails keeps isError true.
- D4 (F8a): resources/read of a missing uri is -32002 with data.uri. A uri with a .. segment is not served, so it gets the same answer. skills/get keeps -32602, which this batch does not change.
- D5 (F4): notifications/cancelled is routed by cancelRequest in `src/mcp.mjs`. A foreground run_shell is aborted: runCommand in `src/exec.mjs` kills its process group, the call settles as cancelled, and handleLine sends no reply for that id. A read-only or side-effecting verb is not stopped; the cancel is logged as cancel-ignored with its reason, and the call answers as usual. In `tools/core.mjs` a run_shell request is kept in shellCalls. Its cancel removes it from inflight (cancelShell) and signals a waiting restart, so a restart does not wait for an answer that never comes. Other requests stay counted until their answer, so a restart waits for a side-effecting call that is still running. Limit: fetch is not in the worker's READ_ONLY_TOOLS set, so a cancel of fetch is logged as side-effecting; the set was not changed here.
- D7 (O8): job_stop is a verb (in BASE_TOOLS, and job_stop in `src/tools.mjs`). It kills the job's process group, and the job event (state killed, reason cancelled) and job-killed are logged before it answers. The status file carries the reason. A job that already ended is an error result, not a throw. A background body is an error result, so stopBackground still stops bodies only. Every kill now writes its reason to the job event and the status file, so a lifetime kill carries reason lifetime there too.
- F5: a foreground run_shell whose _meta.progressToken is a string or an integer gets notifications/progress every 5000 ms. Progress is the elapsed seconds, never less than the one before, and the message is "running N s". The timer ends with the call. Reports go out through notify, which does not run afterReply, because those actions belong to a reply that is not yet written.
- F6: every tool carries a title (its name with the words capitalised) and the hints readOnlyHint (READ_ONLY_TOOLS, except next_context), destructiveHint (true for a tool that is not read-only and is not next_context or finish) and openWorldHint (true for fetch, git_fetch, git_pull, git_push and ci_status). run_code and run_shell are openWorldHint false, as the owner's list says. Both can reach the open world, so the owner should decide that. No outputSchema and no structuredContent.
- F9: the gm prompt has the title "gm run".
- F13: the string members traceparent, tracestate and baggage of a tools/call _meta are copied into the call's action event, and into the action event of a refusal.
- F14: each tools/call result carries _meta with com.anentrypoint.gmnew/run set to the run id that next_context reports. A replayed answer after a restart carries it too. Errors carry no _meta.
- `src/skills.mjs` lists job_stop in FILE_TOOLS, so a skill that names it without the gm. prefix is refused by the gate. `skills/gm/SKILL.md` names gm.job_stop.
- README.md and docs/index.html name job_stop in the verb lists, and state the protocol versions, the cancel and the progress.
- Not in this batch: the 2026 path (F3, F16, the tasks extension), F8b, F15, and any change to the connect text, CAPABILITIES or tools/relay.mjs.

Tools hashes after the batch: default surface sha 836ea50420d01153637b4f5a7d062d324428e8bd97d1c613b70b62b824b89932 (3 tools; was 1789daed); tools surface sha 4550c22c7749581b6790fbf67073fd9ba4a81311c52b609fa14665335a62cad9 (42 tools; was 7914a5eb with 41).

Witness, on a tar copy of HEAD 17a0a14 under /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new (node_modules is a symlink), through tools/relay.mjs and tools/core.mjs of the copy, with the update interval set high. Driver /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/witness-batch1.mjs, results in witness-batch1-results.json, and the two run logs in witness-batch1-logs, both in the same directory. Session A is the default surface, run 198971-5755703. Session B is --surface tools, run 199015-5756097. One run, 21 of 21 checks pass:
- (a) initialize with 1900-01-01 answered 2025-11-25 (run B; a reply only, no log event). With 2025-06-18 it echoed 2025-06-18 (run A).
- (b) server/discover answered -32601; run A error event seq 3.
- (c) tools/call nope answered -32602 "Unknown tool: nope; ..."; run A error event seq 6.
- (d) resources/read of a missing uri answered -32002 with data.uri, run A error seq 7. A uri with a .. segment answered -32002 with data.uri, seq 8.
- (e) run_shell "sleep 30" as id 7 (run B action seq 5). cancel seq 6, then result seq 7 with cancelled true. No response for id 7, and the sleep process was gone 2.5 s later, checked with ps. A touch of src/mcp.mjs restarted the worker: restart seq 9, reason "changed: mcp.mjs". The core stderr showed the restart and the worker running, and next_context was answered after it.

## gm-continue as an MCP prompt (2026-11-03)

Problem. Claude Code's Skill tool does not resolve MCP skills: once the user-level copy of gm-continue was gone, it answered "Unknown skill: gm-continue". The server served gm-continue as a skill resource (skill://gm-continue/SKILL.md), but `prompts/list` returned only the gm prompt, so a host had no prompt for it.

Change, `src/mcp.mjs`:
- `prompts/list` answers two prompts, gm and gm-continue, each with its name, title and description from the skill frontmatter. The titles are "gm run" and "gm continue". The skills are read on each request, as before (`skillPrompt` and `promptResult`).
- The gm prompt keeps its `task` argument, and gm-continue takes none. The brief asked for no arguments on both. The gm prompt keeps one because the session prompt is how a run started without --task gets its task, and the user-level relay starts the worker without one. A host that is not told the argument cannot send it.
- `prompts/get gm-continue` returns the skill's body, the file without its frontmatter, as one user message, as the gm prompt does. It sets no task and clears no rules notice. It logs one `skill-loaded` event with via prompt and an empty cleared list, the kind a resource read of the same skill already logs. No new event kind.
- `prompts/get gm` is unchanged in its behaviour. Only its name check now accepts any served skill and then branches.

Change, `skills/gm-continue/SKILL.md`: the description now says to read skill://gm-continue/SKILL.md with ReadMcpResourceTool (server gm), or to run the gm-continue prompt, before every finish of a gm run. It named no route before. The body already named the MCP route for skill://gm/SKILL.md, and it has no Skill tool wording.

Not changed, `skills/gm/SKILL.md`. The brief asked that its finish step name the MCP route to gm-continue. No such step exists: the gm-continue setup and finish invariants were removed from the served gm skill on 2026-11-02 (Skill cleanup, above), because gm-continue plans gm-new's rows, and runs in other projects read the same served skill. A pointer there would send them to tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs, which only gm-new has. The seven rules are unchanged. If the owner wants a finish pointer in the served gm skill, that is a decision to make first.
