## Outside access allowed (2026-11-03)

Owner direction, verbatim: "all outside access should be allowed we must document that".

Reading taken: outside access means paths outside this workspace, for the file tools and for the agents' own reading. A file-tool path may be relative (it is taken from the workspace, so `../x` reaches outside) or absolute. A `..` segment, a symlink that leads outside and a dangling symlink are followed, not refused. Not changed: the no-API-calls rule (2026-11-01) and the network verbs. The git tools and the row files stay inside the workspace, because they act on the repository and on the run's own state.

Changed code, in the tree after this change:
- src/tools.mjs: `locate` (line 107) resolves a path with no workspace check. read_file (line 114), write_file (126), edit_file (135), stat_file (150) and list_dir (158) use it. `confine` (line 97) is unchanged and still serves the git, row, code-ast and search verbs, which stay inside the workspace.
- src/tools.mjs `planPath` (line 634): the `..` check and the inside check are removed, so an outside path is planned by its real path and dev:ino group, as an inside path is. Before, it returned null, and `access()` made the call a barrier (`{"barrier":true}`), so an outside read waited for every other call.
- src/review.mjs `fileAt` (line 21): review_packet's spec and references take absolute and outside paths. The refusal "is not a file in the workspace" (line 36) is now "is not a file".
- src/mcp.mjs (lines 130 to 134): the five file-tool descriptions say "inside or outside the workspace". This moves the tools-surface hash only (witness g).
- src/prompt.mjs (rule 1 of DEFAULT_RULES, line 5, and of MCP_RULES, line 18), skills/gm/SKILL.md (rule 1, line 16), AGENTS.md (lines 3 and 28), README.md (line 57) and docs/index.html (line 163): the rules text and the docs.

Rules text, the same in both rule-1 entries of src/prompt.mjs, in the skill, and in each new run's rules.md:
"Paths may point inside or outside the workspace. An outside path should be absolute."

Not changed, by decision:
- search, grep, callers and impact read only the git-tracked files of the workspace, and they take no path. A `path` argument is ignored, with no refusal and no event (witness f). query_log reads the run's log only.
- Body loads are still refused: a run_code `import` or `require` that resolves outside the project root (src/runcode.mjs `confined`, line 110, and the check at line 353; src/body-worker.mjs line 48). The brief scoped this batch to the file tools, so the loads were left. They are the one refusal the owner's direction still covers, and the rule-1 notice (src/mcp.mjs line 256, LEAVES_WORKSPACE and RULE_TEXT) still fires for them. Decision for the orchestrator or the owner.
- The git tools and the row files keep `confine`, so they stay inside the workspace.

## HMR step 1: classification and the update check (2026-11-03)

Plan: /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/hmr-plan.md, section 4.1 and step 1 of section 6. Owner decisions D2 (connect-time text stays reconnect-only) and D4 (a body thread is killed and logged at a kernel restart) are unchanged. Base HEAD 7142516. One batch, built in a tar of HEAD (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/hmr1/tree), witnessed on copies, applied to the checkout in one step.

Changes:
- src/worker-modules.json (new): the 29 modules the worker loads, every src/*.mjs except bench, cli, loop and models. tools/core.mjs reads it at each change, and tools/update.mjs reads it from the candidate clone.
- src/gate.mjs: a workers check in gm-new's own checks. workerModules(root) walks the import graph of src/mcp.mjs with acorn. It follows static imports, literal import(), module paths in string literals (new URL, register, the Worker entry), and the leaves loader import(`./${file}`), which adds RELOADABLE. A ./ computed import anywhere else is an error, because the graph cannot follow it. The check fails when the list and the graph differ. The gate now imports hot-config statically.
- tools/core.mjs: a change under src/ restarts or reloads the worker only for a module in the list. A debounce window with none of them writes no event and prints "not loaded by the worker, so nothing restarts" to stderr.
- tools/update.mjs: the restart decision uses the list at the target commit. The candidate clone runs the gate, and for a commit that changes src/ or tools/ it runs tools/core.mjs --probe and starts src/mcp.mjs, which must answer initialize within 20 s. A failing commit is refused before the checkout moves ("the checks failed on the new commit: ..."), so the running worker keeps serving. A pull that changes tools/ (not the relay) with the core digest unchanged no longer reports verified when replaceCore returns ok: it waits for the worker's report when the worker restarts, and reports verified false when it does not.
- Item 2, the smallest correct form: a combined pull (the core and a worker module changed) whose new core does not take over is reported with state unstarted, and it is not reset. Every other failed pull is still rolled back by a reset. Reason: the owner ruled out the reset for the combined case, and the candidate start check keeps the last good worker running for a worker that does not start. Not covered: a failure after the pull. The handover has already stopped the old worker, so that failure leaves the worker retrying the new code until a fix lands. This was not witnessed.
- AGENTS.md line 58 corrected: src/hot-config.mjs restarts, and a module the worker does not load restarts nothing. Two facts added. skills/dev/SKILL.md: the reloadable-leaf line corrected, and two process lessons (witness drivers, heredoc JSON).

The job shell 203813 and its sleep 203815 were alive by ps 17 s after the job started, after the second edit.   - b0, commit 4dfaa49 (tools/ship.sh only), run 206041-5850174: at 30860 ms "update: pulled 1 commit(s) to 4dfaa49; the checks passed", then "update: updated 1 commit(s); the worker did not restart: the changed files are served in place or are not loaded". Verified false, with no restart.

## Body access outside the workspace (2026-11-03)

Owner direction, the same as "Outside access allowed (2026-11-03)" above: all outside access should be allowed. That section changed the file tools (committed in 4160d0b) and left a body's loads refused ("Body loads are still refused"). This section changes the loads. Base HEAD decf5a5; the brief named 4160d0b, which HEAD had passed.

Reading: a body's own file access takes the same paths as the file tools. A literal `require()` or `import()` path, and a static `import` specifier, may be relative (taken from the project root, so `../x` reaches outside the workspace) or absolute, inside or outside it. A symlink that leads outside is followed. A non-literal `import()` or `require()` is still refused, because its path is not known until the body runs. The run's own files, the body file under the run directory, stay where they are.

Before the change, a tar copy of HEAD decf5a5, run 216185-5936068, driven over stdio by /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/body-access/drive.mjs, with the outside files in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/body-outside:
- read_file of an absolute outside path already returned the file (seq 7 code-call, seq 8 result). Not refused.
- require of an absolute outside .cjs already returned its value (seq 10). Not refused.
- import of an absolute outside .mjs already returned its value (seq 15). Not refused.
- require of `../../body-outside/outside.cjs` was refused (seq 12, stage load): "the require ../../body-outside/outside.cjs resolves outside the workspace".
- import of `../../body-outside/outside.mjs` was refused (seq 17): "the import ../../body-outside/outside.mjs resolves outside the workspace".
- require of `./link.cjs`, a workspace symlink to the outside file, was refused (seq 20): "the require ./link.cjs resolves outside the workspace".
- The tools event sha is 836ea50420d01153637b4f5a7d062d324428e8bd97d1c613b70b62b824b89932 (seq 2).

Changed lines (the copy's diff against HEAD):
- src/runcode.mjs, +21 -33: `confined` and its realpath check are removed, with the realpathSync import. `resolveLoad` replaces `confine` in splitBody. It resolves a path, records the load and never refuses. The relative (./, ../) and absolute literal specifiers are all resolved and logged; before, an absolute specifier was neither rewritten nor logged. A load is logged relative to the project when it is inside and in full when it is outside; before, `path.slice(project.length + 1)` cut an outside path into a wrong string. formatOf walks up to the filesystem root for a .js file outside the project, as Node does; before it stopped at the project root, so an outside .js was always cjs. The worker's refusal message and its branch are removed. The comments that described confinement are rewritten.
- src/body-worker.mjs, +1 -12: the import of `confined` is removed. requireBody passes every string to require. A relative spec resolves against the project root, as createRequire does, so the second confinement check is gone.
- AGENTS.md, Decided, the paths bullet: the sentence "A body's `require` or `import` of a path that leaves the project root is still refused." is replaced by one sentence, the rule.
- Not changed: the run_code tool description (src/runcode.mjs, runCodeTool) still says "Relative paths resolve against the project root and may not leave it." The default tools hash covers that description (src/mcp.mjs hashes the served list), and witness (d) requires 836ea50420d01153637b4f5a7d062d324428e8bd97d1c613b70b62b824b89932 to stay. The sentence is now false. Changing it moves the hash, so it is left for the orchestrator or the owner to decide. Also not changed: RULE_TEXT in src/mcp.mjs ("work only inside the workspace"). The rule-1 notice shows it when a git or row verb refuses a path, and those verbs still stay inside, so the notice still fits them.

Witness after the change, a tar copy of HEAD decf5a5 with the three changed files, a temporary workspace with a real task file, driven over stdio. The copy and the outside files are removed afterwards.
- Run 216992-5948448 (main): (a) read_file absolute outside, seq 8 result ok with the file's text. (b) require absolute outside, seq 10 code-load (absolute path, cjs), seq 11 result {"value":42,"kind":"cjs"}. (c) require `../../body-outside/outside.cjs`, seq 13 code-load, seq 14 result 42. (d) import absolute outside .mjs, seq 16 code-load, seq 17 result "mjs-outside-7". (e) import `../../body-outside/outside.mjs`, seq 19 code-load, seq 20 result "mjs-outside-7". (f) require `./link.cjs`, seq 22 code-load (path link.cjs), seq 23 result 42. No refusal event in the run. finish is seq 24, and end is seq 28. The tools sha is 836ea50420d01153637b4f5a7d062d324428e8bd97d1c613b70b62b824b89932 (seq 2).

The absolute import of the same file logs the same (seq 15, 16).

## next_context events cap (2026-11-03)

Problem. A next_context reply of 90,222 characters was saved by the host as over its token limit (events 86,717 characters; saved at /config/.claude/projects/-workspace-gm-new/a87073c7-2771-4162-9c22-f43b6830807d/tool-results/mcp-gm-next_context-1793687368409.txt). Another reply of 63,468 characters held 37 events in full, 59,967 bytes of events, and its step advanced anyway, so those events were never delivered. The cause: the feed sent the newest 20 new events in full, each truncated to 4,000 characters (compactEvent, EVENT_CAP), and 14 records for the seen events.

Rule, as built (src/compact.mjs, feedOf; the reply in src/mcp.mjs):
- NEXT_CONTEXT_EVENTS_CAP = 20000 bounds the serialized `events` array, brackets included. The array holds the seen records, the new events and the records or range after them.
- The new events come oldest first. In full means the event as logged, not truncated. The full part is the longest prefix that fits the cap with the rest of the new events as records after it. The first new event is in full when it fits. An event larger than the cap, or the first one that would take the array past the cap, and every later new event, is a record, the shape the older records have. No new event is dropped.
- If the records of the rest would not fit beside the full part, the rest is one range record, {kind: 'range', seqs: [from, to], count}. query_log reaches each event in it.
- The seen records (H1 = 14 and H2 = 12, unchanged) come after the new events, newest first, as far as the cap still has room. An older event is not in the feed, and `older` counts the events before the first one that is.
- Reply fields: `events`; `fullEvents`, how many new events are in full, from the oldest; `compactFrom`, the seq of the first new event that is not in full, or null; `query`, a text that names query_log, the seq range of the step's new events and how to read one by seq; `older`; `records`.
- The description of next_context in tools/list is unchanged, so the default tools hash is unchanged.
- Not capped, and not changed here: the other reply fields. In the witness step they came to 1,569 characters: pendingCommands 331, rules 674, witness 138, task 126, query 103, pendingFile 87, prefixSha 66, run 28, and the counters. jobsEnded (up to 20,000 characters of output for each ended job), pending, jobsOpen, recovery and the jobsKilled tails are not capped, so a reply can still pass the host limit through them.
- Docs: docs/index.html (step 1 of the feed) and skills/gm/SKILL.md (step 1) name the cap and the query field. README does not describe the feed's shape, so it is unchanged. AGENTS.md's Decided context line names the cap and the owner decision.

Witness. On a tar copy of HEAD d386ef4 with this change (/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/nextctx/tree), and on a tar of HEAD d386ef4 unchanged (base). Both are real workers over stdio (src/mcp.mjs --task task.md), each in its own temporary workspace. Driver /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/nextctx/witness.mjs, results in ws/witness-result.json beside it. Each run: next_context; seven run_code calls of about 8 KB each, the sixth of about 25.6 KB; next_context (step 2); a query_log cover of the step; 300 read_file calls in one run_code; next_context (step 3); a query_log cover; finish.
- Before (base): run 2026-11-03-06-44-18-8b34e1. The new events of step 2 (seqs 4 to 20) were 74,903 characters serialized. The step 2 reply was 33,563 characters, its events array 31,935 characters, 19 entries. The step 3 reply was 9,613 characters, its events array 7,982.
- After: run 2026-11-03-06-44-19-634ec9. Step 2: reply 19,136 characters, events array 17,368, fullEvents 5 (seqs 4 to 8), compactFrom 9, 19 entries (2 seen, 5 in full, 12 records), older 0. Step 3: reply 21,590 characters, events array 19,816, fullEvents 56 (seqs 21 to 76), one range record for seqs 77 to 331 (255 events), 57 entries.
- (a) pass: the events array is 17,368 characters in step 2 and 19,816 in step 3, both at most 20,000.
- (b) pass: fullEvents and compactFrom are right: seqs 4 to 8 are the full prefix and compactFrom is 9 in step 2; 56 full and compactFrom 77 in step 3.
- b2 pass: the next new event in full would take the array past the cap (checked on the array, with its record swapped for the full event).
- (c) pass: query_log by seq returned all 17 events of step 2 (seqs 4 to 20, five pages) and all 311 events of step 3 (seqs 21 to 331), the compact ones included. Seq 17 came back clipped to its head, see (d).
- (d) pass: seq 17, a run_code result of 20,517 characters, is a compact record in the step 2 feed, not dropped. query_log returns it clipped, because a query_log reply holds at most 20,000 characters after escaping.
- (e) pass: the range record of step 3 is inside the cap, and query_log reached every event it covers.
- (f) pass: tools/list has next_context, finish and run_code. The tools hash is 836ea50420d01153637b4f5a7d062d324428e8bd97d1c613b70b62b824b89932 before and after (tools event seq 2 in both runs).
- Gate on the copy (runGate, with the tracked list read from a scratch copy of the checkout's index): gate true, failed none. syntax 41 modules, ascii 86 files, docs 240 paths, sources 3, wiki none, skills 3, connect ec7180e23afa5292 (unchanged), workers 29, smoke finished and the negative control stopped at max_steps.

Open, for the owner (not decided here):
- In a step with more new events than the cap holds, the oldest are in full and the newest are one range record, so the newest events, the ones a debugger needs first, are reachable only by query_log. Newest-first, or records before full events, would change that. The brief asked for oldest first, so this is the rule as built.
- A query_log reply is 20,000 characters after escaping, so an event near the cap can be clipped when queried, while next_context sends a full event of up to 20,000 characters. Both limits stand.
- The next_context description in tools/list still says "the events new since the last step in full". It is accurate, but it does not name the cap, and changing it changes the tools hash for every host.

## Network access with no caps (2026-11-03)

Owner direction, 2026-11-03: outside access covers the network, and there is no cap on it. The network verbs are uncapped now: no size cap on a reply, no count on redirects, no protocol or host allow-list, and no truncation of a reply. Retries stay (they are not caps), and timeouts stay (listed below, for the owner to decide). The model is still the host session: no API call was added, and the no-API rule of 2026-11-01 stands.

Restrictions removed, with their file and line at HEAD 38bc532:
- src/fetch.mjs:61 and :78, a protocol allow-list: `isWeb` refused any scheme but http and https ("only http and https URLs are fetched", and "a redirect led to a URL that is not http or https"). Node's fetch now decides which schemes it serves (data: works; file: fails inside Node with "request failed: fetch failed").
- src/fetch.mjs:7-8 and :53, :127-148, a size cap on the reply: `maxBytes`, default 2000000, up to 20000000, and readCapped's cut at that many bytes. The body is read whole.
- src/fetch.mjs:11 and :93-95, the reply's text was cut at TEXT_CAP = 20000 characters, with a footer. The text is returned whole.
- src/fetch.mjs:9-10, :54, :75, a count cap on redirects: `maxRedirects`, default 5, up to 10. Redirects are followed with no count, and the one timeout bounds the whole call.
- src/fetch.mjs:24-25, the description said "public" and listed maxBytes and maxRedirects; it now says any URL, and the schema has `url` and `timeoutMs` only.
- src/sources.mjs:9 and :23, the archive cap SOURCE_CAP = 1048576: a fetched body was archived only up to 1 MB. It is archived whole now.
- src/git.mjs:14 and :99, a host allow-list: ORIGIN matched github.com only, and ci_status refused any other origin ("origin is not a GitHub remote"). ORIGIN now takes the host, owner and name from https, scp-like (git@host:owner/name) and ssh URLs, and ci_status passes the host to gh with --hostname.
- src/git.mjs:101-105, a reply cap: ci_status read one page of 20 runs (per_page=20). It now reads every page (`gh api --paginate --slurp`, per_page=100), and it merges the pages' workflow_runs.
- src/runcode.mjs (the run_code description's ten most-used verbs) listed fetch's maxBytes and maxRedirects; it lists `fetch({ url, timeoutMs? })` now. src/git.mjs:35, the ci_status description, says every page and origin's host.
- README.md:31 said "an HTTP GET of a public URL", and docs/index.html:155 and :158 listed the verbs with no statement of what they reach; they now say what the verbs reach.

Kept, and not a cap (open, for the owner):
- src/fetch.mjs, the content-type branch: HTML is reduced to text with script and style removed (a transform, not a cut), and a non-text content type returns "(binary content not shown: type)". A binary body is not returned as text. The archive stores the body as UTF-8 text (src/sources.mjs), so bytes that are not UTF-8 are lost there.
- src/exec.mjs:4 and :62, :102, OUTPUT_CAP = 200000 characters, applied by runCommand to every shell command's output, a curl through run_shell included. It bounds the memory of every command, so it is not changed here.
- src/retry.mjs, PERMANENT: the word "invalid" matches in any error text, so a transient error that names a host with "invalid" in it is not retried. Witness e below shows it (attempts 1).

Timeouts kept, with value and location (not changed; the owner decides):
- src/fetch.mjs:5-6, the fetch timeoutMs: default 15000 ms, at most 60000 ms (a larger value is refused). It covers the headers, the body and every redirect.
- src/git.mjs:6, GIT_TIMEOUT_MS = 120000 ms, for git_fetch, git_pull and git_push (network verbs).
- src/git.mjs:7, GH_TIMEOUT_MS = 60000 ms, for ci_status's gh call. Now that gh pages through every run, one call covers all pages, so a long history can exceed 60 s.
- src/git.mjs:8, CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS = 15000 ms, for local git checks (not network).
- tools/update.mjs:25, FETCH_MS = 60000 ms, the update check's git fetch of origin; :26, PULL_MS = 60000 ms.

Each run is the worker over stdio (node src/mcp.mjs --workspace ws --run-dir run --task file) with JSON-RPC from a witness driver in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/net (drive.mjs, scenarios a to e, prep.sh, run-all.sh). Scenario a's local server listens on 127.0.0.1 at a random port and is stopped after its run. The workspaces, the copies, the bare origin and the server are deleted after the witness.

Before, HEAD 38bc532:
- (a) run h-a-1793689180810556405: a 3,000,000-byte body from 127.0.0.1 came back with bytes 2000000, truncated true, and 20034 characters of text (the cut and its footer). seq 8 code-call, seq 14 end.

The reply archived as sources/0b37eeca70f8.txt, 2.9 MB.

- Gate on the copy: ok true, failed none. syntax 41 modules, ascii 86 files, docs 242 paths, sources (none archived in the tree), wiki none, skills 3, connect ec7180e23afa5292 (unchanged), workers 29 modules, smoke finished and the negative control stopped at max_steps. The copy has its own git index (git init and git add -A in the copy), so the checkout's index was not read.

Open, for the owner (not decided here):
- Timeouts, listed above: the owner decides whether the 60 s fetch and gh bounds, and the 120 s git bound, stay.

## Events cleared once delivered (2026-11-03)

Owner's direction, relayed by the coordinator: an event the agent sees, through next_context or through the output of any monitor (a watcher run as `events --wait`, a Monitor, a background command), is cleared, so it is never delivered to the agent again. The log is not changed: every event stays in `log.jsonl`, and query_log reaches it by seq.

Found first, at 38bc532, on a tar of HEAD (the same witness driver as below):
- next_context returned each queued event once and cleared it after its reply was written. Run `2026-11-03-07-01-44-68eeb4`: job events 6, 9, 12 (running) in the first step, none on a second call; exits 19, 22, 25 once; none after.
- `events --wait` printed the queued event, then removed the queue copy. Same run: rules notice 33 printed once, exit 0 in 654 ms, pending.jsonl gone. A second wait with a 1500 ms timeout printed nothing.
- `events --read-clear` printed what it cleared, then removed it. Run `2026-11-03-07-02-18-950dae`: printed 14, the file was gone, and the next next_context returned none.
- The gap: a passive `tail -n +1 -F pending.jsonl` (what a Monitor on the file runs) printed event 7 and did not clear it, so the next next_context delivered 7 again. Same run.
- The default tools hash at 38bc532 reproduces: 462f256b6ee7c52223971735badce28c7bcbec4255cf63ebfe8ebdc1c43e9722 (run `2026-11-03-07-01-44-5c77ef`, tools seq 2).

Decided:
- Delivery clears. A reader claims the queue by renaming pending.jsonl to a copy with its own name, and removes the copy after the records are written out. The rename is atomic: no reader sees a half-removed queue, and one reader gets a record.
- A temporary file with a rename over pending.jsonl was not used. Two readers that both read the queue before either rewrites it would both deliver the same event (a watcher and a held next_context). The claim-by-rename prevents that; witness (g) shows it.
- A crash between the write and the removal delivers the records once more; nothing is lost. A copy left by a crash is claimed first by the next reader.
- The clear is registered under the request's id and runs after that reply is written (afterReply). A next_context step that fails after its claim keeps its copy, so the next reader delivers it.
- pendingCommands.read is removed from the next_context reply and from the gm skill. Once delivery clears events, read-and-clear does what next_context already does, except that it delivers outside a step (no step is counted, and no rules are checked). No route needs that: a host with no background task holds the step with waitMs, and each step's next_context returns the queued events. The CLI flag `events --read-clear` stays for people and scripts, and still prints what it clears.
- pendingFile is removed from the reply. Its only use was the tail, which cannot clear.
- The skill says a Monitor runs the wait command and re-arms it after each event, and says not to tail the file. Open for the owner: a tail that a person or an agent runs by hand still shows events without clearing them (run `2026-11-03-07-05-44-22dfed`: the tail saw 7, and next_context delivered 7 again). A passive reader cannot be made to clear.
- The next_context description says delivery clears events, and no longer names the file. The default tools hash moves from 462f256b6ee7c52223971735badce28c7bcbec4255cf63ebfe8ebdc1c43e9722 to 28b6551c9e4c93f427107bd62c9325b13d0924d74952061fe09b32f808a45c2d (run `2026-11-03-07-05-44-1f08e1`, tools seq 2). The tools surface hash moves from 7cd72c2361acc1065a07a9e72c12ad92a851a4ca4aa1df067fdeaea5a4926573 to 844b36649892a500de8fbcb0f66627d72913d9710c6f5ae39b7c412e842bd2c9 (run `2026-11-03-07-05-44-c21e45`, tools seq 2).
- Rule 1 watchdog: the check and its label are removed, which closes the open item above. The check matched refused results whose text named the workspace, a link or "..". The rules now allow a path outside the workspace, so no such refusal is a breach. The file tools refuse only an empty path. A confined verb's refusal of a ".." path (git_add) returns ok:false without refused: true, so the check never saw it. The label is removed with the check, not kept as a dead entry.

Changed: src/pending.mjs (claim by rename, the copies and the queue), src/cli.mjs (events: print, then clear after the write; --wait polls every 200 ms and claims), src/mcp.mjs (next_context claims and registers the clear per request id; reply has pendingCommands.wait only; the description; the rule 1 check), skills/gm/SKILL.md (Pending events), AGENTS.md (the wake line and one fact). MCP_RULES in src/prompt.mjs is unchanged.

Witness, on a tar of HEAD with the change (`git archive`, node_modules linked), in a temporary workspace, the worker over stdio with `--surface tools`, driver `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/clear-witness/drive.mjs`; the copy and the workspace were deleted afterwards. Run `2026-11-03-07-05-44-c21e45`:
- (a) pass: three job_start calls; next_context returned job events 6, 9, 12 (running), each once.
- (b) pass: a second next_context at once returned none. After the exits, next_context returned 19, 22, 25 (exited), each once, and the step after returned none.
- (c) pass: a watcher (`events --wait`) was started in the background, then a finish with no witness was refused, which raised rules notice 33. The watcher printed 33 once and exited 0 in 655 ms, and pending.jsonl was gone. The next next_context returned none.
- (d) pass: a second watcher with a 1500 ms timeout printed nothing and exited 0 at 1656 ms.
- (g) pass, exactly once across readers: rules notice 42 went to a watcher while a next_context held with waitMs 15000 returned none.
- (f) pass: read_file of a file outside the workspace returned ok; git_add of ../ returned an error (ok false, with no refused flag); no rule 1 notice was raised, and the pending list was empty.
- (e) pass: query_log returned seqs 1 to 49 contiguous, with the jobs 6, 9, 12, 19, 22, 25 and the notices 33 and 42; the log on disk held 1 to 50 contiguous.
- The gate on the copy: gate true, failed none.
The same steps on HEAD (run `2026-11-03-07-01-44-68eeb4`) gave the same seqs for (a) to (e), so the change moves no delivery; it changes the reply fields and the rule 1 check.

Known limits, not closed here:
- An append that opened pending.jsonl before a claim and wrote after the claim's read is lost. The window is the gap between open and write in appendFileSync, a few microseconds. Closing it needs a lock shared by the worker and the CLI.
- A JSON line cut in half (a read that caught a half-written append) throws in takePending, as before. The copy stays for the next reader, which throws the same way, so next_context fails until the copy is removed by hand.

Rebased before the apply, onto a7afdd4 (the network-access commit). It changed runcode.mjs, so the default tools hash moved even without this change. The change was merged onto a7afdd4 (the four files it does not share with that commit are copied; AGENTS.md and DESIGN.md are merged, with no conflict), and the witness was run again on that tree:
- The default tools hash at a7afdd4 without the change is 23b998913a0bd9fd3886cd8cfa86f2c0bf10a1ffb6569130e3a4659fd4efd4b4 (run `2026-11-03-07-08-37-6b8c44`). With the change it is 692c1e7bc2dc7a3ac2302e44013e6e9cd7ba341ddfc91d0ba6b6b31643637aab (run `2026-11-03-07-08-53-126ee5`, tools seq 2). The tools surface hash with the change is b6b1e8cc3ad1522fe55632244f454e67bf6eb1e9c656a3d063d0eee46a2a08c9 (run `2026-11-03-07-08-53-0650da`, 42 tools).
- The steps of the witness on a7afdd4, with the change (run `2026-11-03-07-08-53-0650da`): (a) job events 6, 9, 12; (b) none; exits 19, 22, 25 once; (c) rules notice 33 printed once, exit 0 in 654 ms, queue gone, next_context none; (d) nothing, exit 0 at 1655 ms; (g) notice 42 to the watcher, none to next_context; (f) read of an outside file ok, git_add of ../ returned an error, no rule 1 notice; (e) query_log seqs 1 to 49 contiguous, the log on disk 1 to 50 contiguous.
- The raw tail probe on a7afdd4 with the change (run `2026-11-03-07-08-53-fd0a09`): the tail saw 7 and next_context delivered 7 again, the open gap above; events --read-clear printed 14 and cleared it, and the next next_context returned none.
- The gate on the a7afdd4 tree with the change: gate true, failed none.

## Retry by code and status (2026-11-03)

The defect: `src/retry.mjs` read a failure's text against a PERMANENT word list, and "invalid" was on it. A transient failure whose message named a host with "invalid" in it was final at attempt 1. Fixed: a failure is classified by its error code and its HTTP status. A message decides nothing that carries a code.

Decided:
- fetch: a failed call carries the code of its error (`err.cause.code`; a timeout is ETIMEDOUT). `codeType` retries it only when the code is in `TRANSIENT_CODES`: ECONNRESET, ECONNREFUSED, ECONNABORTED, ETIMEDOUT, EAI_AGAIN, UND_ERR_SOCKET, UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT, UND_ERR_BODY_TIMEOUT. A call with no code is final. An answer's status decides by `statusType`: 408, 429 and 5xx retry, and every other status is final, so a 404 is never retried.
- git_fetch, git_push, ci_status: git and gh print no code, so their message is matched for the transient words (`TRANSIENT_TEXT`, the words as before). Each URL and each dotted host name is removed from the message first, so a host name in it cannot decide the class. The PERMANENT list is removed: a message that names no transient word is final.
- Unchanged: 3 retries, backoff from 1 s doubling to 16 s, plus or minus 20%. Added: EAI_AGAIN (a fetch code, and "temporary failure in name resolution" in git's text), the DNS temporary failure the brief names as transient.
- 409 stays out of the network verbs, as before. The scripted loop (src/loop.mjs) retries it.

Changed: src/retry.mjs, and src/fetch.mjs (failureOf returns the code; classify uses codeType and statusType). src/git.mjs is unchanged and still calls errorTypeOf.

Witness, through the real path (the driver spawns src/mcp.mjs; the body calls gm.fetch or gm.git_fetch), on tar copies of HEAD 2571e55 (`git archive`), each in a temporary workspace with task.md and a git origin. invalid.example does not resolve in this sandbox, so the name goes to local servers. Node 24.22 fetch tunnels http:// targets through a proxy with CONNECT (`NODE_OPTIONS=--use-env-proxy`, HTTP_PROXY): server A resets the first CONNECT and answers the second with a body. git used `-c http.proxy` in the workspace config: server C resets every request. Server B answers 404 directly.
- HEAD, run `a-head2`: fetch, attempts 2 (retry seq 8, ECONNRESET, delayMs 906), body "retry body ok". The fetch path was not the defect: its failed call carries a code, not the host name, so HEAD retried it too.
- HEAD, run `b-head2`: fetch 404, attempts 1, one request to server B.
- HEAD, run `g-head2`: git_fetch, attempts 1, no retry event. The message was "fatal: unable to access 'http://invalid.example:9/repo.git/': Recv failure: Connection reset by peer". This is the defect.
- Fix, run `a-fix2`: retry seq 8 (ECONNRESET, delayMs 930), attempts 2, body returned. Server A: 2 CONNECTs, 1 tunnelled request.
- Fix, run `b-fix2`: fetch 404, attempts 1, one request.
- Fix, run `g-fix2`: retry seqs 8, 9, 10 (ECONNRESET, delayMs 1115, 2137, 3637), attempts 4, exhausted. Server C saw 4 requests.
- Gate on the fix copy: gate true, failed none; syntax, ascii, docs, sources, wiki, skills, connect, workers and smoke all true. The copy took the checkout's node_modules (acorn), because the workers check loads the modules.
- A first pass on a7afdd4 gave the same outcome for all six runs.

Known limits, not closed here:
- git and gh print no code, so their message is still read for words. A host name with no dot in it is not removed, so a permanent message that names such a host and also a transient word is retried.
- The fetch witness needs a proxy because invalid.example does not resolve here. A direct fetch to a real host with "invalid" in its name is not witnessed.

## Pending append and claim under one lock (2026-11-03)

The defect, reported on 2571e55: an event appended while a reader claims the queue can be lost, and a record can be delivered twice when two readers run at once.

The interleavings, on fd93c26 (the tree before the change):
- Lost append. (1) The writer's `appendFileSync` opens `pending.jsonl` (open, write, close); its descriptor is bound to the queue's inode. (2) A reader's claim renames `pending.jsonl` to its copy, which is the same inode under the copy's name. (3) The reader reads the copy, which does not hold the new line. (4) The writer writes the line into that inode, now the copy. (5) The reader removes the copy (clearPending), and the line goes with it. The window is the writer's open-to-write gap, a few microseconds; a writer preempted inside it widens it.
- Double delivery. (1) Reader A claims the queue into its copy and has not cleared it. (2) Reader B lists the directory, takes A's copy as the leftover of a crash, and renames it. (3) A then reads its copy, which throws ENOENT if B's rename came first (the CLI did this once in the stress and exited 1), or A has already delivered it and B delivers the same records again.

Decided:
- One queue lock, `runs/<id>/pending.lock`, created with open flag `wx`. An append holds it for its open, write and close of `pending.jsonl`; a claim holds it for its rename. A claim cannot rename the queue while an append writes to it, so each append is either in the queue before the claim (and read by it) or in the new queue after it.
- Why a lock, not a per-claim file. A writer that appends to a file its claimer owns still needs a rule for a claim that is live and one that is abandoned (the pid rule below), and it must find that file. The lock adds one create and one unlink per append and per claim, and holds nothing across a read or a reply, so it is the smallest change that closes the window.
- A copy is named `pending.<ms>-<hex>-<pid>.reading`. A reader takes another reader's copy only when that pid is gone (`process.kill(pid, 0)` throws ESRCH). A copy with no pid, from the earlier version, counts as gone, so an upgrade does not strand it.
- A waiter sleeps 1 ms (Atomics.wait) between tries. A lock older than 2 s is removed, because its holder died: a holder keeps the lock for microseconds. A waiter that has waited 5 s throws.
- next_context claims last, after its reply is built. Nothing that can throw runs between the claim and the registration of its clear, so a step that fails before that point claims nothing. On fd93c26 a failed step left a copy that any reader took; with the pid rule that copy would keep a live pid, so the claim moved.
- Unchanged: log.jsonl, log.index, query_log, the signatures of appendPending and takePending, the CLI, and the reply fields. `pending` is now the last key of the reply.
- This closes the gap that "Events cleared once delivered (2026-11-03)" lists under known limits.

Not closed:
- A reader that has exited but is not reaped by its parent is a zombie, and `kill(pid, 0)` still succeeds for it. Its copy is not taken until the parent reaps it. Shells and Node reap their children; a parent that never waits holds the copy until it exits.
- A crash between a reply's write and the removal of its copy delivers those records again. This is unchanged.
- A reply that cannot be written after its claim (an IO error in the log or on stdout) keeps its copy until that worker exits; the next worker takes it.
- Two waiters that break one stale lock in the same microseconds can both enter the queue. It needs a holder that died and two waiters at one instant. Not closed.

Witness. The copy is a tar of fd93c26 (`git archive`) with the change. It has node_modules copied in and its own repository (one commit), so the gate's checks run on it. The stress harness is in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/pending-fix (deleted after the run). Per round, a writer process appends 2000 events through `openLog().append('job')`; claimers run in their own processes: "hot" loops `takePending` and `clearPending`, "cli" loops `events --read-clear`. Twelve busy processes load the 12 cores. sent is the seqs the writer returned; delivered is the distinct sent ids delivered; duplicates is the extra deliveries; lost is a sent id neither delivered nor queued after the last pass. Base runs: before `2026-11-03-07-16-40-bac67d`, after `2026-11-03-07-19-02-9d6929`.
- (a) Before, fd93c26: one hot claimer, 10 rounds: sent 20000, delivered 19961, duplicates 0, lost 39 (3 rounds: 6000, 5987, 0, 13). One cli claimer, 3 rounds: sent 6000, delivered 6000, duplicates 0, lost 0. Hot and cli together, 10 rounds: sent 20000, delivered 19967, duplicates 47, lost 33 (3 rounds: 6000, 5990, 40, 10; one cli claim exited 1 with ENOENT on its copy).
- (b) After: one hot claimer, 10 rounds: sent 20000, delivered 20000, duplicates 0, lost 0 (3 rounds: 6000, 6000, 0, 0). One cli claimer, 3 rounds: 6000, 6000, 0, 0. Hot and cli together, 10 rounds: 20000, 20000, 0, 0 (3 rounds: 6000, 6000, 0, 0). No claimer error in any round.
- (c) The gate on the copy: gate true; syntax, ascii, docs, sources, wiki, skills, connect, workers and smoke all true.

Probes, through the real CLI and the worker:
- Reclaim. Before, the earlier code takes a copy whatever its reader: the first `events --read-clear` printed both leftover copies (7001 and 7002). After, a copy of an exited reader is taken first (7001); the copy of a live reader waits and is printed on the next pass (7002), after that process ended and was reaped. A copy with no pid is taken on the first pass.
- Lock. With a fresh lock held by another process, an append waited 2001 ms, went through, and removed the lock; the queue held the event. With a lock 60 s old, the append took 2 ms.
- next_context through the worker over stdio: a job's start and end (`job`, running and exited) came in the first call's `pending`; the second call had none; no queue or copy file was left in the run directory.
- The default tools hash is unchanged: 692c1e7bc2dc7a3ac2302e44013e6e9cd7ba341ddfc91d0ba6b6b31643637aab (tools seq 2 of the probe run), because no tool description changed.

Changed: src/pending.mjs (the queue lock, the pid in a copy's name, the reclaim rule), src/mcp.mjs (context claims last and sets `pending` on the reply), DESIGN.md, AGENTS.md (one fact), skills/dev/SKILL.md (one witness line).

The route to each is its resource, read with ReadMcpResourceTool (server gm), or its prompt, run by name: mcp__gm__gm and mcp__gm__gm-continue. Those prompt names are expected, not witnessed. The instructions also say that the host's Skill tool does not list either, so neither is called with it.

This reverses "Not changed, skills/gm/SKILL.md" in "gm-continue as an MCP prompt (2026-11-03)", which declined the same line; the owner now asks for it. The seven rules are unchanged.
- `AGENTS.md` (Continuation), `README.md`, `docs/index.html` (chapter 3), `skills/dev/SKILL.md` (line 3), and the DESIGN entries that name mcp__gm__gm or mcp__gm__gm-continue: each adds the resource route, read with ReadMcpResourceTool (server gm), and the sentence that the Skill tool does not list it. At apply time four workers ran from the checkout: self (2026-11-03-06-52-45-4d3dfc), oscraft (197518-5696774), hy3d (197547-5697339) and research (207329-5859625). No background job ran in any of them. Each run resumes from its log.

## events --wait exits with a reason (2026-11-03)

Problem. `events --dir <run dir> --wait` sometimes exited 0 with no output and no error while its worker was alive and the run open (self run `2026-11-03-06-52-45-4d3dfc`, last event seq 826 at 08:26:35 UTC). A session that waits on the watcher cannot tell an event from a watcher that quit, or a quit from a paused run. The watcher writes nothing to the run log, so the silent exit leaves no line anywhere.

Cause, reproduced on HEAD 46e3a5e (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/events-wait/drivers/probe.mjs`, scenario repro, `events-wait/repro/results-repro.json`). `events` has one exit path that prints nothing: the deadline. When the wait's timeout passes with no event, it returns 0. The default is 1800000 ms, and the wait command the skill gives does not set it, so a watcher ends silently 30 minutes after it starts. Probe: `--timeout-ms 1500` on an empty run dir exited 0 at 1661 ms with empty stdout and stderr. A SIGTERM had no handler, so the watcher died by the signal at 3008 ms with no line. The deadline was read from Date.now(), so a wall-clock step would also end the wait early, silently. The skill described the silent end as intended ("with no output when its timeout passes"), and the earlier witness of this path recorded it as the pass (DESIGN.md, "Pending events without a channel or a Monitor", the 1500 ms case).

Fix, in src/cli.mjs (`events`):
- A --wait returns only after it prints a line that says why: the queued events (the same JSON lines, cleared after the write as before), or `events: timed out after <ms> ms with no event pending` at the deadline (exit 0), or `events: stopped by <SIGINT|SIGTERM> with no event pending` on a signal (exit 130 or 143).
- The deadline is read from performance.now().
- The stale comments of src/cli.mjs (the replay note, the eventsDir and writeOut notes, the events note) are removed. The replay fact moved to AGENTS.md (Facts).
- skills/gm/SKILL.md, Pending events: the wait prints one line that names its timeout or signal, and an agent starts the wait again after it. AGENTS.md has the fact line.
- --read-clear is unchanged, and src/pending.mjs is unchanged.

Restarts: src/cli.mjs is not in src/worker-modules.json, so applying it restarts no worker (tools/core.mjs, loadedByWorker). The skill applies in place. A watcher already running keeps the code it started with, and so keeps the silent deadline until it is started again.

Witness, on a tar copy of 46e3a5e in its own repository, with node_modules copied in (`/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/events-wait/copy`; the copy and the temporary workspace were deleted after the run). Driver `drivers/probe.mjs`, scenario witness, results in `events-wait/witness-1/results-witness.json`.
- (a) no event: a watcher on an empty run dir was alive at 3 s with empty stdout. A watcher on the open run of a live worker (`events-wait/witness-1/runs/paused`, worker alive, no event pending) was alive at 3 s with empty stdout.
- timeout: `--timeout-ms 1500` printed `events: timed out after 1500 ms with no event pending` and exited 0 at 1682 ms.
- signal: SIGTERM at 3 s printed `events: stopped by SIGTERM with no event pending` and exited 143 at 3080 ms.
- (b) an event: a worker with `--host-idle-ms 1000` (`witness-1/runs/host-idle`) logged host-idle at seq 3 (idleMs 1002). The watcher printed that record and exited 0 at 1070 ms.
- (c) a pause: SIGTERM to the open worker (`witness-1/runs/paused`) logged paused at seq 3 (reason "SIGTERM from the supervisor"). The watcher printed that record and exited 0 at 3062 ms, and the worker exited 0.
- (d) runGate on the copy with this change and this section: gate true, failed none (`events-wait/gate-final.out`). The unchanged copy passed the same gate (`events-wait/gate-baseline.out`).

Limits: on Windows a SIGTERM is not delivered to a Node listener, so a watcher ended there by a kill prints no line; not witnessed on Windows. `--read-clear` on an empty queue still prints nothing, which is its answer. The full 1800000 ms default was not waited out; the 1500 ms run takes the same path. The event lines are unchanged (JSON, with id and seq); the reason line is not an event and has no JSON form.

## gm-continue from a freddie-attached session (2026-11-03)

Run 11347-6820153 ran gm-continue on a gm server whose workspace is /workspace/freddie. Its setup, its row verbs and its git verbs read freddie, so the run saw no gm-new rows, and nothing reported it.

- Path. The skill names `tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs` with no repository. From freddie that file is missing: list_dir on tools returned ENOENT: no such file or directory, scandir '/workspace/freddie/tools'.
- Silent wrong checkout. The setup defaults `--repo` to the process cwd. `cd /workspace/freddie && node /workspace/gm-new/tools/gm-continue-setup.mjs` printed `HEAD ebcf0378; 0 commit(s) ahead of origin`, `Batch plan: 0 open row(s)` and `Open runs: 1`, with no defect line. Run from /workspace/gm-new, the same script printed `HEAD 50a9ecf` and the nine defect rows.
- Row verbs. `gm.prd_list` and `gm.unknown_list` returned `(none)` for the statuses open, resolved and all. gm-new holds 11 resolved PRDs and 13 open questions (4 resolved, 9 blocked). The verbs read the rows directory under the workspace, and freddie has none: list_dir on work returned ENOENT: no such file or directory, scandir '/workspace/freddie/work'. A write verb from this session would create rows in freddie, so none was called.
- Not added here: the row that step 3 of gm-continue asks for. It must be added with `gm.prd_add` from a session whose workspace is gm-new.

Decision, the owner's. No session is named gm in ListAgents (peers: gm-4b, oscraft-6b, hy3d-12), so INVARIANT 2 leaves it to the owner. Two options: run gm-continue only from a session whose workspace is gm-new, with the skill saying so; or give the row verbs and the setup an explicit repository. Either way the setup should take its default from its own checkout, not from the cwd.

The nine blocked rows are unchanged; see "Rows without a hold". Commit 671cbb0 removed the blocked state and migrated none of their files: `git show --stat 671cbb0` lists seven files, and among them only the deleted prd-adaptive-codemode.yml is a row under the rows directory.

Witness: run 11347-6820153, its setup, list and list_dir calls in this session, and `git show --stat 671cbb0` in /workspace/gm-new.

Owner decision: no session is named exactly `gm` in ListAgents (peers were gm-4b, hy3d-12, freddie-09), so INVARIANT 2 leaves these to the owner. Recorded here, not changed.

- `gm.read_file` offset and limit count characters, not lines. Verified: `read_file({ path: 'AGENTS.md', limit: 10 })` returned 10 characters (`# Agent in`). The verb and the Read tool both read as line-based.
