## Peer report: grep without git and read_file lines (2026-11-03)

Peer pw-a1 reported two faults (run 24133-6845321). Both are fixed in commit f6cc36ad446264e1840d0ce7c85530b5431f0eaf, which changes src/search.mjs and src/tools.mjs.

- grep outside git: when `git ls-files` fails, grep walks the workspace root and its reply carries `files: "walk"`. A git repository still lists its tracked files and reports `files: "git"`. The walk never enters `.git`, `runs` or any dot-directory, and enters `node_modules` and `vendor` only when `vendor` is true. It does not follow symbolic links, and each file is read under the search limits: text only, at most 1 MB, no NUL in the first 8000 bytes. search and wiki_search still refuse a non-git workspace; the walk is not applied to them.
- read_file counts lines by default: offset is a 0-based line index, limit a line count (left out, a page takes as many lines as fit), total the line count, and next the next line index or null. A page holds at most 20000 characters. A line longer than 20000 characters is cut to its first 20000 characters, the reply carries `cutLine: true`, and next is the line after it. `unit: "chars"` restores the character paging exactly as before, with offset, limit, total and next counted in characters.
- Superseded: the "Output pages" item in "## Decided" (brief change 3, gm agent 2026-11-01), for read_file's unit. That item stays as the record of the decision. The read_file lines in AGENTS.md ("Facts found by troubleshooting") and in the oscraft friction section above are out of date.

Witnesses: scratch tree of f6cc36a, driver /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/peer-fix/driver/drive.mjs, output and logs under /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/peer-fix/witness-1793698244458/run1.
- Run nongit-tools (non-git workspace, tools surface): grep at seq 6 found 3 matches, all in app/index.js and lib/util.js, with files walk. node_modules, vendor, .hidden, runs, a file with a NUL byte, a 1.17 MB file and a symbolic link were not matched. A vendor grep at seq 8 found vendor/vend.js. read_file default lines at seq 10 (total 300, next 130). unit chars at seq 12 (50 characters, total 6600, next 130). A cut line at seq 18 (cutLine true, 20000 characters, next 2). Errors at seq 32 and 34. Empty, no-final-newline and CRLF files at seq 22, 24 and 26.
- Run git-tools (git repository, tools surface): grep at seq 6 with files git returned only the tracked app.js, not the untracked file.
- Run nongit-code (code surface): run_code at seq 10 called gm.grep (files walk) and both read_file forms.

Resolved: the read_file description in src/mcp.mjs (BASE_TOOLS) now says lines are the default and lists unit, in commit b2ad777.

## MCP client in run_code (2026-11-03)

Owner direction: gm-new's codemode adds any MCP tools natively. Built in commit 6b31b32 (src/mcp-client.mjs, src/mcp.mjs, src/worker-modules.json, README.md, skills/gm/SKILL.md).

- Config: `.gm-new/mcp.json` in the workspace, or the file that `--mcp-config` names, in the `mcpServers` shape of `.mcp.json`. The project's `.mcp.json` is not read: it registers gm-new's own server, and reading it would connect gm-new to itself.
- Transport: stdio only. A server whose `type` is not stdio, or that has no `command`, is a logged config problem. HTTP servers are not built: a witness needs a real HTTP server, and gm-new opens no listener.
- Names: `gm.<server>_<tool>`, each character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` replaced by `_`, and `mcp_` in front of a name that starts with a digit. A name taken by a native verb or by another MCP tool is skipped and logged as `mcp-tool-skipped`. Reason: the code surface's verb list is flat, so a native verb keeps its name.
- Surface: codemode only, meaning run_code, `gm.verbs()` and run_code's description. `--surface tools` does not serve MCP tools. Reason: the request was for codemode.
- Startup: every server starts with the worker, in parallel. tools/list and each request except initialize and ping wait for them, each for at most `startupTimeoutMs` (default 30000). Reason: the verb list is part of the stable prefix, so it must not change after a step has started.
- Calls: each call is an `action` and a `code-call` event, so recovery reads it as a side effect. A call past `timeoutMs` (default 60000) fails with that message, and the server is sent `notifications/cancelled`.
- Failure and restart: a server that fails to start is logged with its error and the tail of its stderr, and the others still run. A server that exits restarts on its next call, and concurrent calls to a down server share one restart.
- Environment: `${NAME}` in `command`, `args`, `env` and `cwd` takes the variable NAME. A missing variable skips that server, and the log names the variable.
- Depth: a worker started as an MCP server reads its workspace's config too. Its `GMNEW_MCP_DEPTH` is one more than its parent's, and servers start only below depth 2. Before this limit, a config that named gm-new itself grew a chain of workers that never ended: 270 processes were found running and were killed by PID.
- Stop: the worker stops its servers when its input ends and when it pauses, not only at process exit. A live child's pipes keep the event loop open, so the exit handler never ran after input ended, and the worker waited on its child until the driver's timeout.

Witnesses, on a tar copy of the tree (drivers in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/mcp-client/driver, runs in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/mcp-client/ws/runs):

- Run outer2, recovery, concurrency and timeout: a non-string `cwd` at seq 2 was a config problem and the worker still ran; the inner server connected with 42 tools at seq 3; a SIGKILL of the server, found by pid, went down at seq 11; two concurrent run_code calls both succeeded after it, with one `restarted` event at seq 14; a `run_shell` of `sleep 3` through the server failed with `tools/call timed out after 1500 ms`, and the server's run logged `cancel` for that request; the worker exited 0 and left no child.
- Run outer4, pause: a SIGTERM from the supervisor wrote `paused` at seq 11, the worker exited 0, and no inner server remained.
- Basic path, driver output `driver/out.txt` (its run was replaced by a later run): 42 `inner_` verbs were listed in run_code's description, `gm.inner_read_file` and `gm.inner_list_dir` returned the file and the listing, a missing file came back as data, and the `broken` server (ENOENT) and the `unset` server (a missing variable) were logged and skipped. The worker exited 0.
- Depth, driver output `driver/out3.txt` (its run was replaced): a config that named gm-new itself with the default config stopped at depth 2, and the depth-2 worker logged `servers are not started at MCP depth 2`. The chain ended with stdin.

Limits: stdio only; `notifications/tools/list_changed` is not handled, so a tool added by a running server appears after its next start; Windows is not witnessed, and a bare command such as `npx` needs its `.cmd` form there; a worker killed with SIGKILL leaves its children to see EOF on their stdin.

## Two gm friction fixes (2026-11-03)

Session prompt (defect 1). A `/mcp__gm__gm` command with several words recorded its task as its first word, "we". gm-new keeps every word it is sent: `prompts/get` reads `arguments.task` whole (src/mcp.mjs). The host drops the rest. Claude Code 2.1.301 splits the command on whitespace and binds the words by position to the declared arguments (its code: `j.split(/\s+/)`), and the gm prompt declares one. A fix needs a host change or a new surface verb, which is the owner's decision and was not made here. The commit gate has a `prompt` check (src/gate.mjs) that sends a 15-word task through the real server and requires the task event to hold all of it. Negative control: with the task cut to its first word in a copy, the check failed with task ["we"]. Commit 37208d7.

read_file (defect 2). offset and limit counted characters, so a caller that assumed lines got a silently wrong slice. Lines are now the default (the handler in src/tools.mjs, commit f6cc36a), and `unit: "chars"` keeps character paging for callers that need it. The served description and schema (src/mcp.mjs) now say so, in commit 03b6ffa. Lines match the host Read tool. No caller in gm-new or the skill files passes offset or limit. job_status's offset still counts characters, as its description says. Witness: offset 401 and limit 72 returned lines 402 to 473, with next 473 and total 500. unit "chars" returned 72 characters from character 401 (total 4392).

## Task carried into a new run after a closed run (2026-11-03)

Answers thorns-35 items 3 and 4. Item 4: a run closed by `finish`, then reopened by a worker start that names it, opened with task null. Cause, in `src/mcp.mjs`: the closed-run branch of `main()` built the new run's meta from the `task` variable, which is '' when the session sends its task through the prompt, and the closed run's task was never read. Item 3: a child agent sent `finish` on its parent's connection and closed the parent's run.

Decided:
- A start whose run directory is a closed run still opens a new run beside it. The new run takes the closed run's task when the start has no `--task`: the text of the closed run's last `task` event (a session prompt writes it), or else the `task` field of its `task.json` (`--task` writes it). Its `task.json` holds that task, and its log gets a `task` event with from "closed run <id>" right after its `start` event, whose `taskFrom` names the same closed run.
- With `--task`, nothing is inherited. The flag is the operator's explicit task. This was decided here, not asked.
- A session prompt that gives a task replaces an inherited task, logged as a `task` event with from "session prompt", as before. A prompt with no task keeps the inherited one.
- A resumed run that is still open keeps its own task from its history, and a prompt does not replace it. Its code path is unchanged; its history now holds the inherited task event.
- A fresh run with no closed run behind it and no task keeps task null and the taskNotice.
- Item 3: `skills/gm/SKILL.md` has one plain paragraph in its Rules section, after the seven bullets: a subagent never calls `finish` and never closes a run, and it reports its result to the session that started it. The seven rules and MCP_RULES in `src/prompt.mjs` are unchanged. Step 6 of the skill already said a child on a shared connection must not send `finish`; the new paragraph is where a reader meets it first.

Witness, through the real stdio path: a driver spawns `src/mcp.mjs` and speaks JSON-RPC to it. The tree is a tar copy of 9ad3e7a with this change, and the workspace is a scratch git repository. Driver `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/task-carry/driver/drive.mjs`, output `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/task-carry/out-after.json`, logs under `ws/runs/` of the same directory.
- a, run `first`: start seq 1 (taskFrom session prompt), task seq 4 ("first task text", session prompt), next_context result seq 6 with task "first task text", run_code action seq 7 (returned 2), end seq 14 (finished, witness "done"), finish ok.
- b, run `2026-11-03-09-50-21-1633f3`, started on `first` with no prompt task: start seq 1 (taskFrom closed run first), task seq 2 (from closed run first, "first task text"), next_context result seq 6 with task "first task text".
- c, run `2026-11-03-09-50-22-e0c461`, started on `first` with prompt "second task text": start seq 1, task seq 2 (from closed run first, "first task text"), task seq 5 (from session prompt, "second task text"), next_context result seq 7 with task "second task text".
- d, the open run from b, started with prompt "third task text": restart seq 8, no task event, next_context result seq 12 with task "first task text", so the prompt did not replace it.
- e, run `2026-11-03-09-50-22-0dfc4a`, default run directory, no prompt task: start seq 1, next_context result seq 5 with task null and the taskNotice.
- Before, on an untouched copy of 9ad3e7a: run `2026-11-03-09-49-50-673a6d` (started on a closed run) had next_context result seq 5 with task null, which is the defect; run `2026-11-03-09-49-50-2e6086` had task seq 4 "second task text"; run `2026-11-03-09-49-50-17c614` (default directory) had task null.

Limits, not closed here:
- A default run directory opens a fresh run and never reaches the closed-run branch, so the reopen path is witnessed with the run directory named.
- The witness runs the worker directly. A live host session that reconnects, or a core that respawns a worker on a finished run, is not witnessed here.
- A child on a parent's connection can still send `finish`; the Rules paragraph is the only guard until the owner chooses between the options under "Finish, the connection, and restarts".

## Fresh run on the first next_context after finish (2026-11-03)

Friction report research-e5: after `finish`, every later call on the same connection was refused, so a host with one long-lived connection needed a restart. 209d5e0 fixed that by rotating on the first tools/call after finish, which let a run_code sent before next_context roll the run over: a new run id, actionSeq back at 1, a rules notice raised again, and seqs cited from the old run refused by prd-resolve. 05c4791 carries the fix below: the rotation happens only on the first next_context.

Decided:
- Every call on an ended run is refused with "run <id> has ended; call next_context to open a new run". The run id does not change, the refusal is logged on the ended run, and it raises no rules notice (callTool skips rule 2 once the run has ended).
- The first next_context on the same connection opens the fresh run: a new run directory from `newRunId()`, a new log, steps from 0, and a start event with taskFrom. The step is then served, so the reply carries the new run id. `acceptLine` in `src/mcp.mjs` makes the swap, and `reopen` builds the run.
- The fresh run takes the ended run's task. A gm prompt with a task, sent after the end, is held for the fresh run and replaces the inherited task, and it writes no task event into the ended log. taskFrom is "closed run <id>", "session prompt", or "task file" (a boot with --task).
- A run that is closed when the process starts stays the current run, with its calls refused as above. Its log gets no start, restart, tools or recovery event. This supersedes the start-time rule of "Task carried into a new run after a closed run (2026-11-03)": the next_context opens the run, not the start. The task rule there is unchanged.
- A run ended by max_steps is not rotated: its next_context is refused like any other call, and a restart opens the fresh run (see Limits).

Witness, on origin's code (05c4791, working tree clean): driver `/tmp/gm-scratch/fresh-run/drive-next.mjs`, base `/tmp/gm-scratch/fresh-run/next-2026-11-03T10-14-06-492Z`. All 29 checks pass.
- Run A (task "rotation witness task"): next_context ok, run_code output 1, finish ok with witness "w1".
- After the finish, run_code is refused with run A: "refused: run A has ended; call next_context to open a new run", and no rulesNotice. A later finish is refused the same way.
- next_context opens run `2026-11-03-10-14-06-2f422c` at step 1 with the inherited task. run_code output 2 names that run in `_meta`, and finish is ok.
- Boot on that closed run: run_code is refused with its id, and no run directory is opened. next_context opens `2026-11-03-10-14-06-faff8d`, taskFrom "closed run" with the closed run's id.
- A gm prompt task "prompt witness task" sent before next_context: run `2026-11-03-10-14-06-ab2547` takes it, taskFrom "session prompt".
- Boot with --task "boot flag task": run `2026-11-03-10-14-07-c9d887`, taskFrom "task file".
- The process exits 0 when stdin closes.

Limits, not closed here:
- Only the first next_context opens the run. The driver checks run_code and finish. Other tools/call names take the same path, because the refusal depends on the ended state, not on the name.
- max_steps: a run that reached its step limit is not rotated; its calls stay refused with "step limit N reached; a restart of the server opens a new run" until a restart. Before this change, the first next_context after that refusal opened a run with a new budget, so a loop could repeat the refusal and bypass the limit.
- A gm prompt after the end still logs its skill-loaded and rules-cleared events on the ended run, after its end event, as before.
- A repeated request id is answered from the ended run only. Sent again after the next_context, it runs again.
- A child agent on the parent's connection that sends `finish` still ends the parent's run. The parent's next_context opens a fresh run; its open rows are workspace files and stay, but its log and steps do not carry over.
- Whether runs are shared across connections stays with the owner (options a to c under "Finish, the connection, and restarts").

## query_log limit argument (2026-11-03)

Agents read `gm.query_log` as reaching every event and tried `limit` to page through them. It was refused as an unknown argument. Code: the `query_log` branch and `pageOf` in `src/tools.mjs`, and the entry in `BASE_TOOLS` in `src/mcp.mjs`.

Decided:
- `limit` is an integer from 1 to 50. Without `js` or `last` it caps the events one reply returns. The default stays 50 events and about 20 KB, and the 20 KB cap still applies, so a reply can hold fewer events than `limit` when events are large. `next` is still the seq to pass back as `after`, and it is set when events remain. Without `after` the read starts at seq 0.
- `limit` with `last` is refused, because `last` already sets the count.
- `limit` with `js` is refused, not ignored. A js expression sees the whole window of matching events, and a silent limit would make its count look like the whole window.
- A `limit` outside 1 to 50 gets the error `last` gets. `limit` is listed in the unknown-argument reply.

Witness: stdio driver `/tmp/gm-scratch/query-limit/drive.mjs` against `src/mcp.mjs` on the working tree, run directory `/tmp/gm-scratch/query-limit/runs/A`. Replies from `run_code` bodies:
- `{after: 0, limit: 3}`: events seqs 1, 2, 3, next 3.
- `{after: 3, limit: 3}`: events seqs 4, 5, 6, next 6.
- `{last: 2, limit: 1}`: refused, "limit cannot be used with last, which already sets how many events a reply returns".
- `{js: 'events.length', limit: 3}`: refused, "limit cannot be used with js, whose expression sees the whole window of matching events".
- `{after: 0, limit: 51}`: "limit must be a whole number from 1 to 50".
- `{after: 0}` with no limit: 27 events, seqs 1 to 27, no next.
- `{bogus: 1}`: refused, and the list of arguments ends "after, last, limit".

Limits, not closed here:
- Only the seq form with no `kind`, `fromMono` or `toMono` is witnessed. Those filters pass through the same page, but no reply with one of them was run with `limit`.
- The witness runs the worker directly over stdio. A live session serves the new description and schema after its worker reloads.

## session.finished crash in mcp.mjs (2026-11-03)

What broke: the gm MCP worker exited with code 1 before the handshake, `TypeError: session.finished is not a function` at `src/mcp.mjs:1058` (run2800-6805174 in /workspace/oscraft). The line listener called `session.finished()` on every line. No commit pairs that call site with a createSession that lacks `finished`, so the crashing copy was a mix. Only mcp.mjs ever held `.finished(` (commits 209d5e0 and 05c4791).

What changed: origin main at 05c4791 calls `session.ended() !== null && isNextContextCall(line)`, which matches the `ended()` that createSession returns. This entry records that fix; no source changed in this commit.

Witness at 05c4791, in temporary workspaces removed after the run: `initialize` (protocol 2025-11-25) and tools/list (next_context, finish, run_code) answered, exit 0 on stdin close. On one connection, next_context, finish, next_context ended run 2026-11-03-10-14-09-533fbb and opened run 2026-11-03-10-14-09-87d951, exit 0.

Line listener guard (2026-11-03): a throw in the line listener, such as a reopen whose run directory cannot be created, exited the worker with code 1 (witnessed: runs/ replaced by a file, `ENOTDIR` at src/run.mjs createRun via reopen); acceptLine now answers that request with -32603 and keeps the worker up, and reopen moves runDir only after its run opens. Witness on a tar copy of the tree: runs/ made read-only, next_context got `EACCES` as -32603, a run_code then was refused as ended, and restoring runs/ let next_context open a run; restart by SIGTERM and finish-then-reopen still answered; a bare `return {ok:1}` and an un-awaited gm.grep both returned JSON with the worker alive.

## grep index refresh (2026-11-03)

Cause: `search` and `wiki_search` reused a file's lines while its mtime and size were unchanged, and the tracked listing while the `.git/index` mtime and size were unchanged. Before this change (run B, on 8cad2bc), a same-size edit whose mtime was restored made `search` return the overwritten line and miss the new text, and a new untracked wiki page was missing from `wiki_search`. `grep` was not stale on this checkout: since ae3c7b6 (2026-11-02) it reads every tracked file from disk on each call. The freddie report (old packages/gm text in pnpm-lock.yaml and a README) matches the grep before ae3c7b6, which served lines from the same mtime and size cache. The build freddie ran was not checked.

Decision: a file's stamp is device, inode, size, mtime and ctime, read by one lstat per call. ctime is set by the kernel on every write, so a restored mtime still changes the stamp. A cached entry is reused only when its stamp is unchanged and settled, meaning it was read at least 3 s after its mtime and ctime. That covers the coarse timestamp steps (FAT 2 s, NTFS about 16 ms), so a write in the same tick cannot be missed. An unsettled or changed stamp re-reads the file, and the sha1 of its content decides whether its lines and the index change. Deleted and untracked paths drop out, because each listing keeps only the paths it names. The tracked listing is cached under the `.git/index` stamp with the same rule. The wiki listing is not cached, so `wiki_search` runs `git ls-files` on each call. `grep` is unchanged and reads each file fresh.

Witness: `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-stale/drive.mjs`, run as `node drive.mjs B` (before) and `node drive.mjs A` (after), on a new scratch repository, over stdio JSON-RPC to `src/mcp.mjs`. The edits are plain fs writes made by the driver.
- grep: correct at every step in both runs (ALPHA, BRAVO, DELTA, ONLYB after git rm). A file created but not committed is not found, so grep covers tracked files only.
- search after the same-size edit with mtime restored: before, `search BRAVO` returned the old line and `search DELTA` found nothing; after, `search DELTA` returns a.txt:2 and `search BRAVO` finds nothing.
- wiki_search for wiki/p1.md created after the first call: before, no match; after, wiki/p1.md:1.
- Cost: warm `search` calls take 3 to 6 ms CPU before and after the change (`/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-stale/bench.mjs`).

Limits: the stamp is not a content hash, so a change that keeps size, mtime and ctime cannot be seen; the witness ran on Linux only, and macOS and Windows are not witnessed. The search set is the tracked files, so an untracked file outside wiki/ is not indexed. A workspace that is not a git repository is unchanged: `search` refuses and `grep` walks.

## grep path outside the workspace (2026-11-03)

Cause: `grep` in `src/search.mjs` took no `path`. Its argument destructuring dropped any `path` a caller sent, so the search always covered the workspace. Before the change, `grep({ pattern: 'export', path: '/workspace/Gemini-OAuth-Flow-Boilerplate/src' })` returned gm-new's own files (`DESIGN.md:49`), and `path: 'src'` returned `AGENTS.md:12`.

Decision: `grep` takes `path`, a file or a directory, inside or outside the workspace. With no path it searches the workspace as before. A path inside searches the git-tracked files under it (every file under it when the workspace is not a git repository); a path outside is walked with the walk's skips (dot-directories, `runs/`, and `node_modules/` or `vendor/` unless `vendor` is true), and its hits show absolute paths. A missing path is refused with `no such path`. A path is not confined, the same as the file tools' `locate`.

Witness: `createTools` from `src/tools.mjs` in node, on the final code, workspace `/workspace/gm-new`. An outside directory returned `/workspace/Gemini-OAuth-Flow-Boilerplate/src/config.ts:11 export interface ConfigOptions {` and `files: walk`; an outside file returned `/workspace/Gemini-OAuth-Flow-Boilerplate/src/pkce.ts:11`; `path: 'src'` returned `src/search.mjs:64` and `src/tools.mjs:9`, `files: git`; `path: 'no/such/dir'` returned `no such path`. A temporary non-git workspace, removed after the run, gave `sub/a.txt:1` for a walk and `vendor/v.txt:1` only with `vendor: true`.

Limits: the witness ran in node on Linux, not through a gm-new worker session; macOS and Windows are not witnessed. The gm server in the session that asked for this serves `/workspace/acptoapi`, so it could not search this repository.

## Run_code after rotation reached the ended session (2026-11-03)

What broke: on one live connection, run 350381-7621611 had finished. next_context opened run 2026-11-03-11-32-35-891e49, and the next run_code was refused as `run 350381-7621611 has ended` (actionSeq 22 of the old run). A second next_context then opened run 2026-11-03-11-32-55-6ae7aa, and its run_code ran.

Cause: `tools/core.mjs` launches each worker on the run directory it chose at start (`runDir` in `begin`). A worker rotates to a new run inside its own process (`reopen` in `src/mcp.mjs`), and the core never learns of it. Run 2026-11-03-11-32-35-891e49 logged `paused` (SIGTERM from the supervisor) at 11:32:35.559Z, 129 ms after the rotation. The checkout had uncommitted edits under `src/` and `tools/` at that time, and the core's stderr was not captured, so the trigger is not named. The replacement worker booted on the closed run 350381-7621611, so its session started ended under that run's id and refused the first call that was not next_context. The closed run's log holds one `mcp-server connected` event per worker boot between 11:32:25 and 11:32:37, and the refusal at seq 23. Each worker logged one pause, and the boots did not overlap, so no two sessions shared one connection.

Fix: `reopen` writes the new run's name into the closed run's `successor` file (`setSuccessor`, atomic rename, `src/run.mjs`). `currentRunDir` follows that chain, and a worker resolves its run with it at boot. The core's busy check (`busyReason`, `tools/core.mjs`) reads the same run's jobs. The core still launches with its first run directory, and the chain resolves it on each boot.

Witness: stdio drivers in `/tmp/gm-scratch/rotation-race/drive.mjs`, outside the repo. Relay runs use `tools/relay.mjs --workspace /tmp/gm-scratch/rotation-race/ws --run-dir /tmp/gm-scratch/rotation-race/runs/relay/A`; a SIGTERM from the driver to the worker stands for a core restart.
- Before the fix, `relay sigterm`: next_context rotated to 2026-11-03-11-39-05-7f855f, and run_code then was refused as `refused: run A has ended; call next_context to open a new run` (replyRun A). Relay stderr: `restarting the worker (the worker paused itself)`, then `run A (finished) is closed`.
- After the fix, `relay sigterm`: run_code output "1" in run 2026-11-03-11-40-25-b76805. `relay chain` (two rotations, a restart after the second finish): run_code output "2" in run 2026-11-03-11-40-37-814f44. `relay nosig`: all calls ok in 2026-11-03-11-40-44-3a6958. `relay same-write-restart` (next_context and run_code in one write after a restart): both ok in 2026-11-03-11-41-28-a66a31. `relay sigterm-now`: the call in flight at the pause gets the existing pause error, and the next call is served by the run.
- Direct worker, no core: `seq`, `same-write` and `back-to-back` as before the fix; a run_code sent before the next_context of the step is refused by its own run, as designed.

Not reproduced: a rotation with no restart, and two workers answering one connection. Open: `restart()` in `tools/core.mjs` flushes held lines after `announceChanges()` has awaited, so a line that arrives in that window reaches the worker first. No wrong-run case was found from it.

## Search verbs honour root (2026-11-03)

Cause: `grep`, `search` and `wiki_search` in `src/search.mjs` took no `root`. Their argument handling dropped it, so a call with `root` searched the workspace and returned those hits as if the root had been used. A root that does not exist, and an unknown argument such as `rootz`, were accepted the same way.

Fix: each verb takes `root` (aliases `projectPath` and `cwd`), an existing directory that replaces the workspace for that call. Each root has its own index and file cache, and the four most recent roots are kept. A root other than the workspace shows absolute paths in its hits. A root that is missing, is not a directory, or differs between its aliases is refused by name, and so is an argument the verb does not take, with the arguments it does take. `src/runcode.mjs` lists `root` in the grep line of the run_code description.

Witness: `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-root/drive.mjs`, a stdio driver for `src/mcp.mjs` with workspace `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-root/a` and two `git init` repositories, `a` and `b`. Before the fix (run `A-prefix`), `gm.grep` with root `b` returned 0 matches and `gm.search` returned a's line. After the fix (runs `A-fix` and `A-fix2`): `gm.grep` with root `b` returned `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-root/b/notes.txt:2 MARKER_B_5e82d4 repo b only`, and the same call without root returned 0 matches. A missing root returned `refused: no such root: .../no-such-root`, and `rootz` returned `refused: unknown argument rootz; grep takes pattern, path, mode, ignoreCase, limit, offset, vendor, root`. `search` with root `b` returned b's line, `wiki_search` with root `b` returned `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-root/b/wiki/page.md:2`, and a root that is a file, and a root that differs from cwd, were refused.

Limits: the verbs are `search`, `wiki_search` and `grep`, since no `codesearch` verb exists in this checkout. The witness ran through the stdio server on Linux only; macOS and Windows are not witnessed. A root's first call builds its index, so it is slower than a warm workspace call. A root is not limited to the workspace, as run_code allows paths outside it.

## Playwriter in the default MCP config (2026-11-03)

Decision: every checkout ships `defaults/mcp.json`, the first of three MCP config layers that `main` in `src/mcp.mjs` reads through `readServerConfig` (`src/mcp-client.mjs`), in this order: (a) `defaults/mcp.json`, required, so a missing file is an `mcp-config` problem; (b) `<workspace>/.gm-new/mcp.json` when it exists; (c) the `--mcp-config` file when given, required. A later entry with the same name replaces the earlier one whole. `"disabled": true` removes the name. An entry that fails validation also replaces the earlier one: it is logged as a problem and not served (witness F). The default entry is `npx -y -p github:AnEntrypoint/pw#v0.1.0 playwriter-project`, with `startupTimeoutMs` 600000 and an empty `env`. `PLAYWRITER_NO_SANDBOX` is not in the default, because the launcher's docs say to drop it on a normal desktop; this host keeps it in `/config/.gm-new/mcp.json`.

Hypotheses, stated before the change: (1) a fresh checkout has playwriter with no workspace file; (2) a workspace `disabled: true` removes it; (3) a `--mcp-config` file replaces the default by name; (4) a first npx start finishes within 180 s. Hypothesis (4) was refuted (witness A), so the timeout is 600000.

Windows: `npx.cmd` was not used. Node refuses to spawn a `.cmd` file without a shell (EINVAL, in its 2024 security release), and the client spawns without one. `launchOf` runs `npx` and `npm` through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` on win32. This is not run on Windows.

Gate and update check: `disabledServers` builds a layer that disables every default name. The gate's prompt check (`src/gate.mjs`) and the candidate worker of the update check (`tools/update.mjs`) pass it. Without it, the prompt check's 20 s spawn timeout would fail on any slow network, and the candidate worker would leave the launcher running after its SIGKILL.

Witnesses. Stdio drivers, outside the repo, in `/tmp/gm-scratch/defaults`. Each run is the relay `tools/relay.mjs` on a copy of the tree, and the log is read from the run directory. The cold cache is a fresh npm cache directory, so the first start is a real download.
- A, first try (180000 ms, cold cache): next_context was answered after 180054 ms. The `mcp-server` event is `failed`, with `error` "initialize timed out after 180000 ms". Verbs `[]`. Relay exit 0. This refuted hypothesis (4).
- A2 (600000 ms, cold cache): next_context was answered after 173634 ms. `mcp-server` `connected`, `tools` 2 (start 11:44:34.076Z, connected 11:47:27.711Z). run_code output `["playwriter_execute","playwriter_reset"]`. Relay exit 0.
- A3 (600000 ms, warm cache, committed HEAD 56d52a2 plus this change): next_context after 11831 ms, `mcp-server` `connected`, `tools` 2, run_code output `["playwriter_execute","playwriter_reset"]`, relay exit 0.
- B (`--mcp-config /config/.gm-new/mcp.json`): the local entry started (`node /workspace/pw/bin/playwriter-project`, cwd the workspace) and the default was replaced, so no npx ran. `mcp-server` `connected`, `tools` 2, 0.8 s after start (B2 on the committed tree: 0.4 s). run_code output `["playwriter_execute","playwriter_reset"]`.
- C (`{"mcpServers":{"playwriter":{"disabled":true}}}` in the workspace): run_code output `[]`, no `mcp-server` event, no child process (C2 on the committed tree: the same).
- D (launcher alone, cold cache, a new cwd): `initialize` answered after 457.5 s with serverInfo `playwriter` 0.9.0 and protocol 2025-11-25. tools/list: `execute`, `reset` (the relay names them `playwriter_execute` and `playwriter_reset`). Stopped by its own process group, exit SIGTERM. No execute or reset was called, and no page was opened. Its stderr had `Using direct CDP connection: ws://127.0.0.1:9767/devtools/browser/`, which the launcher prints at start. This witness did not establish which browser that address belongs to. The warm repeat (D2, same cache) answered `initialize` after 4.5 s with the same two tools.
- F (`{"mcpServers":{"playwriter":{"command":5}}}` in the workspace): `mcp-config` problem `server playwriter: command must be a non-empty string`, run_code output `[]`.
- G (the export had no `defaults/` directory): `mcp-config` problem `<tree>/defaults/mcp.json does not exist`, the worker ran on, run_code output `[]`.
- H (Node engine message, stand-in): `/tmp/gm-scratch/defaults/engine-standin.mjs` writes an engine-style message to stderr and exits 1, through `--mcp-config`. The `mcp-server` `failed` event carries `stderr` with that message and `error` "MCP server playwriter exited with code 1". Relay exit 0, no process left.
- E (gate, `runGate` in `src/gate.mjs`, run on the checkout): ok, with no failed check. syntax 42 modules parse; ascii 87 text files; docs 287 paths; sources; wiki; skills; connect; workers 30 modules; prompt kept all 15 words; smoke finished and its negative control stopped at max_steps.

Limits, not closed here:
- Node: the `pw` package declares `engines` `node >=22`, and gm-new runs on Node 20 and later. On Node 20 or 21 the default may fail to start. This host has no Node 20 binary, so that failure was not run live; H witnessed only the reporting path. The reason is in the stderr of the `mcp-server` `failed` event.
- First start: cold installs took 174 s (A2) and 458 s (D) on this host, because the npm install of `playwriter@0.9.0` resolves a large dependency tree. A warm start took 4.5 s to 12 s. The 600000 ms default covers both measured cold starts with margin; it is not a bound.
- tools/list and run_code wait for the default's start (`mcpReady` in `src/mcp.mjs`), so a cold first install holds the host's first tools/list for up to 600 s. The host's own timeout for that wait was not measured. A tools/list that does not wait, followed by `notifications/tools/list_changed`, would avoid it. That changes protocol behaviour and is left to the owner.
- The pinned tag `v0.1.0` (the remote lists it as ba65a3a) is a choice. A newer launcher needs a hand bump in `defaults/mcp.json`.
- The launcher's `--gate` child is the launcher's own daemon, with a localhost listener. It is not stopped with its MCP child: it kept running after the relay exited in B, A2, A3 and D. Each was stopped by its PID after its working directory was checked. Other workers' playwriter processes on this host were not touched.
- Windows and macOS are not witnessed, and neither is arm64 (this host is linux-x64). The `cmd.exe` path is untested.
- A worker killed with SIGKILL runs no cleanup, so its servers can outlive it. This follows from Node's process model and was not witnessed.
- Foreign uncommitted changes in `src/search.mjs` and `src/runcode.mjs` were not committed.

## Pre-fetch, telemetry off, and gitignore on use (2026-11-03)

Decisions, requested by the owner:

1. Pre-fetch. `setup.sh` runs `tools/prefetch.mjs` as its step 4, after the gate, on Node 22 or later. The module prints what it does first: pw v0.1.0 installs playwriter 0.9.0, the browser tool gm uses, once into the npm cache (`npm config get cache`), which can take minutes on a slow network, and the browser starts only on first use. It then runs `npx -y -p github:AnEntrypoint/pw#v0.1.0 playwriter-project help` with a 900 s cap, and prints the result. A failure prints its reason, says that gm still installs and runs and that the tools are fetched on the first gm start, and names `./setup.sh` as the retry. The module never sets a non-zero exit. On Node below 22, setup prints that the tools need Node 22 and skips the fetch. Reason: a first gm start otherwise waits for the download (cold 182.1 s here, warm 6.6 s).
2. The `help` argument is safe to run. In `bin/playwriter-project` at tag v0.1.0, `main` returns after `console.log(USAGE)` for `help` and `--help`, before `loadPorts`, `ensureGate` and any spawn, so the fetch starts no gate, browser or server and writes no `.playwriter/` (witness W2).
3. Telemetry off. `defaults/mcp.json` sets `"PLAYWRITER_TELEMETRY": "0"` in the playwriter env. `PLAYWRITER_NO_SANDBOX` stays out of the default. The check, in playwriter 0.9.0 `dist/telemetry.js` line 44: `return optOut !== '0' && optOut !== 'false' && doNotTrack !== '1' && doNotTrack !== 'true';`. That sets `TELEMETRY_ENABLED` to false, and `startRelayTelemetry` returns before its `initStrada` call when it is false, so no anonymous event is sent. `trackEvent` is a no-op in a process that did not start telemetry. The module header says Strada is initialised only by `startRelayTelemetry`; the importers of that function were not traced in dist beyond the header.
4. Gitignore on use. A server entry may carry `"ignore": [...]`. `serverOf` validates it: an array of non-empty strings, each relative, with no `..` segment and no leading slash or drive letter, or the server is a problem and the others still run (witness W6). On each successful connect (`connected` or `restarted`) in a workspace with a `.git` file or directory, `ServerProcess.ignoreWorkspace` appends each listed line that `<workspace>/.gitignore` does not hold. The file is created when missing, existing lines and line endings are kept, and a newline is added before the new lines when the file lacks one. A new line is logged as a `gitignore` event with state `added`. A connect with nothing to add logs nothing. A workspace that is not a git repository logs one `gitignore` event with state `skipped` and writes nothing. A write error logs state `failed`. The playwriter entry declares `.playwriter/`.

Witnesses. Scratch under `/tmp/gm-scratch`; the witness copy is a tar of the working tree with `git init` and an empty bare origin, so the update check reports offline. The shell has `NPM_CONFIG_CACHE` (upper case) set, which overrides `npm_config_cache`, so the W1 runs unset it.
- W1, cold: `npm_config_cache=/tmp/gm-scratch/prefetch-cache` (empty apart from npm's `_logs`, which `npm config get cache` creates), 182.1 s, exit 0, printed "setup: playwriter tools fetched in 182.1 s. The browser starts on first use." An earlier attempt with the upper-case variable used the real cache (3.5 s) and is not counted. W1, warm: 6.6 s, exit 0, the same two printed lines and nothing else.
- W2: a `/proc` snapshot of processes whose command names playwriter, taken before and after the warm run: 11 before, 11 after, none with cwd `/tmp/gm-scratch/prefetch-run`, and no `.playwriter/` there. The one match in that cwd was the witness shell, whose own command text contains the word.
- W3, simulated: a stand-in `node` on PATH reports v20.19.0 for `-v`, and in each `-e` string rewrites `process.versions.node` to `"20.19.0"`, then runs the real Node 24. `setup.sh` printed "setup: the playwriter tools need Node 22 or later, and will not start on v20.19.0. The fetch is skipped; gm still installs and runs.", the gate passed, and the exit code was 0. The Node version was simulated, not run.
- W4: `readServerConfig` over `defaults/mcp.json` gives the playwriter entry with env `{"PLAYWRITER_TELEMETRY":"0"}` and ignore `[".playwriter/"]`. Live, the two playwriter-project processes started by a worker in `/tmp/gm-scratch/gitignore/repo` (pids 432076 and 432232) carry `PLAYWRITER_TELEMETRY=0` in `/proc/<pid>/environ`.
- W5, a git repo `/tmp/gm-scratch/gitignore/repo` (`git init`, `.gitignore` = `node_modules/` and `runs/` with no trailing newline). First start (run `run-1`): the file became `"node_modules/\nruns/\n.playwriter/\n"`, and the log has `mcp-server` `connected` (tools 2) and `gitignore` `added` with `added: [".playwriter/"]`. Second start (`run-2`): the file is unchanged, and the log has only the `connected` event. Non-git directory `/tmp/gm-scratch/gitignore/plain`: one `gitignore` event, state `skipped`, reason "the workspace is not a git repository"; no `.gitignore` exists afterwards.
- W6: a config with `ignore` `["../x"]`, `"x"` (a string) and `["/abs/x"]` gave three problems, "ignore lines must not contain a .. segment", "ignore must be an array of non-empty strings" and "ignore lines must be relative paths, not absolute". The fourth server, with `[".playwriter/"]`, was still served.

Limits, not closed here:
- Windows, macOS and arm64 are not witnessed. On Windows, `npx` runs through `cmd.exe` (`launchOf`), which the fetch's `shell: true` covers; that path was not run.
- The fetch depends on GitHub. The 900 s cap is a bound on a hung fetch, not a promise about a cold download, which took 182.1 s here.
- The tag `v0.1.0` is pinned by hand in `tools/prefetch.mjs`, `defaults/mcp.json` and `README.md`. A newer tag needs all three changed.
- Two workers that connect to one workspace at the same moment can both read the file before either appends, so one line can be written twice. The append takes no lock.
- A leading slash is refused as absolute, so an anchored gitignore pattern is written without it.
- The owner's `/config/.gm-new/mcp.json` replaces the playwriter entry whole, so it needs the `ignore` key and the telemetry env. This change does not edit it.
- Each worker start in a git repository with the entry appends to that repository's `.gitignore`. In gm-new itself, `.playwriter/` is already in `.gitignore`, so no append happens there.

Source: the list in "Friction found by oscraft run 2800-6805174" above. Drivers and transcripts are outside the repo, in /tmp/gm-scratch/oscraft-friction/ (drive.mjs; t*-origin.json and t*-final.json). Linux x64 only.

- Item 6, session.finished crash: already fixed on origin. A gm.grep of `\.finished\(` over src/ and tools/ at 28ea4e7 (tree from git archive) has no hit; the one session call is `session.ended()` in src/mcp.mjs. Witness: protocol 2025-11-25 handshake, run_code `{"two":2}`, finish, next_context opened run 2026-11-03-12-20-51-f86d0c, run_code read the file, finish, exit 0.
- Item 1, read_file counts characters: already fixed on origin (b2ad777, f6cc36a). The run_code verb calls the same handler as the served tool. Witness: `gm.read_file({ path: 'thirty.txt', limit: 10 })` on a 30-line file returned unit lines, 10 lines, line 01 to line 10. `unit: "chars"` returned `line 01 of`.
- Item 2, skill:// paths: fixed in c6c33bb5. read_file hands a skill:// path to `serveSkillFile` (src/mcp.mjs), the function resources/read uses, so both log `skill-loaded`. The rules-notice clearing shares resources/read's code path; it is not witnessed. Before: ENOENT for `ws/skill:/gm/SKILL.md`. After: ok true, 12297 characters, byte-equal to skills/gm/SKILL.md; `skill://nope/SKILL.md` returns `no served skill file has the uri skill://nope/SKILL.md`.
- Item 4, grep mode `content`: fixed in b2d82088. Choice: `content` is an alias of `literal`, the default mode, which already returns the matching lines. A new output mode would add a second meaning for one word. The zero-result hint is now the regex fallback in "grep regex fallback (2026-11-03)" below, and `.` counts as a regex character (before, a dotted pattern got no hint). Witness: literal `ab.c` on a file holding `abxc` returned 0 matches with the hint; `mode: "regex"` returned `abxc is the regex hit`; `mode: "content"` with `abxc` returned that line.
- Item 5, events --wait ended on a restart: fixed in db328096 (`paused` removed from PENDING_KINDS, so it is logged but not queued). Witness, origin: the watcher exited at the SIGTERM with `kind: paused, reason: SIGTERM from the supervisor`. Fixed tree: the watcher was alive at the SIGTERM, alive after a new worker started on the same run dir, and exited on `kind: job, job: probe-1`. Trade-off: a worker stopped without a restart wakes its watcher only at host-idle (600000 ms by default). This reverses the 2026-11-03 "Wake by a watcher" decision for `paused` only, on the owner's direction in this item. Not measured on a live host.

The gm reread line (rules notice) and the gm-continue line in skills/gm/SKILL.md name `gm.read_file({ path: 'skill://gm/SKILL.md' })` as the fallback. Changing them is a separate change, since the connect text sets CONNECT_SHA.

## Node floor 22 (2026-11-03)

Owner decision, 2026-11-03: Node 20 is dropped, and the floor is the lowest Node line the code needs, not the latest LTS line. The floor is Node 22: `Number(process.versions.node.split('.')[0]) >= 22`, enforced by setup, the doctor and `engines`. Node 22 is the floor because the `pw` package (the playwriter tools) declares `engines` `node >=22`. A search of `src/` for `Promise.withResolvers`, `Array.fromAsync`, the Set methods, the `toSorted` family, `node:sqlite`, `fs.glob`, `AbortSignal.any`, `Symbol.dispose`, import attributes, `isWellFormed` and `process.getBuiltinModule` found none. Node 26 is supported and not required: the Node 26 floor of commit e6eb8b6 is reverted by the commit that records this section.

Changed: `package.json` and the root entry of `package-lock.json`, `setup.sh` (the header, the node check, and the playwriter fetch, which now runs on every Node that setup accepts), `src/cli.mjs` (doctor), `README.md`, `docs/index.html` (install and troubleshooting), `AGENTS.md` (the language rule), and the Language and Doctor bullets of "Decided" above.

Witness: a stand-in `node` first on PATH (`/tmp/gm-scratch/node22/v21/bin/node`) reports v21.7.0 for `-v`, and through `NODE_OPTIONS` reports `process.versions.node` as 21.7.0 for the real Node v24.22.0 underneath. Run on a tar copy of the working tree with a local `git init` and no origin, since the gate lists files through git.
- W1, node v21.7.0: `setup: gm needs Node 22 or later. This is node v21.7.0. Install it from https://nodejs.org, then run ./setup.sh again.`, exit 1.
- W2, node v24.22.0: `setup: gate: passed`, `setup: playwriter tools fetched in 5.8 s`, the three add-mcp lines, exit 0.
- W3, `doctor`: on v21.7.0 `node v21.7.0: fail, Node 22 or newer is required`, exit 1; on v24.22.0 `node v24.22.0: ok`, exit 0.

Not witnessed: a live run on Node 22 or Node 26 (this machine has neither), macOS, Windows, arm64.

gm MCP entry fixed in the user config (2026-11-03)

Cause: the user-level `gm` entry in `~/.claude.json` (top-level `mcpServers`) still had the placeholder path `/abs/path/to/gm-new/tools/relay.mjs`. `node` exited at once and the host reported CONNECTION_CLOSED, on every reconnect. The relay was fine: a stdio probe answered `initialize` with the gm instructions and protocol 2025-11-25, with stdin held open for the core.

Fix: the entry now runs `node /data/workspace/gm-new/tools/relay.mjs` with no arguments, as `setup.sh` prints it. The relay uses the session's directory as its workspace. Only that path changed; the other top-level keys were checked equal before and after.

Gap: nothing checks that the path in the entry exists, so a placeholder fails silently until the host reconnects. Possible change: `setup.sh` reads the entry and reports a missing path. Not done here.

gm install check: the registered path is reported (2026-11-03)

Change: `src/registration.mjs` reads the user-level gm entry in the Claude config (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `~/.claude.json`) and reports the path its relay runs. `node src/cli.mjs doctor` prints that line and fails when the path does not exist. `setup.sh` prints the same line before its install commands. The README and the manual no longer carry a copyable install line with a placeholder path, and the manual's troubleshooting table has a row for CONNECTION_CLOSED.

Why: a user-level entry with the placeholder path `/abs/path/to/gm-new/tools/relay.mjs`, copied from the documentation, made `node` exit at once. The session then showed CONNECTION_CLOSED on every reconnect and nothing said why. The relay answered `initialize` correctly over stdio, so the fault was the registered path alone.

Checked: five config states give fail for a placeholder path, ok for a real path, ok for no gm entry, fail for unreadable JSON, and ok for no config file. `doctor` exits 1 for the placeholder and 0 for the real path. The gate passed on the working tree: 43 modules parse, 89 text files are ASCII, 212 doc paths exist, 30 worker modules match the import graph, the session prompt keeps all 15 words, and the smoke run finishes with its negative control at max_steps.

Not covered: a gm entry in a project's `.mcp.json` is not checked, and a path that exists but is not this checkout's relay is reported as ok.

## grep regex fallback (2026-11-03)

Friction: chatnano-f6, run 465053-9472994, 2026-11-03. In the default mode, `gm.grep({ pattern: "ternar|BitNet|1\\.58|two-bit|2-bit", ignoreCase: true })` returned `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` with the hint that the pattern was matched as exact text. The same pattern in mode regex returned 92 matches. `gm.grep({ pattern: "^(def |class )" })` returned 0 in the default mode. A silent zero reads as "not present".

Rule (`grep` in `searcherFor`, `src/search.mjs`). In literal mode (the default, also named `content`), when the literal pass finds no match and the pattern has a regex character (`.|()[]{}*+?^$\`), the same files are scanned once more with the pattern as a regex, with the same path, ignoreCase, limit and offset.
- The regex pass finds matches: they are returned, and the hint is `no literal match; matched as regex`.
- The regex pass finds none: the hint is `no match as literal or regex`.
- The pattern is not a valid regex: the reply is `{ ok: false, error }`, and the error says that the literal pass found nothing and that the pattern is not a valid regex.
- The literal pass finds any match: the reply is unchanged.

Why not regex first: `||` and `foo()` are literal code text, and a regex-first rule would change their hits. The fallback runs only when the literal pass is empty, so no literal result changes. Cost: a pattern with a regex character that matches nothing literally reads the files twice. Mode regex and mode word are unchanged; a zero from mode regex is still a bare zero.

The `grep` tool description names the fallback. The comments in `src/search.mjs` were swept in this change: the fallback and the second-horizon cut are now facts in AGENTS.md, and the streaming fact was already there.

Witness: the working tree was copied as a tar (without .git, runs and .gm) to `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/grep-fix/after`. `tools/relay.mjs` ran from that copy with `--mcp-config` disabling the playwriter entry, in a temp workspace holding `notes.txt` (`BitNet b1.58 uses ternary weights.` and `a two-bit quantiser`) and `code.py` (`def f(x):`, `    return x or foo()`, `a = b || c`). One run_code body called `gm.grep` on six patterns. The driver and body are in /tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/grep-fix/, outside the repository. The replies:
- `ternar|BitNet|1\.58|two-bit|2-bit`, ignoreCase true: `{"ok":true,"files":"walk","output":"notes.txt:1 BitNet b1.58 uses ternary weights.\nnotes.txt:2 a two-bit quantiser\n(2 matches; showing 0 to 2; no more)\nhint: no literal match; matched as regex","hint":"no literal match; matched as regex"}`
- `^(def |class )`: `{"ok":true,"files":"walk","output":"code.py:1 def f(x):\n(1 matches; showing 0 to 1; no more)\nhint: no literal match; matched as regex","hint":"no literal match; matched as regex"}`
- `||`: `{"ok":true,"files":"walk","output":"code.py:3 a = b || c\n(1 matches; showing 0 to 1; no more)"}`
- `foo()`: `{"ok":true,"files":"walk","output":"code.py:2     return x or foo()\n(1 matches; showing 0 to 1; no more)"}`
- `zzqq|x(`: `{"ok":false,"error":"no literal match, and the pattern is not a valid regex: Invalid regular expression: /zzqq|x(/: Unterminated group"}`
- `zzqq|yy`: `{"ok":true,"files":"walk","output":"(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)\nhint: no match as literal or regex","hint":"no match as literal or regex"}`

Before, on the unchanged tree with the same driver: the first, second, fifth and sixth patterns returned `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` with the hint `the pattern has regex characters and was matched as exact text; mode regex matches it as a regular expression`. `||` and `foo()` returned the same replies as after.

Commit: the commit whose subject is "grep: a literal miss with a regex character runs as a regex" (in git log of main).

Not witnessed: a host session (Claude Code) calling grep; macOS, Windows, arm64.

## Subagents on one connection: child reads (2026-11-03)

Friction (chatnano-f6, run 465053-9472994): a general-purpose subagent called `next_context` on the parent's connection and got the parent's run (task "lets"). The reply did not say it was a child, and the skill says a child must not send `finish`. The earlier handoff design assumed a child on its own session, but a subagent shares its parent's connection.

Design: a child calls `next_context` with `{ child: true, run: "<owning run id>" }`. The reply has `role: "child"`, the owning `run`, and a `note`. The read takes no step, claims no pending event and does not move the feed cursor. It never opens a run: a child call on an ended run is refused, and a call that names another run is refused. A child on its own connection still gets its own run on its first call. Rejected: a child opening its own run on a shared connection, because a connection holds one session and that would change every parent reply. The skill's child section (`skills/gm/SKILL.md`) names the read.

Witness: the relay over stdio, in a temp workspace. The driver is `/tmp/gm-scratch/child-witness/witness.mjs`, outside the repository.
- Parent `next_context`: run `w1`, `stepsLeft` 49.
- Child `next_context({ child: true, run: "w1" })`: `{"ok":true,"role":"child","run":"w1","note":"child of run w1: the session that opened the run ends it, so a child does not send finish",...}`.
- Child naming another run: `refused: this connection's run is w1, not not-this-run`. The parent's next call then showed `stepsLeft` 48, so the child read took no step.
- After the parent's `finish`, a child call: `refused: run w1 has ended; the session that opened it calls next_context to open the next run`. The parent's next call opened run `2026-11-03-16-48-24-56c7c8`.

Commit: "next_context child read: a subagent on a shared connection reads its run" (`ebeaafe2f283f6d92028d92ba18fc38aa7165d03`).

Not witnessed: a live Claude Code subagent sending `child: true` on a shared connection. A worker started before this commit runs the old code until it restarts.

## grep scope and the skipped-file hint (2026-11-03)

Friction: chatnano-f6, run 465053-9472994, 2026-11-03. `gm.grep` returned `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` for a text that sat in an untracked directory (`mic-runner/`, not yet added) and in `node_modules/`. `grep` reads only git-tracked files and said nothing about the rest, so the zero read as "not present". The peer's suggestion: keep the tracked default, and say in the hint how many files were skipped and how to include them.

Baseline, before the change (`/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-scope-witness/body.js`, run `runs-before`, workspace `/tmp/gm-scratch/grep-scope-witness/ws`): a match in the tracked file had no hint; a match only in the untracked and node_modules files returned `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` with no hint; `scope` was refused as an unknown argument.

Rule (`grep` in `searcherFor`, `src/search.mjs`):
- `scope` is `tracked` (default) or `all`. Any other value is refused with `scope must be one of tracked, all`.
- `tracked` reads the same files as before and returns the same matches. When it skips one or more files under `path`, its reply carries one more hint line: `N files skipped (U untracked, G gitignored or in node_modules); use scope: 'all' to include them`. The line shows only on a zero-match reply, and a reply with matches carries none. A hint on every call is noise in a repo with node_modules (2182 files are skipped on `/workspace/gm`), and a zero is the reply where the skipped files could hold the answer. The count is computed only on a zero-match reply, so a reply with matches runs no extra `git ls-files`. Skipped means a file under `path` that git lists (tracked, untracked or ignored) and that `tracked` does not read. A tracked file under node_modules or vendor counts as skipped when `vendor` is not true. State directories do not count.
- `U` counts untracked files that are neither ignored nor under node_modules or vendor. `G` counts the rest. The split follows the peer's wording.
- `all` reads every file git lists under `path`: tracked, untracked and gitignored, node_modules included. It skips `.git` and the state directories `.gm`, `.agentplug`, `.agentplug-kv`, `.gm-new`, `.playwriter` and `runs`, which hold gm and gm-new state. A tracked file inside one of them is still read. `all` adds no hint.
- Outside the workspace (a walk), `tracked` skips dot-directories, node_modules, vendor and runs/ as before. Its hint counts the directories it skipped, because counting their files would mean walking them: `N directories skipped (dot-directories, node_modules, vendor or runs); use scope: 'all' to include them`, on a zero-match reply only. `all` walks them.
- `vendor` is unchanged. It admits tracked files under node_modules and vendor, and it never reached untracked or gitignored files, which is why `scope` exists.
- Time: `all` lists with `git ls-files -c -o` under the existing 30 s listing limit (`LIST_TIMEOUT_MS`). Past the limit, `all` replies `scope all: git ls-files failed or ran past its 30 s limit`. The tracked count uses the same limit; past it the hint reads `skipped files not counted: git ls-files failed or ran past its limit`, and the matches are still returned.

Cost, measured on `/workspace/gm` (375 tracked files, 29,326 entries from `git ls-files -c -o`), bash `time`: `git ls-files -c -o` 62 ms, `git ls-files -o --exclude-standard` 5 ms, `git ls-files -c` 2 ms. Default `grep` for `zzqqmiss` through the MCP worker: 204 ms and 152 ms, with `hint: 2182 files skipped (0 untracked, 2182 gitignored or in node_modules)`. `scope: "all"` for the same pattern: 788 ms. The default before the change was not timed on this tree. The 20 untracked non-ignored files there are all under `.gm/` and `runs/`, which is why the count shows 0 untracked.

Witness, this change: a tar copy of the working tree of `/workspace/gm-new` with the edit (`/tmp/grep-skip-hint-witness/tree-final`, without `.git` and `runs`), through `tools/relay.mjs` started by `/tmp/gm-scratch/gm-new/grep-fix/driver.mjs` with `--mcp-config` disabling playwriter. Workspace `/tmp/grep-skip-hint-witness/ws`, a fresh `git init` with tracked `tracked.txt`, `.gitignore` and `notes.txt`, untracked `mic-runner/run.txt`, ignored `node_modules/pkg/index.js`, and untracked state `.gm/state.txt`. Body `/tmp/grep-skip-hint-witness/body.js`, run `runs-final`. The same body on the tree before this change (`tree-before`, run `runs-before`) gave the same match lines, with the old hints. After the run the tree copies, the workspace and the outside directory were deleted; `body.js` and the run logs `runs-before` and `runs-final` remain. Each reply is quoted as its `output`; the `hint` field repeats the hint line, and two hints join with ` | `.
- (a) default, tracked file holds `mic`: `tracked.txt:1 mic in tracked` / `(1 matches; showing 0 to 1; no more)`. No hint.
- (b) default, tracked file rewritten without `mic`: `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` / `hint: 2 files skipped (1 untracked, 1 gitignored or in node_modules); use scope: 'all' to include them`
- (c) `scope: "all"`: `mic-runner/run.txt:1 mic in untracked dir` / `node_modules/pkg/index.js:1 module.exports = 'mic';` / `(2 matches; showing 0 to 2; no more)`. No hint. `.gm/state.txt` is absent.
- (c2) `scope: "some"`: `{"ok":false,"error":"scope must be one of tracked, all"}`
- (d) `zzqqmiss`, matches nothing: `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` / `hint: 2 files skipped (1 untracked, 1 gitignored or in node_modules); use scope: 'all' to include them`
- (d2) `zz.qq`, matches nothing, regex fallback runs: `(0 matches; showing 0 to 0; no more)` / `hint: no match as literal or regex` / `hint: 2 files skipped (1 untracked, 1 gitignored or in node_modules); use scope: 'all' to include them`
- (e1) `ternar|BitNet|1\.58|two-bit|2-bit`: `notes.txt:1 BitNet 1.58 two-bit ternar ||` / `(1 matches; showing 0 to 1; no more)` / `hint: no literal match; matched as regex`. No skipped-file hint, since it has matches.
